# DECISIONS

Append-only. One line per settled question, newest at the bottom. Never edit or
delete a line — if a decision is reversed, append the reversal with its reason.

Format: `YYYY-MM-DD — <decision> — <reason>`

---

2026-08-12 — Repo is PUBLIC at github.com/beansbuildin/gigaruns; PROTOCOL Option A — user directive; secrets live in ~/.secrets and are gitignored, so nothing sensitive is exposed. Strategy tuning being public is accepted.
2026-08-12 — TypeScript strict + NodeNext ESM, `"type": "module"` — CLAUDE.md mandates TS; NodeNext matches the actual Node/tsx runtime. Consequence: relative imports need explicit `.js` extensions.
2026-08-12 — vitest 4.1.10, not 2.x — the 2.x tree carried a critical vite/esbuild advisory; 4.x audits clean. Re-audit before any downgrade.
2026-08-12 — `.claude/settings.local.json` gitignored — machine-local permissions state, not repo content.
2026-08-12 — No `.gitkeep` in `data/` or `logs/` — both gitignored wholesale, so a keepfile could never be tracked.
2026-08-12 — `config/bot.json` deferred, not invented — no schema exists yet and CLAUDE.md forbids guessing at energy-budget values. Must be defined explicitly before the orchestrator can enforce a budget.
2026-08-13 — Task 3 runs before Task 2; schemas are written from observed responses, never from SPEC — probe.ts is self-contained and read-only, so writing zod schemas against spec guesses then rewriting them against reality is wasted work in both directions. Ground truth first.
2026-08-13 — Auth is Path A (browser JWT at ~/.secrets/gigaverse-jwt.txt); Path B EOA signing stays deferred — user plays in-browser via Abstract Global Wallet, so a bot-owned EOA would authenticate a different, empty account.
2026-08-13 — Combat resolution: winner OR tie regenerates armor = own move's currentDEF (capped, excess wasted) then deals full ATK; loser gains and deals nothing; regen before damage; damage hits armor then overflows to HP — user-supplied rule, machine-verified 14/14 against recorded exchanges by scripts/verifyCombatModel.ts. EVERY move regenerates, not just Shield.
2026-08-13 — The earlier "only Shield grants armor / ties deal ATK−DEF" model is REJECTED and must not be reintroduced — it also fit 14/14, because the two are identical while armor is 0 and the cap is slack and no win with a DEF-bearing non-Shield move was ever recorded. It grants 0 armor instead of 8 when winning with Spell.
2026-08-13 — No in-combat healing; HP restored only by a post-fight card — observed. Consequence: armor is renewable and HP is not, so SPEC §4b keeps HP and armor as separate utility terms rather than one effective-HP pool.
2026-08-13 — Enemy moves at ≤0 charges are DOWN-WEIGHTED, not pruned to zero — charges were observed going negative (paper 1 → −1) and no move was ever seen attempted at ≤0, so illegality is unproven. Revisit when a run records an enemy playing at ≤0.
2026-08-13 — Raw API dumps live in gitignored `fixtures/**/raw/`; committed fixtures are redacted (0xUSER / <USER> / <JWT>) with all game values intact — raw is ground truth per CLAUDE.md §1, but the repo is public.
2026-08-14 — Username tolerated in git history; wallet address and JWT never are — user directive (session-03 brief §3): the username is already public in-game and on-chain via the Noob NFT, so a force-push on a public repo costs more than it buys. Redaction is built into the capture scripts going forward.
2026-08-14 — Rewards arrive via `rewardPathPhase`/`rewardPathOptions[]` (boons) and `enemyPathPhase`/`enemyPathOptions[]` (next enemy + `lootTable`); `lootPhase`/`lootOptions` are NOT the reward surface — observed false/empty across 5 dungeon attempts that demonstrably awarded loot, a boon and a heal. Session 02's "never reached a loot phase" was a false negative.
2026-08-14 — Boon values are read from `selectedVal1`/`selectedVal2`, never `val1Min`/`val1Max` — `selected*` is the applied value, already multiplied by `basicBoonMultiplier` (2). Heal val1 8 applied as 16 (HP 15→31). Reading the raw range halves every boon.
2026-08-14 — Armor refills to currentMax at every room transition; HP does not — observed at all 3 room boundaries of the deepest run. Consequence: armor is a per-room budget wasted if unspent, HP is the run-long resource; §4b must weight them differently, not merely separately.
2026-08-14 — `rolledEnemyStats` (evasion/block/lck/tenacity) are live and affect damage; the session-02 "all zero, safe to hardcode" assumption is REVERSED — enemy 65 with block 2 / evasion 2 took 8 from a 16-ATK Sword win.
2026-08-14 — Charge cost rule CONFIRMED over 134 played moves: −1 per play, except a play from exactly 1 charge lands on −1 (skipping 0); unplayed regenerate +1 capped, on combat exchanges only. This supersedes and explains session 02's "unexplained decrement of two".
2026-08-14 — Charge LEGALITY (hard prune vs soft cost) remains UNRESOLVED and must be implemented behind a `chargesAreHardLimit` flag (default false) — 23 qualifying turns, 0 forced plays. The session-03 brief's discriminator ("held a move at ≤0 and played a different one ⇒ illegal") is REJECTED as non-discriminating: under the soft-cost hypothesis it is exactly what a free choice looks like.
2026-08-14 — Do not read an enemy move distribution off fewer than ~40 exchanges — I called enemy 63 "Shield-biased 57%" off 14 exchanges and gave play advice from it; over 39 it is uniform (31/38/31). Same overfit shape as the session-02 combat model.
2026-08-14 — Analysis scripts must split consecutive-state pairs on `DUNGEON_ID_CID` — one watcher session can span several dungeon attempts, and the boundary between two attempts is not an exchange.
2026-08-14 — `/game/dungeon/state` returns a 500 HTML error page once a run ends; the Task 2 client must treat 5xx on this path as "no active run", not as fatal.
2026-08-15 — REVERSAL of 2026-08-14 "armor refills to currentMax at every room transition": armor does NOT refill; HP, armor and charges all cross a room boundary unchanged — the corpus has four room boundaries and three crossed with the player already at the armor cap (15/15), where "refilled" and "unchanged" are indistinguishable; the one informative boundary (run-23-29-39 009→010) crossed at ARM 4/15 and stayed at 4/15. What refills is the enemy, because a transition swaps in a new entity at full pools. Consequence: the §4b guidance built on it was backwards — armor is not a per-room budget that is free to spend late.
2026-08-15 — Net damage depends on how the exchange resolved: full ATK on an outright win (the loser regenerates nothing), `max(0, ATK − opponentMoveDEF)` on a tie, 0 on a loss — the session-04 brief's single rule `max(0, ATK − armorRestoredPerWin)` applied to every exchange is REJECTED, as is session 03's "enemy 63's armor fully restores on any win" (regen is the played move's DEF, capped: observed 6→12 on a Sword win with DEF 6). The tie case is an exact threshold, verified in sim across all four enemies: our Shield's ATK 6 clears DEF 2 and 4, and makes literally zero progress at DEF 6 and 8.
2026-08-15 — REVERSAL of 2026-08-14 "chargesAreHardLimit default false": default is now `true` (prune). The flag stays and the question stays open, but the enemy-only recount is 11 clean opportunities to play a move held at ≤0, 0 taken (p ≈ 0.012 under the soft-cost null); player rows are excluded as policy-contaminated. Cost asymmetry runs the same way — wrongly pruning costs one option, wrongly permitting assigns probability to a move the enemy cannot make. One observed enemy play at ≤0 flips it straight back.
2026-08-15 — An exchange requires BOTH sides alive in the `before` state AND no `rewardPathPhase`/`enemyPathPhase` active on it — the boon pickup after a kill otherwise reads as an exchange (`lastMove` persists on both sides, enemy id unchanged, and a Heal boon moves HP so a "did anything change" test passes). This phantom is the sole source of the old count's two `2→2` played moves; the corrected charge totals are 132 played moves, 116 at −1, 14 exceptions all from exactly 1, 264/264 unplayed as specified.
2026-08-15 — `src/sim/corpus.ts` is the only module permitted to know the wire shape; analysis scripts must go through it rather than re-deriving pair-walking rules — chargeTable.ts and verifyCombatModel.ts each shipped a bug those rules exist to prevent, independently.
2026-08-15 — The sim marks any unit touching an unmodelled mechanic UNSCORABLE with a reason code and excludes it from every reported rate; no approximation, no hardcoded zeros. Any claim about win rate must state coverage beside it. Consequence accepted deliberately: a scored *run* is by construction a room-1 death, so run win rate is 0% and uninformative — the room-1 battle rate is the number with content, and `deepestScorableRoom` (currently 1) is the metric that must climb.
2026-08-15 — §4a opponent model must return uniform with `confidence: "low"` below 30 observations for a key, rather than blending 50/50 below 20 — the old blend was already in SPEC and still permitted the enemy-63 "Shield-biased 57%" call off 14 exchanges; a blended wrong read is still a wrong read. The floor is structural so downstream code cannot receive a thin read at all.
2026-08-15 — A boon is modelled ONLY if the corpus has a state pair bracketing its pickup (`rewardPathPhase` state → state where `pickedBoons` grew by exactly one); its effect is never inferred from its name, its option text or its val fields — `UpgradePaper` has `selectedVal2` 4 and a name that says what it does, and stays unmodelled because nobody picked it. Four types have pairs (AddLuck, AddEvasion, Heal, AddBurnSword); seven offered types do not and fail closed as `BOON_UNMODELLED`.
2026-08-15 — `AddBurnSword` changing nothing at pickup is a RESULT, not a gap — the pair proves the pickup applies no stat delta and the effect is latent in combat. Modelled as `{kind:"latent"}` with a verified zero delta, not as an unmodelled type.
2026-08-15 — Task 4.5's gate (`deepestScorableRoom` ≥ 4) is NOT met and was never reachable; it is retired rather than carried forward as a blocker. Enemies 65 and 66 are unscorable innately (rolled stats / Burn+shatterblade), so a perfect boon model caps the number at 2 — the gate asked for something that is a property of the enemies, not of boons. Remaining work is capture, not code.
2026-08-15 — The sim draws boon offers ONLY from the four recorded `rewardPathOptions` triples and never synthesises an offer distribution — the offer set decides how a run develops, and generating it off a sample of four would be inventing the most consequential thing in the model. `SimOptions.offers` exists solely for explicitly-labelled counterfactuals in `scripts/sim.ts` and must never produce a reported number.
2026-08-15 — `ROLLED_STATS` is NOT narrowed despite player-side `evasion 1` (8/9) and `lck 1` (2/2) showing no deviation — counted in damage-taking opportunities the sample is n=9 with one miss, exactly the shape a ~10% dodge proc produces, and the 2026-08-15 opponent-model floor of ~30 observations applies to reading any rate. Narrowing it would unlock room 2 and would be the enemy-63 "Shield-biased 57%" error again.
2026-08-15 — Burn = flat 3 damage per exchange, `amount` non-decrementing, implemented as `BURN_PER_EXCHANGE` and DEFAULT OFF — fits 3/3 exchanges, but the boon's `selectedVal1`, the status `amount` and the damage are all the number 3 from one status instance, so the three candidate rules are one observation, and it is never seen expiring so duration is unknown. Same pattern as `chargesAreHardLimit`. Costs nothing today: the only burning enemy is in room 4, unscorable for ENEMY_BUFF anyway.
2026-08-15 — Rolled-stat and charge-legality evidence must be counted in OPPORTUNITIES (rows where the mechanic could actually fire), never in side-updates or turns — side-update counting inflated player `evasion 1` from 8/9 to 20/21 by including exchanges the player won outright and took no damage in. Same correction as session 04's charge recount; it is the default failure mode of every audit in this repo.
2026-08-15 — The one player-side replay miss (037→038, player took 0 from a 10-ATK tie) is CONFOUNDED and must not be attributed to evasion: it is also the only exchange in the corpus where a side died on a tie, so "evasion dodged" and "a side that dies on an exchange deals no damage" are indistinguishable — and the second needs no new mechanic. One captured die-on-a-tie exchange settles it.
2026-08-16 — Enemy `rolledEnemyStats` and `enemyBuff` are PER-TIER fields on `enemyPathOptions[]`, not properties of the enemy; the session-05 claim that enemies 65/66 are "unscorable innately" is RETRACTED — tier 0 ("Safe") is all zeros with a null buff, both tier-2 options carry non-zero rolls, and the recorded profiles are Dangerous-tier instances the user chose. Task 4.5's gate stays retired on its other two reasons; this one was wrong.
2026-08-16 — Always pick enemy tier 0 ("Safe") — the `lootTable` is IDENTICAL across all three tiers (same table, item, weight and amount 9) in both recorded samples, so higher tiers are pure added risk with zero loot upside, AND they are the sole source of the enemy rolled stats and buffs that make a battle unscorable. Reverses if a deeper room ever shows a tier premium in `LOOT_AMOUNT_CID_array`; both samples are at room 2. Resolves QUESTIONS §6.
2026-08-16 — "A side that dies on an exchange deals no damage" is REFUTED and must not be implemented: run-2026-08-14-03-26-57 004→005 is a clean-model death-on-a-tie where the dying enemy dealt its full 16 ATK. The session-06 brief §4 preferred this on parsimony and asked for it behind a flag; the parsimonious hypothesis was simply wrong. Consequence: the 037→038 miss is now attributable to `evasion`, so player-side evasion evidence stays 8/9 (not 9/9) and evasion probably DOES fire. `ROLLED_STATS` still is NOT narrowed — n=9 remains under the 30-observation floor.
2026-08-16 — Task 5's gate is ROOM-1 BATTLE WIN RATE with non-overlapping 95% CIs, not mean rooms cleared — with `deepestScorableRoom` pinned at 1, rooms-cleared is the room-1 rate with extra steps (always-Sword 1.018 vs its 67.9%). Rooms cleared, coverage and `deepestScorableRoom` are REPORTED beside it, never gated. The rooms-cleared gate moves to Task 11.
2026-08-16 — `src/strategy/` is pure and stateless: `decide(state, model, config, prev)` is a pure function, the opponent model holds counts in memory and exposes `toJSON`/`fromJSON` but never touches disk, and all per-run state (enemy's previous move, our play counts) lives in the `strategyPolicy` adapter. Persistence to `data/opponent-model.json` belongs to the orchestrator, Task 10.
2026-08-16 — SPEC §4a's "maximin fallback over the uncertainty set" is implemented as `(1-λ)·EV + λ·worstCase`, NOT as a separate branch — taking the uncertainty set to be the ε-contamination neighbourhood of the model makes that expression exactly the maximin over it, so one formula covers both regimes and λ merely rises when confidence is low. No cliff in behaviour at the sample floor.
2026-08-16 — §4b's utility normalises HP and armor by the SAME denominator (`hpMax`), not each by its own maximum — the spec's own form gets its own stated requirement backwards: at hpMax 32 / armorMax 15 with `w1=1.0, w3=0.8`, losing 8 armor scores 70% WORSE than losing 8 HP. Sharing one denominator makes `w1 > w3` mean what it reads as. Enemy armor is scored too, or a one-ply evaluation cannot tell a landed hit from a whiffed one.
2026-08-16 — Expectimax depth defaults to 2, not 3 — depth 3 measures ~2 points better (84.2% vs 82.0%) but the confidence intervals OVERLAP and it costs 7× the time, so the difference is not established. Revisit in Task 11 with a real opponent model, where the extra ply may pay for itself.
2026-08-16 — The §4c loot ranking may read boon NAMES; `src/sim/boons.ts` still may not. Choosing between three options is a different act from claiming what they do — the chooser has to use the only information the offer carries, while every effect stays fail-closed. The ranking explicitly does NOT prefer modelled boons: that would raise coverage and `deepestScorableRoom` without playing any better, i.e. tune the metric instead of the game.
2026-08-16 — `src/sim/enemies.ts` PLAYER tracks the NEWEST capture's loadout, not a pinned run — the user changes gear (`armorMax` 15 → 16 this session), so a test pinned to one run keeps passing while the sim models equipment that no longer exists. `tests/enemies.test.ts` asserts the distinct-loadout count so drift is visible; consequence: a baseline measured under one loadout is not comparable to one measured under another, so re-measure both sides of any comparison in the same run.
2026-08-16 — Rolled stats (`evasion/block/lck/tenacity/intuition`) are PERCENT PROC CHANCES, not points — the client renders the boons as "+5% intuition" and "+1% luck" and `selectedVal1` lands verbatim in `.current`. Consequence: every sample-size argument in SPEC §4e was calibrated an order of magnitude too low (`evasion 1` is 1%, not ~10%), so reading one of these rates needs hundreds of observations, not 30. Not narrowing `ROLLED_STATS` was right and is now more strongly right; the reason recorded for it was wrong. Source is option text, so it guides capture and does NOT license modelling a proc.
2026-08-16 — The 037→038 anomaly (player took 0 from a 10-ATK tie) is explained by NEITHER surviving hypothesis and is back to genuinely unexplained: die-on-a-tie is refuted by observation, and evasion at 1% makes a dodge inside nine opportunities an 8.6% event. Do not attribute it to evasion in a later session merely because it is the last hypothesis standing — it is last standing because no third has been proposed.
2026-08-16 — `Regen` ("start each battle with 2 regen, decreases by 1 per turn until 0", val1 2) is offered at room 1 and is the highest-value unmodelled boon in the corpus — §4b's central asymmetry is that HP is not renewable in combat, so a per-battle regenerating resource changes the SHAPE of the utility function rather than its weights. Stays unmodelled until a pickup pair exists; option text is precisely what DECISIONS 2026-08-15 forbids acting on.
2026-08-16 — Corpus-total assertions (exchange counts, side-update counts, boon-pickup counts) are EXPECTED to fail after every capture and must be read one at a time, never reverted — they are the mechanism that forces new data to be looked at. Related: `Exchange.label` is `state-NNN→state-NNN` and is NOT unique across runs, so any assertion naming a specific pair must qualify it with `x.run`; an unqualified one silently started matching a different, legitimate exchange in session 06.
2026-08-17 — Room 3's captured enemy-65 instance is tier 1 ("Risky"), and room 4's captured enemy-66 instance is tier 0 ("Safe") — session 06's "both are Dangerous-tier instances" is CORRECTED, not just its "innate" framing. Re-derived by matching each `enemyPathOptions[]` offer against the state it resolved into. Room 4's `activeEnemyBuff` is null for the whole recorded battle; the Burn status seen there is the PLAYER's own `AddBurnSword` boon (present in that run's `pickedBoons`) landing on a Sword win, not an enemy or tier mechanic. Consequence: under the Safe-tier hard rule, rooms 1/2/4 already have clean Safe captures — the only remaining `deepestScorableRoom` blocker is that no Safe-tier capture of room 3 (enemy 65) exists anywhere in the corpus. `src/sim/enemies.ts`'s `lookupEnemy(3, SAFE_TIER)` is deliberately absent, not invented.
2026-08-17 — Always pick the lowest enemy tier offered (Safe) is now a HARD RULE with a runtime guard (`src/strategy/enemyTier.ts`'s `pickSafeTier()`, `UnsafeTierError`), promoted from the 2026-08-16 preference decision into CLAUDE.md §8 — a scored preference is the kind of thing that gets "optimised away" later by someone reasoning about risk/reward in the abstract, and there is no real tradeoff here (identical loot table, zero upside on higher tiers) for a preference to weigh in the first place.
2026-08-17 — HP persists across a room transition unchanged, confirmed at SIX sub-cap crossings (2/32, 15/32, 31/32, 22/32, 12/32, 4/32, 28/32 across seven boundaries, all unchanged) — strengthens the 2026-08-15 armor-only finding, which had exactly one informative HP data point, to a directly-verified claim about HP specifically. Consequence: `Regen` ("2 regen/battle, -1/turn") compounds across a run rather than resetting with it if it fires every room (~3 HP × up to 16 rooms against a 30 HP pool) — reported per session-06 brief §4 as a capture finding, NOT modelled; `Regen`'s in-combat mechanic still has no pickup pair and §4b is not re-derived on a hypothesis about its size.
2026-08-17 — Expectimax depth 3 is ADOPTED for live play, superseding the 2026-08-16 "stays at 2" decision — re-run at N=20000 (`scripts/depthAblation.ts`, vs. the gate's N=1000) SEPARATES depth 1/2/3 with non-overlapping 95% CIs (77.14% / 79.96% / 81.64% room-1 battle win rate), settling the difference the original ablation couldn't. Depth 4 (82.62%) is NOT separated from depth 3 (gap 0.98pp overlaps) and is not adopted. The "7x the time" reason for staying at 2 does not apply live: depth 3 costs on the order of a millisecond per decision against a 1200ms rate-limit floor. `src/strategy/config.ts` now exports `LIVE_CONFIG` (depth 3) alongside `DEFAULT_CONFIG` (depth 2, unchanged — `scripts/sim.ts` runs thousands of iterations per invocation and that cost is not free in aggregate). Task 6's orchestrator should use `LIVE_CONFIG`.
2026-08-14 (session 08) — `config/bot.json` written with real numbers, un-deferring the 2026-08-12 decision — `dailyEnergyBudget: 60` (3 runs × 20 energy), `maxRunsPerSession: 3`, `maxConsecutiveActionFailures: 3` — session-08 brief §5's explicit conservative first-session figures, not a guess. Dungeon economics (`energyCost`, `maxRoom`, `maxRunsPerDay`) stay in `config/discovered.json` (generated) rather than being duplicated into `bot.json`, per SPEC §6's architecture split between user-editable and generated config.
2026-08-14 (session 08) — Task 6 could not run live this session — `npm run check-auth` fails with HTTP 401, confirmed independently with a raw curl request against `/user/me`. Per CLAUDE.md ("when you get stuck", "missing private key/JWT" is a blocking condition), logged to QUESTIONS.md §7 and NOT retried in a loop. `scripts/liveRun.ts` was built and unit-tested against a mocked client instead, so all four stages are code-complete and ready the moment a fresh JWT lands.
2026-08-14 (session 08) — The `enemyPathOptions`/`rewardPathOptions` selection action (what a live loop POSTs to choose a tier or a boon) is UNCONFIRMED — SPEC §2's action list only names `loot_one`…`loot_four` as index-selecting actions, with no separate name for "choose enemy path" or "choose reward". `scripts/liveRun.ts`'s `selectByIndex()` sends `loot_<n>` for whichever phase is active, as a stated hypothesis (file header, inline comments) rather than a confirmed fact — CLAUDE.md §2 forbids inventing an endpoint, not forming a testable hypothesis about an argument value on the one confirmed endpoint. A wrong guess fails closed through `guards.ts` (`recordActionResult(false)`, 3-strike halt) rather than corrupting a run. Confirm or correct this the moment a real response comes back — same discipline as `DungeonActionResponseSchema`.
2026-08-14 (session 08) — `intuition`'s rare-field hypothesis (session-08 brief addendum §7, check 1) is CHECKED AND FOUND NOTHING, not narrowed to zero effect — `scripts/fieldFrequency.ts` over all 230 corpus side-observations: every key is present 100% of the time (rules out "appears as an extra field"), and the four normally-empty array fields are non-empty in 0/230, 0/230, 0/230, 3/230 (Burn, already known). `intuition.current` is non-zero in only 6/230 observations — nowhere near the "hundreds of observations" floor SPEC §4e already established for a 1–5% proc, so this is evidence of insufficient exposure, not evidence of no effect. Check 2 (live-loop unexpected-key detection, `unknownSideKeys()` in `scripts/liveRun.ts`) is built and wired into the poll loop but, like the rest of Task 6, unexecuted this session.
2026-08-14 (session 08, live) — `/game/dungeon/state`'s "no active run" state has a SECOND wire shape beyond the 500-HTML page recorded 2026-08-14 earlier: an account with no run started at all this session returns HTTP 200, `{success:true, actionToken:0, data:{run:null, entity:null}}`. Found on Task 6 stage 1's first-ever live read, which failed a zod check the moment the JWT was fixed. `DungeonStateSchema` required `data.run` to be a full run object; `DungeonStateOrIdleSchema` (new) allows `null` on `run`/`entity`, and `getDungeonState()` now returns `null` for either "no active run" shape, not just the 500 case. Both wire shapes were live-confirmed the same session, ~15 minutes apart.
2026-08-14 (session 08, live) — `GET /game/dungeon/state`'s `actionToken` field is NOT fresh — it reports 0 regardless of the run's real state, confirmed 3x on one live run (a POST's real token was immediately clobbered back to 0 by the next state read, run state otherwise unchanged). `getDungeonState()` no longer updates the tracked token; only `POST /game/dungeon/action` responses do. SPEC §2's "always send the newest actionToken" holds for actions, not this read endpoint.
2026-08-14 (session 08, live) — The reward-path pick action is `reward_one` (`reward_two`/`three`/`four` inferred, not individually confirmed), NOT `loot_one` as originally hypothesized from SPEC §2's action list — `loot_one` was rejected HTTP 409 on live stage 3, the user captured the real client's `reward_one` request via DevTools. Its envelope also differs from combat/start_run: `dungeonId: 0` (not the real dungeon id) and `actionToken: ""` (empty string, not a number), plus four previously-unseen `data` fields (`itemId`, `expectedAmount`, `gearInstanceIds`, `devBoons`, all zero/empty in this capture). `enemy_one`/`enemy_two`/`enemy_three` for the enemy-tier pick are a NEW hypothesis on the same naming pattern, NOT confirmed — that pick was resolved by the user clicking in-browser on this run, not through the bot's client.
2026-08-14 (session 08, live) — Starting a `start_run` action while a run is already active is rejected HTTP 400 (`"Error starting dungeon"`) — confirmed live on stage 3's first attempt (stage 2 had left a run active, untouched). `scripts/liveRun.ts`'s `runOnce()` now checks `getDungeonState()` before deciding whether to `start_run`, and resumes an existing run instead of attempting a duplicate start.
2026-08-14 (session 08, live) — An HTTP 500 from `POST /game/dungeon/action` does NOT reliably mean the action didn't apply — confirmed both ways on the same run, back to back. A `reward_one` pick for `AddIntuition` returned 500; a state re-check showed the boon HAD applied (`intuition.current` 0→10, `pickedBoons` grew). The very next room's `reward_one` pick for `UpgradeRock` also returned 500, and that one genuinely hadn't applied (`pickedBoons` unchanged). Both requests were byte-identical in shape. Consequence: after ANY action failure, the only safe move is to re-verify live state before deciding whether to retry — never assume "error means nothing happened" (that would justify a blind retry and risk double-applying) and never assume "error means something broke" (that would justify giving up when a retry is actually safe). `scripts/liveRun.ts`'s `postWithVerifiedRetry()` implements this: re-check state after every POST failure, retry only while a predicate confirms the action is still genuinely pending, rely on `guards.recordActionResult`'s existing 3-consecutive-failure threshold as the sole retry ceiling.
2026-08-14 (session 08, live) — User-confirmed game flow, for SPEC's record (not previously documented end to end): combat win → dropped loot ("Dendren roots") auto-collects on walking over it, used to manually level Max HP/Max ARM/Tenacity/Block/Evasion/Luck/Intuition between runs (not automated, out of scope for the bot) → clicking the defeated enemy's spot completes the stage and reveals three reward cards, each showing a "Hard Cores" value (the leaderboard currency the whole project is farming for) alongside its effect → picking one reveals three enemy-tier options for the next room, where picking a modifier tier raises the reward potential of that fight. Confirms the mechanical picture SPEC already had (rewardPathPhase → enemyPathPhase → next room) from the human side. Likely wire correlate, not yet verified: `rewardPathOptions[].gigusOrbItemId`/`gigusOrbAmount` (captured live on the Heal card as `845`/`19`) is the only per-card numeric field that isn't a boon stat — "Hard Cores" is plausibly the display name for a Gigus Orb amount, but this is a naming guess, not confirmed by a captured UI-to-field mapping.
2026-08-14 (session 08, live) — The enemy-tier pick action is `path_<n>` (confirmed: `path_two`), NOT `enemy_<n>` as hypothesized on the `reward_*` naming pattern — `enemy_two` failed 3/3 live (2× HTTP 500, 1× HTTP 400 on an otherwise identical retry, the 400 being the tell that this was a wrong name, not flakiness), the user then captured the real client sending `path_two` via DevTools. 2026-08-15 (session 09) — `GuardState` now takes an optional seed (`{energySpent, runsStarted}`) and `src/orchestrator/guardPersistence.ts` persists it to `data/guard-budget.json` keyed by UTC date — closes the gap flagged at the end of session 08 (STATE.md: "each `npm run live` invocation builds a fresh `GuardState`, so the 60-energy session budget isn't tracked across the several separate invocations"). `GuardState` itself stays fs-free (persistence is a separate module, called from `scripts/liveRun.ts`'s two mutation sites) per its own "no network, no fs" testability contract. 2026-08-15 (session 09, live) — `runOnce`'s `guards.assertCanStartRun(...)` call is now conditional on actually being about to send a NEW `start_run`, never on resuming an already-active run. It previously ran unconditionally at the top of the function, before the existing-run check — a real ordering bug, invisible through session 08 because guard state never persisted across process invocations so the session cap never actually bound. Session 09's persistence fix (above) made it bind for the first time, and it immediately stranded a live run at room 2 (HP 2/32, mid-combat): a later invocation had pushed `runsStarted` to the cap, and every subsequent attempt to RESUME the stranded run — which costs no new run slot — was rejected by the same check meant for new starts. Fixed with a regression test (`tests/liveRun.test.ts`, "resumes an already-active run even at the session cap") pinning both halves: resuming is never blocked, a genuinely new start still is.

2026-08-16 (session 10) — Task 5's gate (room-1 battle win rate) is RETIRED to the permanent reported-metrics block, not re-run or tuned against; Task 11's mean-rooms-cleared gate is PROMOTED to the live dungeon objective — session-10 brief §2's call, made once `deepestScorableRoom` reached 4 (session 09) and made the room-1-only framing stale: the bot's five live runs all died before room 5 despite an 81.8% room-1 battle rate, so "wins the opening fight" and "wins the run" had already come apart. See TASKS.md Tasks 5 and 11.
2026-08-16 (session 10) — `scripts/deathRooms.ts` groups the corpus by `DUNGEON_ID_CID` ACROSS capture directories, not within one directory as `loadCorpus`'s per-dir grouping does — one bot-driven live run frequently spans several directories (each `npm run live` invocation writes its own), and grouping only within a directory would silently count one real run as several. Only attempts ending at player HP 0 count as a death; every pre-session-08 capture is a human-supervised research session that stopped without a death (captured for combat-model verification, not played to a game over), and folding those in would mix "the researcher stopped recording" into "the bot died" — they are reported separately.
2026-08-16 (session 10) — **Death-room histogram (6 confirmed deaths across the corpus): room 2 ×2, room 3 ×2, room 4 ×2, room 1 ×0 — spread evenly across rooms 2–4, not clustered at 2–3.** Per session-10 brief §1's own diagnostic ("if deaths cluster at 2–3 it's early-room HP economy, if they spread evenly it's enemy scaling"), this reads as enemy scaling, not early-room HP mismanagement — though n=6 is thin. Corroborated independently by the utility-weight sweep below, which found no lever in the direction an HP-economy problem would predict.
2026-08-16 (session 10) — `src/strategy/utility.ts`'s win terminal is no longer flat: a win now scores `winValue + base` (the same continuous HP/armor margin the non-terminal case computes) instead of a bare `winValue`, so the engine can tell a win that leaves HP/armor for the next room from one that barely survived — previously it was indifferent between the two whenever both were reachable within the search horizon, i.e. it scored each battle as if it were the run's last one. Death is deliberately NOT given the same margin (stays exactly `deathPenalty(room, cfg)`): a death forfeits the room's loot and every room after it regardless of leftover HP/armor, so there is nothing left to price, unlike a win, which carries state into the next battle. One test (`tests/strategy.test.ts`, "decides that case on expectation") rewritten to identify a winning cell by actually resolving the exchange rather than by `.value === cfg().winValue`, an equality the change necessarily breaks.
2026-08-16 (session 10) — The win-margin fix above (and a matching continuity fix to §4c's `Heal` urgency bonus, step function → continuous in `1 - hpFraction`) is TESTED AND FOUND TO HAVE NO MEASURABLE EFFECT on `meanRoomsCleared` or room-1 win rate, even with the weight sweep amplified 10× (`weights.hp` and `depthBonus` each independently and combined, N=20000, seed 1 — every number lands inside every other's 95% CI; see TASKS.md Task 11 for the table). Both changes are kept — each fixes a real defect and neither regresses anything (236/236 tests, `npm run sim`'s Task 5 report unchanged) — but neither is reported as the fix for attrition: the null result, read together with the even death-room histogram above, points at single-battle lethality escalating with room depth as the binding constraint at the corpus's current observable depth, not cross-room HP mismanagement that a utility-weight retune could address. A gate that could not be moved by a 10× amplification is not a gate waiting on better-tuned numbers; it is evidence the lever is the wrong one.

2026-08-15 (session 13) — `cardChoice.ts`'s `chooseCard` had a real bug, not a design choice: it picked `argmax EV(card,f) / card.manaCost`, but SPEC.md §5 was itself wrong to specify that (session-13 brief §1, corroborated by the real cast escaping with 5/10 mana unspent — the MISS counter capped it, not mana). Fixed to `argmax P_hit(card,f)` (hit probability), mana as an affordability filter only, with EV/mana retained solely as a late-cast correction (`isManaConstrained`) for when mana genuinely can't finish the fish. Uncovered a SECOND, dependent bug in the same fix: `shouldRedraw` compared `best.evPerMana` against its threshold when SPEC always specified raw `ev` — harmless while `chooseCard`'s own objective was also EV/mana, but once it wasn't, the mismatch triggered redraw almost every turn and erased most of the gain. Both fixed together: 500-synthetic-cast catch rate moved from 19.0% (95/500) to 92.4% (462/500), unchanged 7.8% random baseline. `REDRAW_THRESHOLD` re-tuned 3→0 (`src/sim/fishing/castSim.ts`, sweep in its own comment).
2026-08-15 (session 13, live) — `focusMeter`'s spend rule is CONFIRMED, resolving the `[VERIFY]` left by the one prior capture (which never moved it off 3/3): moving the focus costs its Manhattan distance from the CURRENT focus, out of a 3-point per-cast budget that does NOT regenerate within a cast. Four clean data points from this project's first-ever live fishing cast — three moves at distance 0/1/1 (meter 3→3→2→1) and a fourth at distance 2 with only 1 point left, REJECTED server-side with HTTP 400 (not a display-only number). `cardChoice.ts`'s `chooseCard`/`bestFocusForCard` gained an optional `FocusBudget` param (`src/sim/fishing/geometry.ts`'s new `reachableCells`) so the live loop never proposes an unreachable focus again; `src/sim/fishing/castSim.ts` does NOT model this yet, so its 92.4% catch-rate figure (above) assumes free focus movement and is an optimistic ceiling, not a live prediction.
2026-08-15 (session 13, live) — Task 12 Stage A's `use_item` blocker is CONFIRMED: `POST /game/dungeon/action` with `{action:"use_item", dungeonId:5, actionToken:<real>, data:{consumables:[], isJuiced:false, index:0, itemId:0}}` (the COMBAT-style envelope, not reward/path-style) returned HTTP 400 `{"success":false,"message":"Item not found in index"}` — a clean, meaningful rejection (not 404/405 wrong-endpoint, not a crash), confirming the action name, the combat-style envelope, and that `itemId` addresses the target item. 2026-08-15 (session 14) — Sim authority is earned PER DOMAIN, against live outcomes, and never inherited from another domain's sim — the dungeon sim's own live-vs-sim agreement (session 11: predicted 1.946 mean rooms cleared vs. live 2.0) said nothing about the fishing sim's trustworthiness, and the fishing sim's 92.4% (session 13) turned out to be badly wrong. No sim's number should be used to justify a design decision until it has demonstrated agreement with real outcomes in that same domain. See SPEC.md §5.
2026-08-15 (session 14, live+offline) — `focusMeter` is now MODELLED in `src/sim/fishing/castSim.ts` (new `FOCUS_METER_MAX=3`, `defaultStartFocus`, tracked per-cast budget threaded through `chooseCard` via `FocusBudget`), settling whether it explains the session-13 sim-vs-live gap (92.4% sim vs. 0/6 live). PARTIAL explanation only: modelling it alone drops the 500-cast catch rate to 69.9–71.6% (two independent runs) — real, but still statistically incompatible with 0/6 (P≈0.05%). The dominant cause is a SEPARATE, larger gap: the sim's true fish pattern is always drawn from the same synthetic pool the matcher searches, unlike real Dendren, whose pattern library is unknown and has never been matched by any of this project's six real casts. `castSim.ts` gained a `matcherPool` option (independent of `candidatePool`, which still controls the true pattern) to make this reproducible: `matcherPool: []` forces the matcher permanently blind, dropping the rate to ~7–10%, indistinguishable from random and consistent with live 0/6 (P≈55–65%). See `scripts/fishFocusMeter.ts`, `tests/fishing/castSim.test.ts`'s session-14 describe block, SPEC.md §5.

2026-08-16 (session 15) — `~/Downloads` is now DENIED to Read/Glob/Grep in `.claude/settings.local.json` — user directive, after a whole-filesystem `find /` (run while searching for a community notes file previously used in this project, per CLAUDE.md §9) surfaced unrelated personal file paths outside the project. The deny rule is a partial fix: Bash's permission matching is command-prefix based, not path-aware, so it cannot generically block an arbitrary shell command that happens to reference that path — only the dedicated file tools are reliably covered. Should not be needed going forward regardless, since the community-notes file's content is already captured in this session's SPEC-fishing.md citations.

Under the shared-pool hypothesis, the break-even math favors spending that energy on MORE RUNS instead (≈1.21 rooms per potion's-worth of energy vs. potions' 0.534) — the upper-bound sim only strengthens this conclusion for a more honest (imperfectly-timed) model. 2026-08-16 (session 16) — `dungeonSim.ts` now models potion TIMING (`SimOptions.potions: {heals, threshold}`), superseding `scripts/potionSweep.ts`'s all-committed-at-room-1 model, kept for the record but no longer the best answer — a heal fires the instant own HP fraction crosses the threshold, checked once per exchange, threaded across rooms within a run via a mutable queue. Still an explicit upper bound (heal application modelled free — no exchange/charges cost — since that half was unconfirmed in the sim at write time); NEW this session vs. the old model: the heal restores current HP only, and does NOT raise `hpMax` — the old script's `p.hpMax += bonus` was a real modelling error (a heal item isn't a stat boost). `scripts/potionTimingSweep.ts` (new) sweeps thresholds {0.2, 0.34, 0.5} × {1,2,3} potions, N=2000: threshold 0.5 (proactive) beats 0.2 (reactive) at every loadout size — waiting until critical risks a single exchange crossing the check point before it ever fires. Best row (0.5, 3 potions): 3.477 ± 0.034 mean rooms cleared vs. 2.130 ± 0.051 baseline (+1.347).

Isolated via `git worktree` (not `git checkout -- .`, which doesn't remove files added in later commits and gave a false "still broken at the earlier commit" reading on first attempt) — confirmed clean at `5b406fd` (the commit before `db1f76c`), broken at `a3bb9ae` (after it). Fixed: added the 3 new offers to `OBSERVED_OFFERS` (`src/sim/boons.ts`) with sourced commentary, updated every stale literal. 2026-08-16 (session 19) — `POST /roms/factory-claim` is CONFIRMED (user DevTools capture) and now live-verified: the claimed amount lands in the SAME spendable energy pool `GET /offchain/player/energy` reports (before/after read around a successful romId-2097 claim, +1 energy net of regen) — not a separate currency. But the captured request's `amount` field (57 for this ROM) does NOT determine the real payout: the actual credited delta was ~1.0 energy, roughly two orders of magnitude smaller. `amount` omitted entirely (romId 7959) got a clean-looking but uninformative HTTP 500 `{"error":{}}`, so the field is required in some form without controlling the outcome. This REVISES the session-19 brief's framing ("may remove the real bottleneck") — a single real claim delivering ~1 energy does not look like a bottleneck-removing lever on its own; whether it matters depends on (a) how many ROMs this wallet owns (no enumeration endpoint found — checked `/user/me`, `/game/account/{address}`, `/offchain/static`, none list owned ROMs) and (b) the claim cooldown, only lower-bounded at >34s this session (an immediate re-claim of the just-claimed ROM failed with the same uninformative 500 seen on the ROM that never once succeeded). See SPEC.md's new "ROM factory-claim" section and `fixtures/probe/roms/attempt{1,2,3,4}-*.json`.
