2026-08-17 (session 23, INCIDENT) — A `liveRun.ts` invocation silently resumed a pre-existing active dungeon run it did not start, playing it to a room-2 death under the invocation's own `--potions=0` policy. That run was later established to be unrelated to the user's manually-started juiced run (the user's juiced run had already committed 3x Big Heal Juice via the "dungeon sack" before this session began — see below), but the local guard-tracked run count was ALSO found to have drifted 3 runs behind the real server count (8 vs. 11) because it never sees manually-started runs. Root fix (not a workaround): `liveRun.ts` now REFUSES to resume any run it didn't start in the current invocation unless `--resume-existing` is explicitly passed (throws `ResumeConfirmationRequired`, no POST sent) — `opts.requireResumeConfirmation` is set only on a `--runs=N` loop's first iteration, so it never blocks resuming across iterations of the same invocation, and `orchestrator.ts`'s continuous unattended loop is deliberately exempt (no opts passed → no confirmation required, matching its designed always-resume behavior). Separately, every real (non-`--status`) invocation now reads and prints the REAL server-side runs-used-today count (`GET /game/dungeon/today`'s `dayProgressEntities`, new `findRealRunsToday()`) alongside the local guard number, flagging drift immediately rather than letting it compound silently across a batch.
2026-08-17 (session 23, user-confirmed) — "Juice"/"juiced" are THREE unrelated game concepts sharing one word, root cause of the above incident's confusion: (1) `isPlayerJuiced` is an account-level PURCHASED buff (more energy, more ROM output, 4x Hard Cores across dungeons and fishing) — nothing to do with a specific run; (2) "Juice" as an item name (Big Heal Juice, etc.) — ordinary potions; (3) a "Juiced" Forbidden Woods RUN MODE — 60 energy (3x normal), consumes 3 of the 12 daily run-count units (not 1, confirmed against the real `dayProgressEntities` counter moving 3→6 after one juiced start), requires clearing only 1 room's worth of fights same as a normal run, and pays 3x every room's reward (5 Dendren Root → 15 at room 1). `juicedMultiplier: 1` in `config/discovered.json` does NOT represent this 3x and must not be read as the reward multiplier for anything (SPEC.md corrected). Economic rationale for combining potions with juiced mode (user's own framing): 3 Big Heal Juice across one juiced run buys the yield of 3 normal runs, vs. 9 Big Heal Juice needed to match it via 3 separate normal runs.
2026-08-17 (session 23, user directive) — The "dungeon sack" will be left EMPTY going forward, user's own choice, rather than relying on the bot detecting/clearing whatever's pre-loaded in it — the sack persists potions across dungeon entries and commits them at `start_run` regardless of that request's own `consumables` field, which is what let the incident above happen invisibly. Potion loading is the bot's job via `consumables` on a genuinely NEW `start_run`, confirmed still working correctly this session (a fresh start with `consumables: []`, Big Heal Juice balance unchanged 70→70). Equipping potions specifically when entering a JUICED run — the user's actual ask — is scoped as TASKS.md Task 14, blocked on a live capture of the real juiced `start_run` request body (CLAUDE.md §2 forbids guessing at it, and guessing here is exactly what caused this incident).

2026-08-17 (session 24, INCIDENT) — Task 10's live 2-hour orchestrator gate run committed 3x Big Heal Juice (`consumables: [131, 131, 131]` on `start_run`, `isJuiced: false`) on a genuinely PLAIN run, contradicting the user's standing rule that non-juiced runs never use potions. User caught it from the live client and Ctrl-C'd the run before further damage; confirmed real balance dropped exactly 70→67 (3 units), matching the 3 committed at `start_run` exactly — no more, no less. Root cause, checked directly rather than assumed: `config/bot.json`'s `forbiddenWoods.potions.maxPerRun` was still `3`, the value session 23 raised it to specifically for that session's juiced-Tier-3 batch (own comment: "Ask the user whether to revert to 2 after this batch") — that batch never happened (session 23 became incident response instead), so the elevated value sat unrevered into session 24's plain orchestrator run. This is NOT a cap-enforcement bug: the log shows exactly 3 committed (matching the stale config) and exactly 2 `use_item` POSTs actually fired in combat (both HTTP 200, using 2 of the 3 loaded) — the cap did exactly what the config said, no overrun. The deeper defect is structural, not this session's stale number: `liveRun.ts`/`orchestrator.ts` apply `forbiddenWoods.potions` unconditionally to EVERY `start_run` they send — there is no juiced-vs-plain gating in code at all yet. That gating is TASKS.md Task 14 (still BLOCKED on a live juiced-`start_run` capture), whose own scoped design already says potions should load "ONLY when starting a genuinely new juiced run." Until Task 14 lands, ANY nonzero `forbiddenWoods.potions` value — not just today's stale 3 — would have auto-loaded potions on a plain run. Fix applied this session: the `potions` block is REMOVED from `config/bot.json` entirely (absence = 0 potions, the established fail-closed contract from session 17 — `src/orchestrator/config.ts`'s schema requires `maxPerRun` positive, so 0 isn't a valid in-block value; removal is the only way to actually zero it). Do not re-add this block until Task 14 implements juiced-only gating in code — re-adding it before then reproduces this exact incident.

2026-08-17 (session 24, live) — Resuming the incident's stray room-5 run (Enemy Room 67, own HP 9/42, user-confirmed 1 Big Heal Juice left in slot 3 of that run's own already-committed consumables — the OTHER 2 of the original 3 had already been consumed by the interrupted orchestrator invocation before the Ctrl-C) surfaced a SECOND, distinct resume-boundary bug from the same root cause class as session 20's: `usePotionLive`'s index is `potionPolicy.used`, which is PROCESS-LOCAL and always seeds at 0 — correct for a run this same invocation started, wrong for a run whose consumables were already partly used by an earlier, now-dead invocation. The server's per-slot index doesn't reset (slot 0 stays "used" permanently for that run), so retrying at index 0 got the same HTTP 400 "Item not found in index" session 15 first saw. Fixed with a new `--potions-used=N` CLI flag (`scripts/liveRun.ts`) that seeds `potionPolicy.used` from the real count instead of assuming 0 — used live as `--resume-existing --potions=1 --potions-used=2`, which correctly targeted index 2 (slot 3) and got HTTP 200, healing 9→29. The run continued via the normal Safe-tier/expectimax strategy, dealt real damage across 6 exchanges (enemy 45→19 HP) but a bad final exchange under thin opponent-model data (n=0–6 samples, "confidence=low" throughout — genuinely under-sampled for THIS specific enemy, not a bug) killed the player at HP 0 vs the enemy's remaining 19/45. Room-5 stray run is now fully resolved either way (win or death both end it) — account confirmed clean afterward (`--dry-run`: "no active run"). The temporary `config/bot.json` potions re-add used to make this possible was reverted to the safe absent state immediately after this one invocation, per the incident fix above.

2026-08-17 (session 25, live) — Task 10's 2-hour orchestrator gate is MET, retired as a recurring verification target — real wall-clock time to exhaust both daily caps (12 dungeon runs, 20 fishing casts) was ~45 minutes end to end (~27 of which was one energy-regen sleep), settling a number this project had guessed at across four prior sessions (originally 8 hours). Zero unhandled exceptions, zero potions used, clean cap recognition and idle, energy within budget on both modes, scheduler balanced the two modes by relative daily-budget headroom throughout (matches `scheduler.ts`'s session-19 design, confirmed live over a full multi-hour run rather than one smoke test).
2026-08-17 (session 25, live) — `VulnerableEvade` and `AddLifestealMagic` are MODELLED as `{kind:"latent"}` (zero delta at pickup, contaminates `["STATUS_EFFECT"]`) — both got their first-ever pickup pairs this session (`run-2026-08-17-20-37-00` state-009→010, `run-2026-08-17-21-14-12` state-035→036), both show zero change to any tracked player field, same shape as `AddBurnSword`/`CorrosiveShield`/`CorrosiveMagic`. Whatever they arm in combat stays unconfirmed and is not inferred from their names, per the standing rule.
2026-08-17 (session 25) — The energy-regen sleep in `orchestrator.ts` cannot be shortened or cancelled by an external energy top-up without ending the whole unattended session — `sleepUntil()` computes its duration once, from the energy value at the moment the scheduler decided to sleep, and only re-polls on wake or process restart; a single SIGINT during a sleep sets `shutdown.ts`'s `requested` flag on the FIRST press, which both `sleepUntil`'s inner loop and `main()`'s outer `while` loop check, so it ends the entire run rather than just skipping the current wait. Confirmed live this session when the user manually topped energy to 420/420 mid-sleep and had no way to signal it in. Not fixed — flagged as an open question for whether it's worth building a lighter-weight "skip this sleep" signal.
2026-08-17 (session 27, live-loop code + fixture audit) — The live fishing loop DOES actively reposition the focus point every turn; this is NOT a no-op or an always-centered default. Confirmed two ways: (1) reading `scripts/liveFishing.ts` (line ~541) and `src/strategy/fishing/cardChoice.ts` end to end — `chooseCard`/`bestFocusForCard` search every cell `reachableCells()` allows under the live `focusBudget` and argmax raw EV against the predicted fish distribution, then the chosen `[x,y]` is sent on every `play_cards` POST; (2) the fixture corpus — `data.focusPoint` actually changes value within-cast (with `focusMeter` decrementing in lockstep) in **29 of 30** committed live casts. The user's "catch rate feels very low" concern is real (see the entry below) but its cause is NOT a broken/no-op focus mechanic.
2026-08-17 (session 27) — The "~70% sim catch-rate baseline" cited in the 2026-08-17 (session 21) entry above is the MATCHER-OMNISCIENT ceiling (`matcherPool` defaults to the same pool the true pattern is drawn from — the matcher can, in principle, always identify the fish's true movement rule), not a live-comparable number — this was already established by DECISIONS.md 2026-08-15 (session 14) but the session-21 entry restated the figure without that qualifier, which is exactly the kind of framing that invites a future reader to treat live's ~13% as anomalous. The live loop's matcher genuinely starts EMPTY every cast (`scripts/liveFishing.ts`'s own header) and is seeded only from `mineFishPatterns.ts`-promoted patterns (2 promoted as of this session: `perimeterWalk(cw)`/`(ccw)`, support 4/3 respectively, out of 50 real casts logged) — the correct comparison is the MATCHER-BLIND sim baseline, re-run this session at 6.6% (500 casts, `matcherPool: []`), or 20.8% with the current 2-pattern mined library seeded. Real observed catch rate across the 30 committed live-cast fixtures is **4/30 caught (~13.3%)** — statistically consistent with "mostly blind, partially helped by 2 mined patterns," not with the omniscient 70% figure, and not anomalously low relative to what this project's own sim already predicts for this exact regime. Consequence: the path to a materially higher live catch rate is more mined-pattern coverage (Task 11, already active/working — support grew 1→2 patterns between session 18 and this session purely from ordinary play) and/or Task 13's deck-composition scoring, not a fix to the focus-point mechanic, which is already working as designed.
2026-08-17 (session 27) — The session-27 brief's "Fintuition explains `nextPosition`" hypothesis is CHECKED AND NOT CONFIRMED — see QUESTIONS.md §12 for the full derivation. Two problems found by reading the raw fixture JSON directly: the brief's 8/225 (3.56%) figure counts turns where the key is merely PRESENT (including 6 `null` turns after a single real firing persists the key), not turns where it actually fired — the real firing count is 2/225 (0.89%), undershooting the stated 3% Fintuition rate rather than matching it; and `activeFintuitionTurns`/`fintuitionOilBoostPercent`, the only candidate trigger fields this project reads, are constant `0`/`null` across ALL 225 turns of the entire corpus (not just at the 2 `nextPosition` sightings) — a field that never varies cannot confirm or refute anything, so session 26's "ruled out as the gate" phrasing overstated what that check actually showed. Per CLAUDE.md §9, this correction is recorded here rather than implementing any Fintuition-reactive strategy code off the brief's unverified claim. Status unchanged from session 26 in substance: real, rare (now more precisely ~0.9–1%, not ~3.6%), cause unconfirmed.
2026-08-18 (session 27, live) — The LOCAL fishing guard budget (`data/guard-budget-fishing.json`, UTC-calendar-date-keyed per `todayKey()`) rolled over to a fresh 0/20 at UTC midnight as designed, but the REAL server-side daily fishing cap has NOT — confirmed by a direct `start_run` attempt at 2026-08-18 03:33 UTC (guard showed 0/20 used, correctly fresh), rejected HTTP 400 with body `{"message":"Player has reached max runs for fishing"}`. `liveFishing.ts`'s guard correctly fail-closed on the very first attempt (0 energy spent, confirmed by the before/after accounting print: 228→228) — this is CLAUDE.md §5 working exactly as designed, not a bug. But it means the real fishing daily-reset boundary is NOT UTC midnight (or is a rolling window rather than a calendar boundary) — the same class of mismatch session 23's dungeon-run-count-drift incident found on the dungeon side, now confirmed independently on fishing. Consequence: the local guard's "fresh day" reading cannot be trusted as "server has real budget available" — a real POST attempt (or a read of whatever endpoint reports the true reset time, unconfirmed to exist for fishing — `GET /game/dungeon/today`'s `dayProgressEntities` is the dungeon-side equivalent, session 23) is the only way to know for certain. No live fishing casts were completed this session as a result — this was discovered on the FIRST attempted cast of a planned batch, so the batch stopped immediately, per fail-closed. QUESTIONS.md has the open item on when the real reset actually happens.
2026-08-17 (session 27, fixture audit) — Dual Yield (user-reported skill, level 2, stated 4% chance to catch 2 fish at once) has NEVER fired in the existing fixture corpus, checked directly rather than assumed: every real catch's `caughtFish` is a single object (never an array or multiple entries, 15 catch-turn observations across the corpus — some catches appear on 2 consecutive turns as the field persists into the `loot`-resolution response, same persistence pattern as `nextPosition`), and the single largest `gameItemBalanceChanges` array seen (`cast-2026-08-17-00-03-25` state-023) has exactly 2 entries — one fish item (`id 517`, amount 1) and one currency/orb credit (`id 845`, amount 160, matching the `gigusOrbItemId` pattern from DECISIONS 2026-08-14 session 08), never two distinct fish. `dualYieldOilBoostPercent` is constant `0` throughout, same as `fintuitionOilBoostPercent` (no oil ever equipped). Given ~4% per catch and roughly 8-10 real catches on record, an expected ~0.3-0.4 fires — not seeing it yet is consistent with insufficient exposure, not evidence it doesn't exist or that `loot`'s single-fish handling is broken. No code change; revisit once more real catches accumulate, and `unknownDocKeys`' existing detector (session 26 widening) would already surface a genuinely new response shape if one ever landed.
2026-08-18 (session 30) — Dual Yield: forward detection only, per the user's own framing — the skill was added to the account AFTER the existing corpus was captured, so there is nothing to backfill (session 27's audit above stands unchanged, not re-run). `scripts/liveFishing.ts`'s new `detectPossibleDualYield` watches every `play_cards` and `loot` response for two independent signals that don't bet on one specific double-catch shape: 2+ `FISH_DIED` entries in one response's `data.events[]`, or 2+ DISTINCT non-currency item ids in one response's `gameItemBalanceChanges` (currency id 845 "Hard Core" excluded, since a normal single catch already credits fish+currency together). On a hit, dumps the full raw response to `logs/fishing-unknown-dual-yield-*.json` and logs `possible_dual_yield_event` — same "loud dump, no strategy change" pattern as the existing unknown-field detector. No live occurrence yet; this only makes the next one impossible to miss.
2026-08-17 (session 26) — Fishing's `data.nextPosition`/`data.nextMovePath` (session 25's find) is REAL but RARE, not the standing per-turn look-ahead either prior framing assumed — checked entirely against the existing committed fixture corpus (30 live casts, 225 turns, `fixtures/fishing-casts/live/`), no new casts spent. The field appears on 8/225 turns across only 2/30 casts: neither "terminal-doc only" (the original catch-resolution guess, `unknownDocKeys` previously only checked terminal docs, now widened to every turn) nor "every turn" (the hopeful look-ahead reading) describes it. The one checkable instance (`cast-2026-08-17-20-40-57` turn 1 → turn 2) matched exactly on both fields, but n=1 does not establish a mechanic — same standard this project applies to every other percent-chance-shaped observation (`ROLLED_STATS`, `intuition`, etc.). No candidate trigger (fintuition/critBoost turns, oil boosts, jebaitor, the card played immediately before) explains the 2/30 rate; whatever gates it is invisible in every field this project currently reads. Consequence: this is NOT a bigger lever than `mineFishPatterns.ts` as session 25 hoped — at ~7%/cast it cannot replace the general prediction problem, only occasionally supplement it. No strategy code reacts to it yet, correctly — see QUESTIONS.md §12 for the full derivation and what would need to change this (more real sightings, now auto-surfaced live via the widened detector).
2026-08-18 (session 28, Codex review) — CORRECTION to the 2026-08-17 (session 27) "CHECKED AND NOT CONFIRMED" entry above and to STATE.md's session-27 "Fintuition hypothesis REJECTED" framing (STATE.md itself is superseded by this session's recap, not hand-edited — the corrected numbers below are what the session-28 STATE.md carries forward): session 27's own fishing-corpus count used the wrong unit (225 = total response documents across all three action types, not turns where `nextPosition` could fire) and its conclusion was consequently too strong. Direct recount by `docId` (new `src/sim/fishingCorpus.ts` loader, CODEXREVIEW #1): the corpus is 50 distinct casts / 225 response documents / **169 actual `play_cards` turns** / 7 catches (14%), not "30 casts / 225 turns" as session 26/27 assumed. The correct `nextPosition`-firing rate is **2/169 = 1.18%**, not 2/225 = 0.89%. More importantly, a binomial test against the stated 3% Fintuition base rate does NOT reject at n=169 (P(X≤2) ≈ 11.5% under that null) — 0.89% merely looked more clearly incompatible with 3% than the correct 1.18% does. **Corrected status: Fintuition as the `nextPosition` cause is UNCONFIRMED, not REJECTED** — the substance (real, rare, cause unknown, `activeFintuitionTurns`/`fintuitionOilBoostPercent` constant `0`/`null` across the entire corpus and uninformative either way) is unchanged from session 26/27, only the confidence of the negative claim changes: this project's data cannot yet confirm OR refute the hypothesis, not "argues against it." Separately, the same recount corrects two other session-27 STATE.md claims: focus point changed within **48/50 casts** (not 29/30), and real catch rate is **7/50 = 14%** (not 4/30 ≈ 13.3%) — both drawn from the same directory/file-count-as-cast/turn conflation, now fixed at the loader level so future audits can't repeat it. See QUESTIONS.md §12's session-28 addendum for the full derivation.
2026-08-18 (session 28) — `acquireGuardLock()` (`src/orchestrator/guardPersistence.ts`) holds an exclusive lockfile for the WHOLE PROCESS LIFETIME of `liveRun.ts`/`liveFishing.ts`/`orchestrator.ts`, not just around one `saveGuardBudget` write — chosen over a lock scoped to a single load→assert→increment→save transaction (CODEXREVIEW #2's other stated option) because those four steps are not contiguous in this codebase: `assertCanStartRun` runs in memory at the top of a run, `recordRunStarted`/`recordEnergySpent`+`saveGuardBudget` run much later, sometimes after a whole dungeon run's worth of network calls. A lock not held continuously across that gap wouldn't actually close the two-writer race CODEXREVIEW flagged. A lockfile left behind by a crashed process is reclaimed automatically if its named PID is no longer alive (`process.kill(pid, 0)`), so a hard crash doesn't require manual cleanup before the next invocation can run.
2026-08-18 (session 28) — `GigaverseClient.getDungeonState()` now retries a 5xx exactly ONCE before throwing, using the existing rate limiter's natural 1200ms+jitter gap rather than an added delay — a single retry was judged sufficient because the two known real 5xx causes (a run that just ended, returning an HTML 500; a transient server blip) both plausibly clear within one request's worth of time, and CODEXREVIEW's own suggested fix was "retry the read," not "retry N times." If a live capture ever shows a 5xx persisting across MORE than one retry for the legitimate "run just ended" case, this retry count should become configurable rather than assumed sufficient forever.
2026-08-18 (session 29) — QUESTIONS.md §13 RESOLVED: user-confirmed both dungeon and fishing daily caps reset at **11am Pacific** (`America/Los_Angeles`, DST-aware — not a fixed UTC offset), not UTC midnight as `guardPersistence.ts`'s original `todayKey()` assumed. `todayKey()` now derives the guard's calendar day from Pacific wall-clock time via `Intl.DateTimeFormat`, rolling to the next day at 11am local rather than midnight UTC — the actual root cause behind session 24's dungeon-run-count drift and session 27's wasted fishing `start_run` attempt (both showed a "fresh" local guard while the real server cap hadn't lifted). Applies to BOTH modes automatically since they share this one function. Verified DST-safe across the 2026-11-01 PDT→PST fall-back boundary (`tests/orchestrator/guardPersistence.test.ts`), not just the ordinary daily boundary.
2026-08-18 (session 29, CODEXREVIEW #6) — Dungeon scheduling now reconciles against the authoritative `GET /game/dungeon/today` count BEFORE attempting a genuinely new `start_run` (`scripts/liveRun.ts`'s new `assertDungeonCapNotExhausted`), not just printing drift after the fact as before — a confirmed server-exhausted cap blocks locally (no POST sent) and marks the local guard exhausted for the rest of the persisted day via new `GuardState.recordServerCapReached()`. Fishing has no equivalent authoritative pre-check endpoint, so it stays fail-closed on the real rejection as before — but a CONFIRMED "Player has reached max runs for fishing" rejection is now reclassified from a generic anomaly into the same budget-type trip (`isBudgetGuardTrip` already covers "session run cap reached"), so hitting fishing's real cap can no longer take the orchestrator's dungeon side down with it. This directly implements CODEXREVIEW #6 (dungeon reconciliation + fishing backstop), separate from and complementary to the 11am-Pacific date-key fix above — the date-key fix prevents most mismatches from occurring at all; this is the backstop for whatever it still misses.
2026-08-18 (session 29, CODEXREVIEW #5) — Resumed-fishing-cast transition numbering is fixed at both ends: `scripts/liveFishing.ts`'s `runOneCast` derives the next turn to log from `lastRecordForCast()` (new — the highest-turn record already on disk for that `castId`) instead of always restarting a resumed cast at turn 0, and validates the resumed doc's real position against the log's last entry before trusting it enough to append further — a mismatch disables logging for that cast this run rather than risking a second wrong write. `scripts/mineFishPatterns.ts`'s `groupByCast`/`testPrimitives` now REJECT casts with conflicting duplicate turn records or gapped trajectories from exact-match testing entirely (reported as `excluded`, with reasons), rather than the old behavior of silently skipping gaps mid-loop and still calling the result an exact full match. Verified against the real bug: cast `12923189` (two turn-0 records ~5 minutes apart, the exact scenario CODEXREVIEW #5 named) is now correctly excluded, and BOTH of session 28's promotions (`perimeterWalk(cw)` support=4, `perimeterWalk(ccw)` support=3) survive unchanged after the fix. See QUESTIONS.md §14 for an unrelated, still-open finding from the same re-run (castId `9001`/`9002`, currently under active unidentified concurrent write) that the fix's exclusion logic does NOT cover (it isn't gapped or duplicate-conflicting internally) and should not be conflated with the `12923189` bug this fix targets.
2026-08-18 (session 30) — QUESTIONS.md §14 RESOLVED, not an unknown live process: `tests/sim/fishingCorpus.test.ts` (session 28) called the real `runOneCast` with synthetic docIds `9001`/`9002` but never passed `transitionsPath`, which defaults to the real `data/fish-patterns.jsonl` — every test run appended a real record there, and session 29's own `lastRecordForCast` resume-fix made repeated test runs during a session increment the turn number, producing exactly the "active process" appearance. Fixed by isolating the test's `transitionsPath`; the 14 accumulated pollution records were removed from the real (gitignored) corpus file. `twoCellCycle(0,-1)` unchanged at support=1 after the cleanup — the pollution's zero-movement records never matched any primitive, so no mined pattern was ever actually affected, but the leak is closed going forward.
2026-08-18 (session 30) — `IS_JUICED_CID` on a dungeon response's `data.entity` is NOT the per-run "Juiced mode" flag — checked against the full corpus (47 attempts) and found `true` on every single one, including pre-session-08 captures that predate the Juiced RUN MODE's existence. It is the ACCOUNT-level purchased buff DECISIONS 2026-08-17 (session 23) already named (`isPlayerJuiced` — "more energy, more ROM output, 4x Hard Cores... nothing to do with a specific run"), just mirrored onto the entity object under a confusingly similar name. The real per-run flag is `WANTS_JUICED_MODE_CID`, confirmed stable within an attempt (no corpus attempt has mixed values) and varying across attempts (45 false / 2 true) — this is what `src/sim/dungeonReport.ts`'s run-visibility report uses. Caught before shipping the report, not after.
2026-08-18 (session 30) — Dungeon reward fields CONFIRMED against real captures, not `SPEC.md`'s `[VERIFY]` guesses: Hard Core (item 845) is credited via the top-level `gameItemBalanceChanges` array on a `"Reward chosen"` response (confirms the session-08 `gigusOrbItemId`/`gigusOrbAmount` hypothesis directly). The user's "Dendren Root" is wire item 846, `NAME_CID: "Dendren Remnant"` — credited on a `"Move Used"` response landing a kill. The naming mismatch (user's term vs. wire NAME_CID) is real and is documented rather than silently reconciled to one or the other — see `src/sim/dungeonReport.ts`'s header comment for the full capture (`fixtures/dungeon-runs/run-2026-08-15-15-38-09/state-{054,079,110}.json`).
2026-08-18 (session 30) — Fishing catch data (fish name/rarity) is read from a response's `data.events[]` `FISH_DIED` entry, NOT `doc.data.caughtFish` — checked against every committed live-catch fixture before writing `src/sim/fishingCorpus.ts`'s extraction and found `caughtFish` null in all of them despite `SPEC-fishing.md` documenting it as the catch shape. `FISH_DIED`'s `data.fish` carries the real object (`gameItemId`, `name`, `rarity`, ...) in every real catch fixture checked.
2026-08-18 (session 30) — `nextPosition`'s live behavior stays validation-only this session, not a live override, despite the user's directive to "act on it when it fires" — user-confirmed, but paired with the brief's own caveat (CODEXIMPROVE #6/CODEXREVIEW's binomial check: 2/169 real firings, statistically compatible with a 3% Fintuition rate, neither confirmed nor rejected) that asked for validation logging BEFORE a live override. `scripts/liveFishing.ts`'s `runOneCast` now logs predicted-vs-actual to `data/nextPositionValidation.jsonl` every time a prediction can be checked, and the override path (`certainDistribution`, forcing `chooseCard`'s distribution to the predicted cell) is wired but gated behind `NEXT_POSITION_OVERRIDE_THRESHOLD = 10` confirmed hits — at 2 hits total in this project's history, unreachable this session regardless of live play volume. Revisit the threshold once real validation data accumulates; it is a stated "a handful," not derived from a power calculation (the event is too rare for one yet).
2026-08-18 (session 31, CODEXREVIEW #8) — The daily-budget guard now enforces off COMMITTED spend, not the observed before/after account-energy delta: `guards.recordEnergySpent(config.energyCostPerRun)` (dungeon) / `dendren.energyCostPerCast` (fishing) is called the moment `start_run` succeeds, right alongside the existing `recordRunStarted()`, and persisted immediately via the same `saveGuardBudget` call. The before/after read stays in `main()`/`orchestrator.ts` but is now purely diagnostic — reconciled against the committed figure by the new `src/orchestrator/energyAccounting.ts` (`reconcileEnergyAccounting`/`describeEnergyAccounting`), never fed back into the guard. This closes the real gap CODEXREVIEW #8 named: an external top-up (a ROM claim landing mid-run) could previously mask real spend down to a smaller number or zero (the old delta was clamped at 0), letting persisted spend drift below what was truly committed.
2026-08-18 (session 31) — Found while implementing the above, not part of CODEXREVIEW #8 itself: `tests/sim/fishingCorpus.test.ts` (session 30's own `9001`/`9002` mystery-writer fix) and two tests in `tests/liveFishing.test.ts` (session 30's `nextPosition` validation tests) never set `guardStatePath` on a real, non-dry-run `runOneCast` call — every run of these tests silently fell back to `DEFAULT_GUARD_STATE_PATH` (`data/guard-budget.json`, the DUNGEON guard file, not fishing's) and overwrote real committed state. Confirmed live by diffing that file before/after running just these tests (`energySpent` 0 -> 12). This was already happening before this session for `runsStarted` (via the pre-existing `recordRunStarted()`+`saveGuardBudget` at the same call site) — this session's `recordEnergySpent` addition made the corruption cover `energySpent` too, which is what surfaced it. All three tests now point `guardStatePath` at their existing temp dir, same fix shape as session 30's `transitionsPath` leak in the identical file. The real `data/guard-budget.json` was restored to its pre-pollution content (`energySpent: 0, runsStarted: 1, date: 2026-08-17`) after confirming the fix stops further writes.
2026-08-18 (session 31, CODEXIMPROVE #2) — Equal-EV ties in fishing card/focus choice now conserve the scarce, non-regenerating focus-movement budget instead of resolving by grid enumeration or hand order. `bestFocusForCard` (`src/strategy/fishing/cardChoice.ts`) breaks an EV tie (within `EV_TIE_EPSILON = 1e-9`) in favor of the placement closer (Manhattan distance) to the current focus. `chooseCard`'s cross-card choice now goes through a shared `isPreferred` comparator applying CODEXIMPROVE #2's full order: partition lethal from non-lethal first (unchanged from session 15's "lethal check first"), then within each partition — higher EV (or EV/mana under the existing mana-constrained branch) / on a tie, lower focus movement cost / on a further tie, lower mana cost / otherwise first-seen hand order. Provably EV-neutral (only ties are affected, never a strict EV comparison), so no sim re-validation was run — the existing 500-run sim baseline stays valid.
2026-08-18 (session 31) — **CORRECTION to session 30's "fishing catch data comes from `data.events[]` `FISH_DIED`, NOT `doc.data.caughtFish`" claim** (both the DECISIONS 2026-08-18 session-30 entry above and `src/sim/fishingCorpus.ts`'s header comment). Checked directly against the fixtures while folding this into SPEC.md/SPEC-fishing.md per this session's brief (CLAUDE.md §9 applies to a prior session's own unverified claim exactly as it does to a chat brief's) — `doc.data.caughtFish` IS reliably populated on real catches: `fixtures/fishing-casts/live/cast-2026-08-17-00-03-25/state-023.json` and five other fixtures all show it as a full populated object matching `FISH_DIED`'s `data.fish` field-for-field (identical content, exactly as SPEC-fishing.md already documented from session 15). The actual pattern session 30 missed: `caughtFish` PERSISTS across every subsequent response once a catch lands (multiple turns, including the `loot`-resolution response — same persistence shape as `nextPosition`), while `FISH_DIED` fires exactly once, on the kill turn only — so `caughtFish` is present in MORE files than `FISH_DIED`, the opposite of "never populated." Root cause found in `handoff/log/session-30.md`'s own verification script: it read `d['data']['doc'].get('caughtFish')`, one `.data` level too shallow — the real path is `doc.data.caughtFish`, so the check always returned `None` regardless of the real value. `fishingCorpus.ts`'s choice to key off `FISH_DIED` still works correctly for cast-level catch counting (one `FISH_DIED` per catching cast is sufficient to flag that cast caught), so no code or corpus-summary numbers change — only the false justification comment is corrected, and SPEC.md is NOT amended with the wrong claim. SPEC-fishing.md's existing `caughtFish` documentation (session 15, `[RESOLVED]`) was correct all along and needed no fix.
2026-08-18 (session 31, CODEXREVIEW #10 — NOT implemented as stated) — Checked before acting, per CLAUDE.md §9: `viem` is NOT unused. `scripts/probe.ts`'s `authFromEoa()` (Path B EOA auth, gated behind `AUTH_MODE=eoa`) imports and calls `privateKeyToAccount` from `viem/accounts` at a live, reachable call site — real code, not a dead/commented-out remnant, even though Path A (browser JWT) is the path actually used per DECISIONS 2026-08-13. CODEXREVIEW #10's premise was stale by the time this session read it; `viem` stays in `package.json`.
2026-08-18 (session 31, session-30 open question 4) — Standalone `liveRun.ts`/`liveFishing.ts` invocations (Task 6/9) now regenerate `handoff/reports/*.md` at the end of `main()`, same as `orchestrator.ts`'s end-of-session rollup, rather than relying on a stated manual convention. The shared non-fatal implementation moved into new `scripts/regenerateReports.ts` (`regenerateRunReports(config)`), called from all three entry points — no behavior change, same real-fixture-corpus/real-committed-path regeneration session 30 already built, just no longer duplicated three ways. Smoke-tested directly (not unit-tested — the function touches real committed report paths by design, and this project's tests never touch real committed paths per the §1 `guardStatePath` fix this session): confirmed `regenerateRunReports` reproduces session 30's exact backfill numbers (47 dungeon attempts, 50 fishing casts) and that a standalone `liveFishing.ts --dry-run` invocation reaches the new call and regenerates cleanly end to end.
2026-08-18 (session 32, CODEXIMPROVE #1) — The dungeon opponent model now persists to `data/opponent-model.json` and bootstraps from clean (`reasons.length === 0`) historical corpus exchanges, instead of starting blank every launch. New `src/orchestrator/opponentModelPersistence.ts` reuses `guardPersistence.ts`'s schema-validation/atomic-save/`acquireGuardLock` patterns directly rather than reinventing them, adding only a `schemaVersion` field (zod `z.literal`, rejected on mismatch) that guard-budget's own schema doesn't have. Bootstrap identity is `${run}::${exchange label}` (a label alone isn't unique across runs, DECISIONS 2026-08-15), persisted in a `bootstrapImportedIds` set so calling bootstrap on every launch is safe and idempotent — already-imported exchanges are skipped, newly grown corpus is picked up automatically. `OpponentModel` itself stays pure; only its internal `Counts` type gained an `export` keyword (type-only, no I/O added). Live-verified: a real `--dry-run` bootstrapped 64 clean room-1 exchanges (enemy "Enemy Room 63") on first launch, wrote the file correctly, and a second launch imported 0 new (idempotent, confirmed live not just unit-tested).
2026-08-18 (session 32) — All 64 of the corpus's clean, catalogued-enemy exchanges resolve to exactly one `(enemyId, room)` key: `"Enemy Room 63|room1"`. Rooms 2+ mostly carry at least one unmodelled mechanic per capture (rolled stats being the most common), so the opponent-model bootstrap's current live-usable value is concentrated entirely at room 1 — the deeper-room, sparser-evidence benefit CODEXIMPROVE #1's own rationale named won't show up in practice until the corpus has more clean exchanges at rooms 2+, which needs more capture, not more code. Not a blocker, not re-opening any gate — noted so a future session doesn't assume the mechanism's full value is already realized just because it shipped.
2026-08-18 (session 32, CODEXIMPROVE queue §2) — The isolated-test-path rule (tests must never write to a real `data/`/`logs/` path) is now written explicitly into CLAUDE.md's working-style section, rather than left as unstated convention — session 30 (fishing-corpus `9001`/`9002` pollution) and session 31 (`guard-budget.json` leak) both shipped this same bug class by accident, twice in a row. A dedicated grep audit of every `LiveRunDeps`/`LiveFishingDeps` test construction beyond the 3 already-fixed session-31 offenders found none missing isolation — `tests/liveRun.test.ts` and `tests/liveFishing.test.ts` both route every construction through a single per-file `makeDeps()` helper that always sets `guardStatePath`, which is a structural guard against a 4th instance, not just a clean grep result. No test opts into the new `opponentModelPersistence` dep from CODEXIMPROVE #1, so that addition introduced no new pollution surface either.
2026-08-18 (session 40) — `tests/liveFishing.test.ts` now constructs `LiveFishingDeps` through exactly ONE helper, `makeLiveFishingDeps()`, whose parameter type requires `transitionsPath`/`guardStatePath`/`nextPositionLogPath`/`logsDir` explicitly (`Required<Pick<...>>`) — the prior five independent inline literals plus one narrower wrapper were exactly how session 39's `data/nextPositionValidation.jsonl` contamination happened (one site omitted two of the four). Dropping any of the four at the call site is now a compile error, not a silent fallback to a real path.
2026-08-18 (session 40) — `tests/liveRun.test.ts`'s existing single `makeDeps()` helper (session 32) is reconfirmed live as the ONLY `LiveRunDeps` construction site — zero inline literals bypass it. Its return type now requires `guardStatePath` (`Required<Pick<LiveRunDeps, "guardStatePath">>`) since that's the one field that falls back to a real path when omitted (`DEFAULT_GUARD_STATE_PATH`). `opponentModelPersistence`/`playCountsPersistence` are deliberately NOT required the same way — both are no-op-safe when undefined (every use site in `runOnce` is gated `if (deps.X && ...)`), so mechanically requiring them would misstate their risk rather than close a real gap.
2026-08-18 (session 33, CODEXIMPROVE #3) — The fishing fallback is now conditioned on the fish's previous movement displacement before falling back to cell-only, then uniform (`src/strategy/fishing/contextualFallback.ts`'s `contextualFallback()`, gated on `DEFAULT_MIN_INDEPENDENT_CASTS = 3` distinct real casts supporting the exact `${cell}|${dx},${dy}` key). Offline leave-one-cast-out CV against the real corpus (49 clean casts / 165 hops — exactly matching the brief's stated corpus size) reproduced the core finding: cell-only 16.4% top-1 (EXACT match to Codex's number), previous-direction 37.1% vs Codex's 33.9% (both ~2.3x cell-only), turn number adding nothing in both runs. `minIndependentCasts=3` was chosen over 2 specifically because threshold 2's log loss (6.264) regresses versus the cell-only baseline (5.860) despite a bigger top-1/Brier win — a sign of confident-wrong predictions at thin support (2 independent casts can concentrate probability on 1-2 cells). A simulator ablation (matcher-blind, training corpus mined from the sim's own true pattern pool) showed the algorithm correctly exploits genuine structure when present: uniform 7.9% → cell-only 33.8% → contextual 72.5% (N=2000) — framed as an algorithm-correctness check per the sim-authority-per-domain rule (2026-08-15), not a live catch-rate claim.
2026-08-18 (session 33) — Top-1 accuracy evaluation on the fishing corpus requires a DETERMINISTIC tie-break (lowest x, then lowest y, among cells tied for max probability) to match Codex's reported numbers — insertion-order or "any tied cell counts as correct" tie-breaks produced 23.0%/29.1% respectively for the cell-only predictor against Codex's reported 16.4%; the (x,y)-sorted rule reproduces 16.4% exactly. Scoped to `scripts/fishingContextualCV.ts`'s evaluation only — the shipped `contextualFallback()`/`emptyFallback()` never picks a single top-1 cell, only a full distribution that `chooseCard` consumes whole, so no tie-break exists in production code.
2026-08-18 (session 33, live) — A live `start_run` HTTP 400 this session (before any new code ran) coincided with the account carrying a completed-but-unresolved doc with `SUCCESS_CID: false` (an ESCAPE) rather than the previously-documented catch-specific stuck shape (DECISIONS 2026-08-16 session 15, QUESTIONS §10) — no `cardsToAdd`, `cardChosenId: -1` (not the documented `null`). NOT confirmed as the cause of the 400, and NOT resolved (no confirmed action shape exists for an escape-stuck doc, so none was guessed). See QUESTIONS.md §15.
2026-08-18 (session 34, CODEXIMPROVE #4 stages 1-2) — `decide()`'s top-level move choice now breaks a genuine `.score` tie (within `1e-9`) by preferring the candidate whose ATK-weighted charge reserve is higher, not play-share — play-share (`playCounts`) lives in the stateful `strategyPolicy` adapter and threading it into `decide()` would break the pure-function contract DECISIONS 2026-08-16 already records for this module, while ATK is already on `state.me.moves` and needed nothing new threaded in. Our own post-exchange charges depend only on which move WE play (`combat.ts`'s `applyCharges` ignores the enemy's reply), so the reserve for a candidate move is exact, not an expectation. Provably non-regressive: it only resolves cases already tied within epsilon on the primary score, verified by a synthetic test where a real score gap survives a 1000x-inflated charge reserve on the losing move untouched.
2026-08-18 (session 34, CODEXIMPROVE #4 stage 3) — `utility()` gains a small continuation term, `cfg.chargeReserveWeight * chargeReserveFraction(me)` (ATK-weighted charge reserve normalised to [0,1]), and it ships NON-ZERO at `0.4` — unlike the session-06 HP/armor weight sweep's documented null result, this axis showed a real, reproducible effect: `scripts/chargeReserveAblation.ts` at N=20000/weight over two seeds found weights 0.2/0.4/0.8 all separating above the zero control on mean rooms cleared (95% CI), and a follow-up N=60000/weight sweep found an inverted-U (0.8 drops back toward 0.3's level in both seeds — evidence of real structure, not the term just inflating every state's score) with 0.4/0.5/0.6 forming a mutually-indistinguishable plateau. 0.4 was picked as the plateau's low-risk edge, not because it beats 0.5/0.6 (it doesn't, at this N) — smallest weight reaching the flat top, least likely to crowd out the HP/armor terms it composes beside as those get retuned later.
2026-08-18 (session 35, CODEXIMPROVE #5) — `loot.ts`'s `"pool"`/`"upgrade"` boon scores are now scaled by the offer's real `val1`/`val2`, normalised against the ONE confirmed real sample of each shape (`AddMaxArmor` +4, `UpgradeRock`/`UpgradeScissor` +4 DEF) rather than a flat per-category constant — a same-category boon with a bigger delta now scores higher, not identically. `AddMaxHealth` is split out of the `AddMaxArmor`-sharing `"pool"` case (still categorised `"pool"`, only the score branches) and scored with the same `usableHealScore()` formula `"heal"` uses, but with `usable = val1` UNCAPPED — `boons.ts`'s `maxHealth` effect moves current HP WITH the new ceiling, so (unlike a capped `Heal`) nothing is ever wasted against it, and copying `Heal`'s literal `min(val1, hpMax-hp)` cap would have wrongly scored it 0 at full HP.
2026-08-18 (session 35, CODEXIMPROVE #5) — `playCounts` (the move-distribution `loot.ts`'s `"upgrade"` case ranks against) now persists across a process restart, keyed by `DUNGEON_ID_CID` (new `src/orchestrator/playCountsPersistence.ts`, same schema-versioned/atomic-write/`acquireGuardLock`-reused pattern as `guardPersistence.ts`/`opponentModelPersistence.ts`) — loaded once per run in `scripts/liveRun.ts`'s `runOnce()`, saved after every increment, deleted at every run-end path (win, death, or flee all land on `phase === "over"` or the null-state return). A load against a persisted `runId` that doesn't match the current run's `DUNGEON_ID_CID` returns zero (a different attempt's stale leftover, not corruption) — same "prior day is a fresh budget, not an error" convention `guardPersistence.ts`'s `loadGuardBudget` already established for date keys.
2026-08-18 (session 35) — `scripts/boonRankingCheck.ts`'s controlled rollout comparison (applying a boon via `applyBoon()` then a short `simulate()` continuation) found that `AddMaxHealth` vs `AddMaxArmor` at the same `val1` is a genuine near-tie at 50% player HP (both the ranking score, 49.05 vs 50.00, and the rollout's mean-rooms-cleared, well inside each other's 95% CI) — NOT a bug: armor is renewable through every future battle's own regen, HP is a one-time bank, so which is "better" at middling HP is a real, close strategic tradeoff that the current ranking doesn't (and isn't required to) resolve decisively. At 30% HP ("already going badly," matching the existing `PROBE_HP_FRACTION` threshold) the two separate cleanly in `AddMaxHealth`'s favor with non-overlapping CIs.
2026-08-18 (session 36) — **CORRECTION to session 35's STATE.md claim that "both Codex docs' standing backlog is now fully closed."** An independent read-only audit (`CODEXAUDIT`, run against commit `85c8093` with 532/532 tests / `tsc` / `git diff --check` independently re-verified passing, same validation discipline this project already applies to itself) found that claim did not fully hold, and this session re-checked its two most consequential findings directly against the live tree before trusting them (CLAUDE.md §9's discipline applied to an outside audit doc exactly as it applies to a chat brief). Confirmed real: **CODEXREVIEW #2 is only partial** — atomic temp-file+rename is in place in `guardPersistence.ts`/`opponentModelPersistence.ts`/`playCountsPersistence.ts`, but none of the three ever `fsync`s before rename, so CODEXREVIEW #2's explicit "write sibling temp file, **flush it**, then atomically rename it" was never fully implemented — a power/filesystem crash (not just a process crash) can still lose a supposedly-committed write. **CODEXIMPROVE #1 had a real live-observe double-count regression** — `scripts/liveRun.ts`'s combat-POST call site called `model.observe()` and saved the model on every live exchange, but never added that exchange's identity to `bootstrapImportedIds` before saving, so the NEXT restart's `bootstrapFromCorpus()` found the fixture the live session itself had just written, saw its id absent from the persisted ledger, and re-imported (double-counted) it — every live observation this feature was built to persist was silently doubled on the next restart. **CODEXIMPROVE #3's shipped threshold has a real, previously un-flagged calibration cost**: leave-one-cast-out log loss got WORSE at `minIndependentCasts=3` (6.151) than the cell-only baseline (5.860), because the tier hard-switches to a raw empirical distribution assigning exactly zero probability to any cell outside a thin 3-cast sample, and `chooseCard()` consumes the whole distribution, not just top-1 — queued as CODEXAUDIT #2, not attempted this session. **CODEXIMPROVE #5's orchestrator half was genuinely missing**: `scripts/orchestrator.ts` — the primary UNATTENDED long-running entry point, not `liveRun.ts` — never wired `playCountsPersistence` into its `runOnce()` call, so an orchestrator session interrupted mid-run and resumed forgot every move played before the restart, exactly the resume gap CODEXIMPROVE #5 was written to close, left open in the entry point that matters most for it. **CODEXIMPROVE #6 (`nextPosition` override gate) counts only cumulative hits** (`confirmedHitCount()`), not hits-out-of-attempts, so ten hits and ninety interleaved misses would still satisfy the threshold and flip on a one-hot override actually right only 10% of the time; the loader also skips schema/grid-bounds validation — queued as CODEXAUDIT #4, not attempted this session. This session fixed CODEXIMPROVE #1 in full (see the entry directly below) and closed CODEXIMPROVE #5's remaining orchestrator gap as a stretch item; CODEXREVIEW #2's fsync gap, CODEXIMPROVE #3's calibration, and CODEXIMPROVE #6's gate stay queued (CODEXAUDIT #5/#2/#4 respectively), not attempted this session, deliberately — see CODEXAUDIT itself (repo root, untracked) for the full independent write-up. **Process lesson, stated once rather than left implicit**: the last several sessions' GATE PASS self-assessments checked the code paths each session's own brief and tests happened to exercise, not every real entry point a fix was supposed to cover — that is how #5's orchestrator half and #1's live-observe path both went unchecked while their sibling paths looked done. Before this project declares a Codex item fully resolved going forward, grep every real entry point that's supposed to use the changed code, not just the one path the session's own smoke test touched.
2026-08-18 (session 36, CODEXAUDIT #1 fix) — The opponent-model live-observe double-count bug (corrected above) is fixed by giving the live-observe call site the SAME per-exchange identity `bootstrapFromCorpus()` already computes for a corpus exchange, instead of inventing a second scheme: new `exchangeLabel`/`exchangeIdentity` helpers in `src/sim/corpus.ts` (the sole wire-shape-owning module, DECISIONS 2026-08-15) are now the ONE place `${run}::${beforeFile}→${afterFile}` gets computed, reused by both `opponentModelPersistence.ts`'s `collectBootstrapObservations` and `scripts/liveRun.ts`'s combat-POST call site. `FixtureWriter.write()` now returns the exact file name it just wrote (previously `void`) and gained a `runName` getter, so the live call site can build the identity from its own two most recent writes (the GET-state "before" and the POST-response "after", always consecutive within one loop iteration — a probe/potion detour always `continue`s back to a fresh GET before either is used) and mark it into `bootstrapImportedIds` — kept as that name rather than renamed (e.g. `observedExchangeIds`) to avoid a schema-version bump for what both a corpus-import and a live-observe already treat as the same question ("already folded into the model"), not two different ones. Since `scripts/orchestrator.ts` calls the SAME shared `runOnce()` (not a separate implementation) and already threaded `opponentModelPersistence` through, this fix covers both real entry points without a second code change. Verified: a NEW `runOnce()`-level regression test (`tests/liveRun.test.ts`, real `FixtureWriter` against a temp dir, real `loadOpponentModel`/`bootstrapFromCorpus`/`loadCorpus`, not mocks) drives one live combat exchange, confirms it's marked into the persisted ledger, simulates a restart's bootstrap pass against the SAME fixture root and confirms the observation count is unchanged (not doubled) while a genuinely new canary corpus exchange in a separate run directory still imports normally — and was confirmed to FAIL against the pre-fix code (reverted temporarily to check) before being confirmed passing against the fix. 533/533 tests passing (532 + 1 new), `tsc --noEmit` clean, checked at this session's final commit.
2026-08-18 (session 36, CODEXAUDIT #3, stretch) — `scripts/orchestrator.ts` now takes the play-counts guard lock and threads `playCountsPersistence` into its `runOnce()` call, mirroring `scripts/liveRun.ts`'s `main()` exactly (same `DEFAULT_PLAY_COUNTS_PATH`, same `acquireGuardLock` pattern) — closes CODEXIMPROVE #5's actual remaining gap (corrected above), so an orchestrator session interrupted mid-run and resumed no longer forgets its move distribution before `loot.ts`'s `"upgrade"` case needs it. No dedicated orchestrator-level regression test was added — `scripts/orchestrator.ts`'s `main()` isn't exported/structured for unit testing today (its own header comment already documents this: the scheduler/shutdown logic are unit-tested, `main()` itself is smoke-tested via `--dry-run`/`--hours=`), and building that harness was judged bigger than this stretch item's stated scope. The wiring itself routes through the SAME already-tested `runOnce()` and the same `playCountsPersistence` shape `liveRun.ts` already exercises in `tests/liveRun.test.ts`, so the risk is low but genuinely unverified by an orchestrator-specific test — flagged honestly rather than claimed as covered.
2026-08-18 (session 37, CODEXAUDIT #5) — **CODEXREVIEW #2 is now finished** (the fsync half that session 36's audit confirmed was still missing). New `src/orchestrator/atomicWrite.ts` exports one shared `atomicWriteJson(path, body)`: builds the same `${path}.tmp-${pid}-${Date.now()}-${rand}` sibling temp name every module already used, opens it with `openSync`/`writeSync` (not `writeFileSync`, which never exposes a file descriptor), `fsyncSync`s that descriptor, `closeSync`s it, then `renameSync`s it into place — and best-effort `fsyncSync`s the parent directory afterward, wrapped in its own try/catch since directory-fsync isn't supported on every platform/filesystem. On any failure before the rename completes, the temp file is removed (`rmSync({force:true})`) before rethrowing. `guardPersistence.ts`'s `saveGuardBudget`, `opponentModelPersistence.ts`'s `saveOpponentModelAtomically`, and `playCountsPersistence.ts`'s `savePlayCounts` all now call this one helper instead of each carrying its own copy of the write block — mechanical extraction only, none of their body-construction logic (`schemaVersion`, the `bootstrapImportedIds` sort, etc.) changed. **What this proves and what it doesn't, stated explicitly per the brief's own instruction**: a unit test cannot prove a write survives a real power loss — that remains a filesystem/OS-level guarantee this project is trusting, not one it can independently verify. What the new `tests/orchestrator/atomicWrite.test.ts` (8 tests) DOES prove: `fsyncSync` is actually invoked during a real save (a spy confirms the call happens, closing the literal gap CODEXREVIEW #2 named — the old code never even had a file descriptor to fsync); a failed rename or a failed fsync both clean up the temp file and rethrow rather than leaving a `.tmp-*` orphan; a failed *directory* fsync does NOT take down an otherwise-good write (best-effort, per spec); and the existing round-trip/corruption/atomic-write tests for all three original modules pass completely unchanged after the refactor (no behavioral regression). `vi.spyOn` cannot target Node's built-in ESM module exports directly ("module namespace is not configurable") — the test file uses `vi.mock("node:fs", { spy: true })` instead, which auto-spies every export while calling through to the real implementation by default.
2026-08-18 (session 37, CODEXAUDIT #6, stretch) — Opponent-model persistence schema tightened: `opponentModelPersistence.ts`'s per-move counts are now `z.number().int().nonnegative()` (was bare `z.number()`, so a persisted file could previously carry a negative or fractional count and load as if it were legitimate data), and `CountsSchema` gained a `.refine()` rejecting any key where a transition row's sum exceeds its marginal predecessor count — a transition FROM move X can only be recorded (`OpponentModel.observe()`'s `c.transitions[prev][move]++`) on a turn where X was ALSO the move actually played (`c.total[move]++`), so `sum(transitions[X]) > total[X]` is structurally impossible under the model's own accounting, not an unusual-but-valid count. Both fail closed with `OpponentModelPersistenceError`, same discipline as the existing schemaVersion/shape checks. 4 new tests in `tests/orchestrator/opponentModelPersistence.test.ts` (negative count, fractional count, a violating transition row, and the exact-equality boundary case confirmed to NOT throw) plus all 12 pre-existing tests in that file pass unchanged. 545/545 tests total (533 + 8 atomicWrite + 4 schema), `tsc --noEmit` clean, `git diff --check` clean, checked at this session's final commit. No real `data/*.json` path touched by any new or existing test (grep-confirmed: only `atomicWrite.ts` itself contains the `.tmp-${pid}` write pattern anywhere in `src/`/`scripts/` now, all three call sites route through it).
2026-08-18 (session 38, CODEXAUDIT #2) — The fishing contextual fallback's hard `minIndependentCasts` threshold is RETIRED, fully replaced (not layered) by continuous shrinkage: `contextualFallback()` now mixes the context and cell-only distributions by `weight = n / (n + shrinkageK)` via a new `mixDistributions()` helper in `matcher.ts`, rather than hard-switching to the raw context distribution once `n` clears a count. `DEFAULT_SHRINKAGE_K = 1` — picked from a leave-one-cast-out sweep over shrinkageK in {0.25..1000} against the real corpus (49 clean casts/165 hops): logLoss 5.700 and Brier 0.852 both beat the cell-only baseline (5.860/0.932) that the old threshold (minIndependentCasts=3) had regressed against (6.151/0.927, per session 36's audit finding). The whole plateau shrinkageK∈[0.4,3] clears the gate; 1 sits at the logLoss minimum and is the standard add-one/Laplace value, not a fitted knife's-edge pick. `DEFAULT_MIN_INDEPENDENT_CASTS` no longer exists anywhere in the tree.
2026-08-18 (session 39, CODEXAUDIT #4) — The `nextPosition` override gate (`scripts/liveFishing.ts`) is REPLACED, not patched: `nextPositionOverrideStats()` requires BOTH `NEXT_POSITION_OVERRIDE_MIN_ATTEMPTS` (10) total attempts (not just hits — the audit's core finding, that 10 hits buried in 90 interleaved misses satisfied the old raw-count threshold identically to 10/10) AND a 95%-confidence Wilson-score lower bound on hit rate clearing `NEXT_POSITION_OVERRIDE_MIN_LOWER_BOUND` (0.5), chosen as a round, legible bar deliberately far above a 4x4 grid's ~6.25% blind-guess rate rather than a formal power calculation — same "a handful, not derived" honesty the retired threshold's own comment carried. Wilson (not the normal-approximation interval `dungeonSim.ts`'s `roomStats` uses elsewhere) was chosen specifically because it stays well-behaved at small n / p near 1, where a Wald interval degenerates to zero width. `loadNextPositionValidations()` now schema-validates every line via zod instead of a bare type assertion; a malformed-but-JSON-valid record (wrong type, out-of-`[1,gridSize]` coordinate) is skipped, not trusted. `NextPositionValidation` gained a per-record `gridSize` field (matching `TransitionRecord`'s existing convention, since gridSize is read live per doc and never assumed constant). `extractNextPosition()` also bounds-checks against the doc's own live `gridSize` when present, as defense in depth.

(2) Heal is no longer taken merely because HP is below max — it now requires `hpCurrent < hpMax` AND wasted overflow (`healAmount` minus the actual deficit) ≤15% of the heal's value; a Heal that fails this gate scores 0 and falls through to the next-ranked boon (Sword upgrade, then max HP/armor, then rare-move ATK). This SUPERSEDES the old pure continuous-urgency Heal scoring as a standalone sufficient rule (2026-08-16, session 10) — that scoring is now necessary but not sufficient, gated by the overflow check on top. Both implemented as large flat tier-separation bonuses (`HEAL_TAKEN_BONUS` > `SWORD_PIN_BONUS`), not bigger multipliers, so neither can be outscored by a large-magnitude offer in another category — same "hard rule, not a scored preference" shape as CLAUDE.md §8's tier-selection rule.

Two ((b) deliberate non-scoring play/redraw to let the fish drift closer, (c) an oil-reserve floor) are judgment calls documented as decision points rather than forced into a scoring formula, per the brief's own framing — (c) additionally blocked from going live by an unconfirmed oil-use action shape (QUESTIONS.md §16). None of the four implemented functions are corpus-validated yet — stated explicitly in SPEC-fishing.md §8, not left implicit.

2026-08-18 (session 44, brief §2-§3, live) — Today's live batch: 16 completed casts (15 new `start_run`s + 1 resumed pre-existing cast, user-confirmed OK to take over), 0 caught. All-time figure moved 14.0%(7/50) → 10.4%(7/67). This was NOT ordinary variance at the sim's own predicted 22-24% rate (under 2% chance) — a real, dominant cause was found and confirmed in BOTH live and sim domains: `chooseCard`/`bestFocusForCard` chronically burn the entire 3-point, non-regenerating focus budget within the first 2-4 turns of every cast (16/16 live this session, and 43% of N=300 simulated casts by a median of turn 2 when directly instrumented), then play the rest of the cast — often 5-9+ more turns — from a frozen focus position, producing the long miss-streaks that pushed every cast back to escape. Diagnosed by the USER first, off their own reading of the account's real mid-cast state (7/10 mana, 0/3 focus), not found by this session's own analysis first. Root cause: the current EV formula is purely single-turn-greedy with no cost on depleting a scarce multi-turn resource early — the existing focus-movement tie-break (session 31, CODEXIMPROVE #2) only conserves budget on an exact EV tie, never when there's any positive edge to moving. See SPEC-fishing.md §4c for the full writeup. Explicitly NOT fixed this session — asked the user directly (document-only vs. design-and-validate-now), user chose document-only, consistent with CLAUDE.md §4's "simulate first" rule applying even under time pressure. Proposed fix shape: a focus-reserve continuation term in `bestFocusForCard`'s scoring, mirroring the dungeon side's `chargeReserveWeight` precedent (2026-08-18 session 34 entry below) — sim-ablate before any live wiring. Scoped as Task 11's revival condition and next session's top priority (TASKS.md).

2026-08-18 (session 45, brief §0) — **The session-45 brief's Facts 1-3 about fish movement are CONFIRMED against the corpus, with one correction to how the exceptionless claim must be stated.** `scripts/auditStepClass.ts` (new, re-runnable as the corpus grows) re-derives all three independently. FACT 1 (every move lands on a fixed Manhattan-`k` ring, `k` constant per cast) is exceptionless on the `isCleanCast`-filtered corpus — 0 off-ring moves in 279 transitions, 66/66 multi-move casts constant-`k` — but NOT on the raw log the brief quoted: the raw log's one zero-length "move", two off-ring moves and one `k`-inconsistent cast all come from a single cast, `12923189`, whose turn 0 carries two disagreeing records (the session-29 CODEXREVIEW #5 duplicate-turn artifact, a logging failure, not fish behavior). Every trajectory analysis in this project already excludes it. Any future restatement of FACT 1 must carry the exclusion, or the fact reads as having counterexamples when it does not. FACT 2 reproduces (k=1 P(reversal) 0.0%, 0/109; k=2 39.2%, 40/102 — the brief's 41.7% was at n=84 pre-batch). FACT 3 reproduces exactly, including the template id lists and the 100.0%/71.3% focus-co-location hit rates; the brief's prose generalisation "at Chebyshev distance 2 a k=1 cast is a guaranteed miss" is too broad — it holds for the DIAGONAL-2 offsets (±2,±2), where the measured rate is 0.0%, but (2,0) is also Chebyshev 2 and scores 41.1%. See SPEC-fishing.md §9.
2026-08-18 (session 45, §1) — `src/strategy/fishing/stepClass.ts` (new) makes the step class a HARD CONSTRAINT on prediction: once `k` is known, every off-ring cell gets probability exactly zero. **§1's gate MET** — leave-one-cast-out on the real corpus (68 clean casts, 211 scored transitions, `scripts/fishingRingCV.ts` new): ring+conditional top-1 46.4% / logLoss 1.118 vs. the cell+prev baseline's 42.7% / 3.536, and zero zero-probability events vs. the baseline's 23 (structural, from the ring floor, not luck). **The brief's claimed baseline of logLoss 2.070 does NOT reproduce** — measured it is 3.536, because 23 held-out cells get exactly zero probability and take this project's standing `-log(1e-9)` floor; the ring model's own result came in slightly BETTER than the brief's 1.123/47.4%. Both divergences favour the model so the gate's direction was never in doubt, but the brief's baseline number is wrong and must not be carried forward. Defaults `{shrinkageK: 3, ringFloor: 0.1}` from the sweep's plateau INTERIOR (logLoss 1.065-1.09 across shrinkageK 2-5 × ringFloor 0.02-0.2; the global minimum is 0.003 better, noise at this n) — same robustness reasoning that picked `DEFAULT_SHRINKAGE_K = 1` in session 38. Top-1 is ~unchanged across the entire sweep: the ring CONSTRAINT buys top-1, the smoothing knobs only move calibration. Classes are NOT symmetric and the intuition is backwards from the obvious one: `k=1` is the EASIER class (top-1 54.1%, 4 ring cells and it never reverses), `k=2` harder (38.2%, up to 8 ring cells) — a live batch that draws one class must be scored against that class's row, never the mixed one.
2026-08-18 (session 45, §2) — `src/sim/fishing/empiricalFish.ts` (new) samples the sim's ground-truth fish from the real corpus's ring/conditional statistics instead of `patterns.ts`'s synthetic primitive pool; `castSim.ts` gains opt-in `empiricalFish` and `ringModel` options (both additive — omit them and the sim is byte-for-byte unchanged) and `CastSummary` gains outcome counts + `meanFinalFishHp`. **Session 44's heuristic-(d) verdict is CORRECTED to NEUTRAL**: 24.8%/24.6% (off) vs 24.6%/24.6% (on) against the empirical fish at N=20000, two seeds. Session 44's trace to `patterns.ts`'s `bounceDelta` was right and its conclusion backwards — the real corpus has 0 reversals in 109 k=1 observations, so `bounceDelta` models a fish this game does not have, and the SIM was wrong rather than the heuristic. Confirmed mechanically too: a k=2-only fish gives BYTE-IDENTICAL results with (d) on and off, because its guard tests `|prev.dx|+|prev.dy| === 1` (the displacement's length, not the step class) and so never fires for k=2 — it silently no-ops on exactly the class where reversal is the most likely move. No code change; (d) is left as a harmless no-op so that retiring it can be a deliberate change rather than a side effect.
2026-08-18 (session 45, self-correction, found mid-session) — **A sim arm labelled "today's live config" was not one, and it changed two conclusions.** `scripts/liveFishing.ts` wires mined matcher → `contextualFallback` over the real corpus maps; the first version of both new ablation scripts passed `matcherPool: minedPool` with no `blindFallback`, so the sim fell through to hardcoded UNIFORM. A uniform distribution makes every focus placement EV-identical, the tie-break therefore never moves the focus point, and the budget is never spent — the exact opposite of live behavior. Consequences, both corrected in place: (1) the live-config baseline is ~24.8%, not 5.5%, which WITHDRAWS the calibration claim that the empirical sim tracks live — it over-predicts live (10.1% all-time) by roughly 2.4x, and is additionally in-sample twice over (its predictor and its fish are both fitted to the same corpus), so the leave-one-cast-out table remains the only out-of-sample evidence this session produced; (2) heuristic (d) measured +1.7pp on the mis-specified arm and is neutral on the faithful one. The faithful arm also reproduces the live defect it was supposed to model — 79.5% of casts exhaust the focus budget by median turn 4, matching session 44's live 16/16 at turns 1-4, where the mis-specified arm showed 1.6%. Worth remembering as a failure mode in its own right: a sim arm is only as good as the care taken to make it match the live call path, and "it's the same policy" is not enough when the tiers below it differ.
2026-08-18 (session 45, §3) — `cardChoice.ts` gains `focusReserveFraction` and a `focusReserveWeight` parameter on `bestFocusForCard`/`chooseCard`, fixing SPEC-fishing.md §4c's focus-budget exhaustion: `score = ev + w * max(0, remaining − manhattan(current, focus)) / FOCUS_METER_MAX` becomes the primary sort key, with raw `ev` kept and still used by `isLethal`, `isManaConstrained` and all reporting (the reserve term prices a FUTURE option; letting it into a lethality test would be a category error). Parameter defaults to 0 so every pre-session-45 caller is byte-for-byte unchanged; `DEFAULT_FOCUS_RESERVE_WEIGHT = 3` is what `liveFishing.ts` passes, picked from a two-seed N=12000 sweep against the empirical fish showing the same inverted-U-with-plateau the dungeon's `chargeReserveWeight` found (peak 3 on both seeds, collapse past 8, and 3 sits inside the real deck's `hitEffect` range of 3-6, which is the intended sanity check). **The lift is +1.6pp, not the ~+5pp the brief projected** — stated as measured. `FOCUS_METER_MAX` moved `castSim.ts` → `geometry.ts` (already the shared strategy/sim dependency and already the home of the spend rule's docs) so strategy does not import from the simulator; `castSim.ts` re-exports it, so no import site changed.

2026-08-18 (session 45, live-found defect, fixed) — The two turn-0 rows in the live batch were scored by the matcher tier and BOTH assigned probability exactly 0 to the cell the fish reached — one from a fully-converged mined candidate predicting a single cell with p=1. The ring model's hard-zero treatment is therefore unguarded and it failed live in exactly the predicted way (that cast: logLoss 11.316, 3 zero-probability events, vs a corpus LOO of 0.803 for k=1). NOT fixed this session — one cast in 73 cannot choose between the candidate fixes. 2026-08-19 (session 49, brief §1) — **The live/offline movement gap was NEVER a gap — it was three compounding comparison errors, and there is no bug.** `scripts/liveGapDiagnostic.ts` runs the brief's four diagnostics: live top-1 recomputed by hand is 13.8% with 0 disagreements against the logged `hit` field (no aggregation bug); logged `predicted` sits on its own declared k-ring 24/24 (one coordinate convention end to end, so no second orientation mismatch); 1 of 5 casts alternated; and — decisively — an offline leave-one-cast-out re-derivation of the EXACT same 29 turns reproduces the shipped 13.8% to the row, so the distribution the policy scored IS the model's distribution and it is not a wiring bug. The three errors: (1) five turn-0 rows pooled into a figure the offline LOO never scores, since it skips hops with no previous displacement — 13.8% pooled vs 16.7% on the comparable set; (2) a k=2-heavy batch read against a class-MIXED average, where the strata are k=1 53.6% / k=2 33.9% / alternating 0/5 and the batch drew 16 of its 24 scored turns from k=2 casts; (3) one alternating cast contributing 5 turns whose only corpus peer scores 0/5. Composition-matched expectation 30.0% against an observed 16.7% [6.7%, 35.9%] — INSIDE the interval, nothing left to explain. `scripts/fishingRingCV.ts`'s OWN session-45 header already warned that reading a k=2-heavy live batch against the class-mixed figure "would flatter or damn the model for the wrong reason"; the warning was in the repo and was not applied to session 48's readout. Corrections to the session-49 brief (CLAUDE.md §9 — Claude-chat has no fixture access and could not have computed its §1 table): "live lands on the union-of-rings null to within 0.1pp" is FALSE (on those turns the union null is 10.3% and the ring model BEATS it), the k-ring null is 20.7% not 29.3%, and the offline LOO is 42.6% at 73 casts not the 46.4% quoted (that was the 68-cast number).
2026-08-19 (session 49, brief §2) — **The ring is no longer a HARD constraint; `stickyStepDistribution` replaces it and is shipped as the default** in `scripts/liveFishing.ts`, `src/sim/fishing/castSim.ts` and `src/sim/fishing/offPolicyReplay.ts`. The per-turn step count is OBSERVED the moment a hop resolves, so the only unknown is the NEXT turn's count and a two-state Markov chain on the last observed one is its whole sufficient statistic: `P(next cell) = (1-s)*P(cell | lastK) + s*P(cell | other count)`. `lastStepClass` replaces `classifyStep`'s cast-wide MODE at every ring call site (they agree on all constant casts and disagree on every turn of an alternating one). Gate at 83 clean casts / 281 scored transitions: logLoss 1.689 -> 1.337, top-1 42.3% -> 41.3%, and the structural criterion **zero-probability events 8 -> 0, PASS by construction**; paired ΔlogLoss -0.351 [-0.982, +0.051] cluster-bootstrapped over CASTS. Costs reported not buried: 3 near-tied argmaxes flip out of 281 (Δtop-1 -1.07pp [-3.38pp, 0.00pp]) and every constant cast pays +0.051 nats. All the log-loss gain is the two alternating casts. On the replay the change is behaviorally INERT at every s <= 0.05 (+0/-0 casts, +0/-0 turns) — this is a calibration and robustness fix that removes an unbounded-loss failure mode, and it does NOT raise the catch rate. `s = 0` is NOT the pre-session-49 model (it is the sticky arm's degenerate case, using the last count instead of the mode, and is worse on its own at logLoss 1.576); `ReplayOptions.hardRing` is the real before-arm.
2026-08-19 (session 49) — **`DEFAULT_SWITCH_PROBABILITY` = 0.05, and this constant has risen every single time anyone has counted it.** The session-49 brief estimated "one switch across ~309 transitions ... roughly 0.5-0.7%"; at 73 clean casts the real count was 5 switches / 238 consecutive hop pairs = 2.50% Laplace(+1); at 83 clean casts it is **14 / 284 = 5.25%**. The jump came from ONE cast — `12991364` alternates 2,1,2,1,2,1,2,1,2,1 over ten turns, the second such cast ever seen, and it appeared in the very next ten casts played after the first was found. The swept optimum tracks the estimator at both corpus sizes (0.02-0.025 at 73, 0.050 at 83), which is the check that the two are measuring the same thing. Under the hard ring this replaced, the corpus's zero-probability-event count went 3 -> 8 in those ten casts. Re-run `scripts/stickyStepSweep.ts` whenever the corpus grows; do NOT assume this constant has settled (CLAUDE.md §9 — an exceptionless count is a claim about the sample's power, not the mechanism).
2026-08-19 (session 49, brief §3) — **All three cheap focus-budget policies were built and A/B'd, ALL failed, and NOTHING SHIPPED — but the null result is uninformative and the real finding is that no harness can evaluate a focus fix.** `src/strategy/fishing/focusBudget.ts` implements `costCap` / `threshold` / `schedule` as a `FocusSpendConstraint` applied as an eligibility filter inside `bestFocusForCard` (NOT another score term — session 48 proved a constant penalty cannot encode an opportunity cost that changes with the turn index). Two invariants are asserted in `tests/fishing/focusBudget.test.ts`: a cost-0 placement always survives every policy, and a LETHAL placement is never blocked by any cap. Replay A/B paired per cast: costCap(0) +2/-16 casts (McNemar p=0.001, catastrophic), costCap(1) +1/-3, costCap(2) and threshold(<=1) byte-for-byte INERT (+0/-0, identical hit counts), threshold(2) +0/-3, schedule +0/-2 to +3/-6. The PRECONDITION check is what matters: the replay's opening-move focus spend is 0.64 of 3, against a **live 1.80** measured directly this session (`focusMoveCost`/`focusRemainingBefore`, replicated exactly across two batches). The replay disables the matcher tier (its own conservatism #3) and that is precisely the tier that pulls focus a long way, so its arm does not spend and the capped arms can never fire. Default stays `NO_FOCUS_POLICY`. The brief's "shadow price only if the cheap three fail" was deliberately NOT built: building the most expensive instrument against a target the harness cannot see is the wrong move.
2026-08-19 (session 49) — **The focus budget IS the binding constraint, now measured LIVE rather than inferred from the recorded corpus.** Pooled over 56 live turns / 10 casts: 29 turns (51.8%) are entered with an EMPTY meter, and at an empty meter realized hit collapses to 9/29 = 31.0% against 13/27 = 48.1% with points left — visible in the policy's OWN pre-shot belief, predicted P(hit) 0.286 vs 0.706. Cast `12991359` is the failure mode in one trace: focus moved 3 on turn 0, then sat at `[4,1]` for nine turns playing cards at P_hit 0.00, 0.00, 0.01, 0.00, 0.00, 0.00. **Explicitly NOT causal** — long casts accumulate empty-meter turns AND are the casts going badly, so the columns are confounded by cast length; this establishes that the constraint is real and current, not that spending less early would fix it. Two related corrections: session 48's §5c figure of 1.62 on the opening move is a TRANSPOSED-ERA number (68 of 73 casts), since `lossDecomposition.ts` measures the RECORDED corpus — correctly for its own question; and my own mid-session conclusion that session 47's zone fix had already fixed the overspend was WRONG, based on an n=5 reading of 1.00 that the next five casts refuted at 1.80. Standing rule added to SPEC-fishing.md: a decomposition measured on the recorded corpus describes the policy that RECORDED it, and an A/B run on the replay describes the replay's policy — check that the harness reproduces the phenomenon before treating either figure as a target.
2026-08-19 (session 49, brief §6) — **Null-model comparators now print alongside every live prediction metric**, in `scripts/ringPredictionReport.ts`: uniform-over-grid, uniform-over-the-union-of-both-rings, uniform-over-the-legal-k-ring, and the shipped model, with an explicit verdict line naming which nulls it beats. Scored on turn >= 1 ONLY, and that restriction is half the point — the offline LOO skips hops with no previous displacement, so pooling turn-0 rows into a number compared against it is one of the three errors that produced session 48's "unexplained" gap. `from` is reconstructed within the cast (turn t's start is turn t-1's `actual`) so the report stays self-contained on one log file. It earned itself immediately: session 48's batch beats the union-of-rings null but LOSES to the k-ring null (21.4% vs 22.6%), while session 49's two batches beat both (41.7% vs 26.4%; 26.5% vs 23.3%). A number is not interpretable until you know what it would be if nothing worked.
