2026-08-19 (session 50, §2/§3, GATE FAIL) — The expected-COVERAGE focus objective does NOT ship, in either the override or the blended form. Its premise is right — the hindsight ceilings say the 3-point budget is not scarce (99.7% at budget 3 vs 100.0% unlimited, 83 casts; 99.5% at 88) so spend quantity was never the binding dimension — but the policy that follows loses: coverage 73.6% → 89.6% (+42/−5, p<0.001) while conversion falls 62.3% → 48.5%, hit 45.8% → 43.7%, caught 24/83 → 18/83. Mechanism measured: cards played average 3.57 of 9 zones (39.7% conversion with no aiming), so the EV placement contributes +22.6pp of aiming and the coverage placement +8.8pp — it buys window and spends aim. Card mix identical across arms. Replicates at 88 casts. `coverageFocus.ts` and the two replay options stay in the tree as instrument and arms; the live default is unchanged.
2026-08-19 (session 50) — "Conversion is the binding half" is NOT a standing fact; which half binds is REGIME-DEPENDENT — the replay says conversion (73.6% × 62.3%), session 50's own live batch says coverage (37.5% × 66.7%, conversion above the pooled live 54.5%). Coverage is downstream of prediction quality: when the movement model is right the window contains the fish and only aim remains; when it is wrong the window misses and no placement objective can rescue it. Both readings are true of their own regime and neither generalizes. Recorded because the first commit of SPEC-fishing.md §9 stated only the replay's half.

Note the trend is NOT monotone: 2.50% (73 casts) → 4.98% (83) → 4.67% (88); the "it has risen every time" reasoning is superseded, the "it moves" reasoning is not.

2026-08-19 (session 50, §6) — Two standing reporting guards in SPEC-fishing.md §9: (1) no corpus statistic without its `n`, and no comparator without re-derivation at the composition of the thing it is compared to — five separate session-49 claims were this rule violated; (2) report coverage alongside hit rate on every live readout with the `hit = coverage × conversion` decomposition, because without it a low hit rate does not say which half to fix. 2026-08-19 (session 51, §2) — **`RingModelOptions.shrinkageK` is now PER STEP CLASS: `shrinkageKByClass: {1: 0.1, 2: 8}`, resolved by `shrinkageFor()`.** The two classes want opposite smoothing and the shared value (3, not the brief's claimed 1 — that is `contextualFallback.ts`'s `DEFAULT_SHRINKAGE_K`, a different knob on a different model) was optimal for neither. Mechanism is ring SIZE: k=2's legal ring is bigger, so the same corpus spreads its (prevDelta → delta) keys thinner and its conditional needs more smoothing; k=1's is near-deterministic (FACT 2, 0 reversals in 109) and wants almost none. Swept under the SHIPPED sticky path, not `runFold`'s pre-session-49 form, at 88 clean casts / 300 scored transitions (150 per class): k=1 K=0.1 → logLoss 1.040 vs shared 1.072; k=2 K=8 → 1.649 vs shared 1.718. Gated on a FIXED pair at five corpus prefixes it was NOT chosen on — paired ΔlogLoss −0.063 / −0.047 / −0.054 / −0.047 / −0.047 at 55/66/73/80/88, CI excluding zero at every size, top-1 never worse. `SHARED_SHRINKAGE_BASELINE` is added as the named before-arm so the gate cannot compare the default to itself. **The selection rule is the log-loss argmin SUBJECT TO top-1 not falling below the shared value, not the bare argmin**, for two reasons the sweep itself forced: k=2's logLoss is FLAT from K=64 to K=∞ (spread 0.002 at n=150) and K=∞ *is* the conditional-tier-OFF arm, so the bare argmin (128) ships "drop the tier" labelled "smooth more"; and k=2's conditional BUYS top-1 while COSTING calibration (34.0% at every K ≤ 8, falling to 25–29% out on the plateau), refuting the brief's "top-1 rises to 42.9% at K=16" and its sparse-and-worthless mechanism — it is sparse-and-OVERCONFIDENT. `{1: 0.1, 2: 16}` scores a better ΔlogLoss and loses top-1 at the two largest sizes; rejected. k=1's 0.1 is the argmin at all five sizes; **k=2's argmin wanders (16, 16, 512, 512, 128) and is NOT identified by the data — only its direction is.** Behaviourally INERT on the replay (+0/−0 casts, +1/−1 turns): a calibration fix, ~0.047 nats pooled, not the brief's 0.34, and not a catch-rate change.
2026-08-19 (session 51, §3) — **The matcher tier's weight is a POSTERIOR, not the fixed `1 - ringFloor` = 0.9 it had been since session 45** (`src/strategy/fishing/matcherPosterior.ts`, shipped live and as the replay default `matcherWeighting: "posterior"`). Correcting the brief's framing: the tier was never a switch — it already mixed with the ring at a CONSTANT 0.9, and the defect is a constant standing in for a belief, not a missing floor. Instrumented, the old weight handed 0.9 to all 134 matcher-active turns across **88/88 casts**, against a library supported by 8 of 88; the posterior's median is 0.135 and only **4/88** casts ever exceed 0.5. `logit(π_t) = logit(π_0) + Σ [log P_matcher(obs) − log P_ring(obs)]`, π_0 = the LOADED library's own support rate (`supportingCastCount`, 8/88 Laplace → 0.100 — deliberately the loaded library, since live reads a `mined-patterns.json` that can be older than the corpus), one turn's ratio clamped at 3 nats, refutation absorbing and unclamped, weight capped at `1 - ringFloor`. GATE: paired on 88 clean traces, cluster-bootstrapped over casts, ΔlogLoss **−0.632 [−0.760, −0.504]** vs the fixed weight, caught 19/88 → 26/88 (McNemar p ≈ 0.12 — the log loss is the result, the catch count is not). Third independent measurement in the same direction after session 49's +1.337 at n=15 and session 50's +1.300 at n=6.
2026-08-19 (session 51, §3) — **The session-51 brief's claim that the mixture "cannot lose to either arm, so the measurement stops being decision-relevant" is REFUTED, and dropping the matcher tier entirely remains the better-scoring option.** Paired: posterior − matcher-OFF = **+0.030 nats [+0.015, +0.044]**, CI excluding zero. Dominance holds for a mixture whose posterior is CORRECT; a real one carries approximations (per-turn likelihoods treated as independent given the hypothesis, a point-estimate prior), which is exactly why it was gated empirically instead of accepted from the algebra. Keeping the tier costs 0.030 nats and buys the 4/88 casts the posterior identifies. NOT decided here — dropping a whole tier is a larger reversal than the brief authorised, and the replay cannot see that session 50 measured the matcher-off arm as one that stops SPENDING FOCUS (0.71 opening spend vs live 1.80), so the tier is entangled with spending and not only prediction. QUESTIONS.md §19; one live batch reading the new `matcherWeight` log field settles it.
2026-08-19 (session 51, §3) — **The replay's `matcherWeighting` DEFAULTS to the new behaviour, deliberately breaking `offPolicyReplay.ts`'s usual "defaults preserve published numbers" convention.** That convention guards against a stale default invalidating published figures; the competing risk here is the one this repo keeps being bitten by — a future session runs `matcherTier: "loo"`, believes it is measuring live, and is measuring a weighting live abandoned. Reproducibility is preserved by NAMING the old arm (`matcherWeighting: "fixed"`, verified to reproduce session 50's LOO numbers exactly: 19/88 caught, 144/321 hit), not by making it the default. A test pins the choice.
2026-08-19 (session 51, §4) — **The armed `nextPosition` override is FLOORED at `NEXT_POSITION_OVERRIDE_WEIGHT = 0.99`, mixing 0.01 back to the ring model, and stays armed.** Does not reverse QUESTIONS.md §18; makes the armed behaviour survive its first miss. What it bounds, stated precisely rather than quoting the brief's "~5 nats": the residual is spread by the RING model, so the worst case is `-log(0.01 · p_ring(actual))` ≈ **9 nats** against 20.7 unfloored; it does NOT rescue a cell the ring itself assigns zero, which is the ring's own residual exposure (0 zero-probability events across five live batches). Costs 0.01 nats when right. The shadow dual-log is widened from "the matcher overrode the ring" to "anything overrode the ring", so the first armed batch gets a paired before/after on the SAME turns rather than two batches on different fish — the brief's §4 ask, at zero delay. Note for the next reader: `data/ringPrediction.jsonl` still contains ZERO `tier: "override"` rows; the override has never fired.
2026-08-19 (session 51, §4) — **Ring comparators were selecting on an outcome-adjacent variable and `ringPredictionReport.ts` now has a "the RING MODEL on every turn" section.** Every ring comparator filtered on `tier`, so `tier: "override"` rows left the ring model's sample entirely — and the override fires precisely on the turns the server told us the answer, which are not a random subset. The fix is NOT relabelling an override row as a ring row: it pools ring rows scored on their own prediction with overridden rows scored on their SHADOW row. Rows predating dual-logging are COUNTED and printed as unrecoverable rather than dropped (today: 100 native + 6 recovered, 23 unrecoverable, n=106 top1 24.5% logLoss 2.263).
2026-08-19 (session 51, §5) — **`liveRun.ts --dry-run` SKIPPED the energy preflight entirely, so the one change §5 most wanted verified was the one a dry run could not reach.** `ensureEnergyFor` gains `{ readOnly }` (every read, every verdict, no claim) and the dry run now passes `readOnly: args.dryRun`. Verified live at zero cost: pool 270 covers a juiced 60; forcing the ROM-bank branch read-only reads 37 ROMs / 27 collectable / 2480 claimable, selects 1 ROM (snapshot 315) for a 130 deficit, and fails closed correctly at an unfundable requirement with a full detail payload. `--juiced` without `--juiced-index=N` correctly refuses to guess. NOT reachable by any dry run and still unexercised after nine sessions: tier enumeration and the first in-run decision point, both of which require an active run and therefore the 60-energy entry.
2026-08-19 (session 51, §5) — **`serverErrorDetail` dropped `TokenExpiredError`'s `.body`, so every auth failure on both game modes logged this repo's own summary string and nothing the server said.** Found by the §5 dry-run by corrupting the JWT: the real 401 body is `{"error":"Unauthorized"}`, and an expired token, a revoked session and a rate-limited auth all produced the identical line while wanting different responses. Fourth instance of SPEC-fishing.md §4's recurring shape — a fix applied to one class and the sibling with the identical field never re-scored (this function was created in session 46 to fix exactly this omission for `UnexpectedResponseError`). Fixed, and `tests/api/errors.test.ts` — the function had NO test at all — now asserts that EVERY body-carrying class in the module is handled, so a fifth instance fails the suite instead of shipping. Separately, `shutdown.ts`'s "thin enough not to need its own test" comment is retired: two tests now raise a real in-process SIGINT and assert the flag flips and the disposer genuinely unsubscribes.
2026-08-19 (session 51, §6) — **The session-51 brief's rejection of a per-cast reversal parameter is REFUTED; the status is OPEN-but-not-significant, not closed.** Brief: 15 casts, 32/69 = 46.4% pooled, dispersion ratio **0.80** ("at or below binomial, no heterogeneity to exploit"), 0 casts never reverse. Measured on 88 clean casts by `scripts/reversalDispersion.ts` (committed, not run once and discarded): **24 casts, 43/124 = 34.7%, dispersion 1.452** (χ² = 33.39, df = 23, **p = 0.0746**), and **3 casts never reverse**. 0.80 says "less spread than chance"; 1.452 says the opposite direction. The practical instruction is unchanged — do not build it yet — but the REASON must be cited as "p = 0.075 at n = 24", never as "there is no heterogeneity", which the corpus does not support. Re-run the script as the corpus grows; if the ratio holds above 1 and clears p < 0.05 the parameter becomes a live candidate. Also recorded: the pooled k=2 reversal rate has DRIFTED DOWN, 41.7% (35/84) at 66 casts to 34.7% (43/124) at 88 — another constant that moves when counted again. The script's corpus test asserts the DIRECTION (ratio > 1), not today's value, so a future flip fails loudly.
2026-08-19 (session 51, §6) — **SPEC-fishing.md §9 gains the OVERRIDE-VS-MIXTURE principle as the frame unifying three fixed defects**: the hard ring (session 49's sticky latent, zero-probability events 8 → 0), the matcher tier (session 51, ΔlogLoss −0.632), and the `nextPosition` point mass (session 51, 20.7 → ~9 nats worst case). The tell is always the same and is the thing worth learning to spot: **a constant standing in for a belief** — `k` is constant per cast, 0.9, p = 1; three numbers, none estimated from anything, each asserting certainty about a proposition the corpus only ever supported as usually true, and in every case the corpus held the counterexample before anyone looked. Two limits are written in alongside it, both learned this session: a mixture is NOT automatically better and still needs its gate (the matcher mixture measurably lost to the drop-the-tier arm), and "mix it" is not a licence to keep a tier that is not paying.
2026-08-19 (session 51) — **`data/mined-patterns.json` is STALE and was deliberately NOT regenerated.** Live loads `perimeterWalk(cw)`/`(ccw)`, explaining 8 of 88 clean casts; re-mining at 88 promotes FOUR patterns — adding `bounce(2,0)` and `bounce(-2,0)` — explaining 11 of 88. Not re-mined because it changes live matcher behaviour and `scripts/mineFishPatterns.ts` owns that decision. The posterior's prior is computed from the LOADED library so it stays correct either way (a re-mine simply raises π₀ from 0.100 to ~0.144). QUESTIONS.md §20 — worth doing BEFORE §19 is judged, since a bigger library is a different tier and "is the matcher worth keeping" should be asked of the library that would actually ship.
2026-08-19 — `ensureEnergyFor` gains `order` ("ascending"|"descending", default DESCENDING) and `maxClaims` (default unbounded); omitting both is byte-for-byte the session-47 behaviour — ascending is the right posture for a FIRST live exercise of an unproven path (walks the loop, risks the smallest ROM) and NOT a better steady-state default; the descending rationale (an interrupted pass made the most progress it could) still stands once the path is proven.
2026-08-19 — The ROM-claim path is CONFIRMED working live: 4 claims across 2 runs, measured pool delta == snapshot total exactly (drift 0) both times, every claimed ROM reading `energyCollectable: 0` afterwards — session 20's observed +1 accrual drift did not reproduce.

2026-08-19 — SPEC §2's "path-selection actions don't use a numeric token at all" is CONTRADICTED live as of today: the empty-string `actionToken` is rejected on first attempt 26/26 and the identical retry always succeeds. Envelope deliberately NOT changed — a DevTools capture of the browser making a reward pick is the cheap resolution (no energy), a one-line token experiment is the expensive one (60-energy entry). See QUESTIONS.md §21.

2026-08-19 — The mined library's `bounce(2,0)` and `bounce(-2,0)` are exact ALIASES on every supporting cast; dedup NOT applied because the deduped variant is equally indistinguishable (ΔlogLoss -0.0056, caught 24 vs 27 vs 26 — noise at n=88). The fix belongs upstream in `buildPatternPool()`/`promotePatterns`, with its own gate. See QUESTIONS.md §22.
2026-08-19 — `matcherTier: "loo"` re-mines the library per fold and has NEVER read `data/minedFishPatterns.json`, so every session-50/51 matcher replay figure describes a LOO-mined library rather than the one live loads; `ReplayOptions.matcherLibrary` added to make live-library A/Bs possible at all.
2026-08-19 — A live run must never be piped to a truncating reader (`head`, `grep -m`): SIGPIPE killed session 52's run 2 mid-battle with 60 energy already committed. Redirect to a file and read the file; `--resume-existing --potions=N --potions-used=M` is the recovery.
2026-08-20 (session 53, §21 RESOLVED) — **The path-selection rejection was NEVER a server change; session 52's diagnosis is REFUTED by the same logs it cited.** Counting `post_attempt_failed` on `reason` — populated on BOTH sides of session 47/51's `serverErrorDetail` fix — the 2026-08-18 logs hold 40 rejections in 40 decisions, the identical 100% rate as 2026-08-20's 26/26. Session 52 grepped for `"Invalid action token"`, a string that could not exist before the fix landed, and read *newly visible* as *new*. The mechanism is TIMING: the server holds one outstanding action token and rejects any POST carrying `actionToken: ""` while it is still outstanding. Over ten pre-fix logs, on local response timestamps: empty-token rejected n=66 at 0.90–1.54s since the last response, empty-token accepted n=66 at 3.40–4.92s, numeric n=224 with 0 failures at 0.90–1.79s, `start_run` 4/4 accepted (the control — same empty token, no token outstanding). Zero overlap; threshold in (1.54, 3.40)s. `actionToken: ""` is CONFIRMED and correct — it was only being sent too soon.
2026-08-20 (session 53, §0c) — **The fix is `RequestPacing.minGapSinceResponseMs = 4000` on `reward_*`/`path_*` only, expressed on the RESPONSE clock, and the session-53 brief's 3600ms was on the WRONG CLOCK.** `RateLimiter` stamps `lastCallAt` BEFORE dispatch, so `MIN_GAP_MS` is request-to-request; it differs from the response clock by one response latency (0.72–1.78s, median 1.45, n=296). 3600ms request-to-request leaves ~1.8s since the response in the worst case — inside the band where 66/66 POSTs were rejected — and would very likely have failed the brief's own gate. Caught before the runs from the brief's own data, not by raising a number until it passed. `start_run` is deliberately EXCLUDED (control case, 4/4 accepted, delaying it would slow every entry for nothing). `postWithVerifiedRetry` untouched — the double-apply hazard it guards is real and independent. **GATE PASS: 24 live path-selection decisions, 0 first-attempt rejections, against a historical 100%.**
2026-08-20 (session 53) — CLAUDE.md gains **rule 10: date an effect on a field that predates the instrumentation change.** This repo improves its own logging constantly, and every before/after comparison that straddles such a fix is comparing "what happened" against "what was recorded". Before concluding behaviour changed at date D, check whether any field you are counting on first APPEARS at D; if so, re-ask using a field that predates it, or say plainly that the logs cannot date it.
2026-08-20 (session 53, §1) — **First-attempt failures are counted per action class even when the retry succeeds** (`src/orchestrator/attemptTelemetry.ts`), reported in the RUN SUMMARY including on the failure path, WARN at >=20%. A per-class SUCCESS rate that scores the retry as the outcome reports 100% for a class rejected on every first attempt — which is exactly how §21 hid for thirty-nine sessions across seven runs. Generalises: any retry loop that succeeds is a place a persistent server-side disagreement can hide indefinitely.
2026-08-20 (session 53, §22 RESOLVED) — **Pattern aliasing is fixed UPSTREAM in `buildPatternPool()` via `dedupePatterns`, and it was FIVE aliases of 23 primitives, not the one pair session 52 found.** Equivalence is decided on a full behaviour signature (trajectories from every start cell at every `GAME_GRID_SIZES` entry; all 531 corpus `gridSize` values are 4). Pool 23 -> 18, re-mined library 4 -> 3 patterns, support unchanged at 4/4/3 = 11 casts of 89 so pi_0 stays ~0.133 while the oscillation hypothesis drops from 2/4 of the candidate mass to 1/3. GATE: DlogLoss -0.0017 [-0.0063, +0.0033], caught 27 -> 24 — INERT, and shipped as a correctness fix to the PRIOR, not a prediction improvement. Fixed in the pool rather than in `promotePatterns` because aliasing is a property of the primitive set at a grid size, not of any one corpus.
2026-08-20 (session 53) — **Gating a de-aliasing change requires a RAW before-arm (`minedLibraryGate.ts --before-raw`).** Loading the pre-dedup library through the new `resolvePatternsByName` collapses the duplicate, silently turning the A/B into 3-vs-3 and scoring DlogLoss exactly 0.0000 — a clean-looking null result that measured nothing. `resolvePatternsByName` itself maps a retired alias name onto its surviving twin so pre-dedup library files still load rather than being silently dropped.
2026-08-20 (session 53, §3) — **`maxSnapshot` and `headroom` are reported in `claim_audit`, and they CLOSE the overflow question by construction for this code path.** Live both runs: largest single ROM snapshot 315 vs pool headroom 394/395, so no single claim `ensureEnergyFor` can make is capable of reaching the 420 cap. Switching the default claim order to descending therefore cannot trip the never-tested "overflow past the cap is non-wasting" comment by accident. Served from the `getEnergy` response already made (no extra request) and reported as null rather than a guessed 420 when the cap is unknown.
2026-08-20 (session 53, §6) — **The "modelled boon never offered in room 1" list is EMPTY: 0 of 17.** Session 52's own `AddMaxHealth` capture closed the last wall-1 hole, so that retroactive mechanic (session 11 AddMaxArmor, 43 UpgradePaper, 52 AddMaxHealth) cannot recur until something new is added to `BOON_MODELS`. The real untested surface is the OPPOSITE gap — 36 boon types offered in captured runs with no `BOON_MODELS` entry. `scripts/boonCoverage.ts` reports both directions so this is read from a list rather than rediscovered by accident.
2026-08-20 (session 53) — **The action token cannot be used as an absolute clock.** It is a ms epoch, but `token_epoch - dispatch_ts` measures 0.70–2.68s (median 1.93) — larger than the full POST->response round trip (median 1.45s). The server clock runs ~1.5–2s ahead of this machine. Relative comparisons within a session are fine; absolute "elapsed since the token was issued" is not. Every session-53 timing conclusion is anchored on LOCAL response timestamps only.
2026-08-20 (session 53) — **`rejectionAudit.ts`'s gap fields are measured at LOG-WRITE time, not dispatch.** `liveRun.ts` writes its `post` event before calling the client and the rate limiter sleeps inside it. Historically the first-attempt sleep was ~0–0.2s so the bands approximate dispatch; after the session-53 pacing fix they do NOT (an empty-token POST logs at ~1.2s and dispatches at ~4.0s). Use the `postToOutcomeMs` column to see pacing. The pre-fix 66/66 split and the zero-overlap property are pinned against `PRE_SESSION_53_LOGS` explicitly, because the fix deliberately makes them untrue of the corpus as a whole.

Not regen — regen has the right sign but is ~0.6 energy over a ~2-minute run and would not land on exactly -1 thrice. Conservative in the safe direction since the guard enforces off COMMITTED spend, so nothing is at risk. Resolvable at zero energy by reading `GET /offchain/player/energy` immediately before and after a juiced `start_run` and comparing with the same pair around a plain 20-energy run.

2026-08-19 (session 54, §1) — **Closing the dungeon arm required DROPPING three guard locks, which was not in the brief and is a bug the closure itself would otherwise introduce.** `orchestrator.ts` took `DEFAULT_GUARD_STATE_PATH`, `DEFAULT_OPPONENT_MODEL_PATH` and `DEFAULT_PLAY_COUNTS_PATH` locks and held all three for the life of the process; `acquireGuardLock` is an exclusive-create lockfile and `liveRun.ts` needs all three. An 8-hour orchestrator session would therefore have REFUSED every rule-11 dungeon run the user approved during it — the exact runs rule 11 exists to enable. With the arm closed this process never writes any of the three files, so holding their locks is pure obstruction. Only the fishing lock is kept.

2026-08-19 (session 54, §1) — **The brief's stated potion invariant ("no code path constructs a `LiveRunDeps` with a `potionPolicy` and no `juicedStartRun`") is FALSE of a legitimate path and was NOT implemented as written.** `liveRun.ts --potions=N --resume-existing` deliberately builds a potion policy without `--juiced`, because that run's consumables were committed server-side by whoever started it; refusing them would strand a resume. "descending"` and `liveRun.ts` `claimOrderRaw ?? "descending"` since session 52; sessions 52 and 53 ran ascending by passing `--claim-order=ascending` EXPLICITLY, and the brief read those runs as the default. The change is to stop passing the flag. 2026-08-19 (session 54) — **The FIRST redaction fix reproduced the same defect one level down, and the test that caught it is the only version worth writing.** Redacting `NOOB_TOKEN_CID` left the identical id fully readable in the same 2,725 files as the suffix of an `EntityEquipment` docId, and once more as the account doc's own `docId`. 2026-08-20 (session 56, §3) — **Every `enemyBuff` is SELF-DESCRIBING on the wire, and the session-56 brief's plan to "APPLY" one would DOUBLE-COUNT it.** All 46 corpus ids carry structured `effects[]` (`kind`/`amount`/`percent`/`statusType`/`moveType`), not just `bloodthirsty`. A stat buff is ALREADY inside the numbers the combat model reads. So the correct change is the opposite of the brief's: stop marking a battle unscorable for a buff that moves no RULE. `applyStatBuff()` exists for VERIFICATION only and says so; combat must never call it. Percentages round UP — 14 × 1.3 = 18.2 lands on 19, ruling out floor and round-half.
2026-08-20 (session 56, §3) — **The buff fail-closed line is drawn on the effect KIND, not on the buff ID.** 46 ids but only 12 kinds; the game adds ids far faster than mechanics, and a NEW id built entirely from kinds we already understand should be handled on sight rather than halting a run. `classifyBuff` returns `unknown` for an unrecognised kind, an unknown id, or an empty `effects[]`, and reads the LIVE effects rather than the table's stored class so a server-side redefinition of a familiar id is caught rather than trusted. `coverage.ts`'s `ENEMY_BUFF` is now raised only for a mechanic buff (status / lifesteal / corrode / start-status) or an unknown one.

Tier composition: tier 0 = 188 paths / 0 buffed / 0 rolled; tier 1 = 298 / 298 / 293; tier 2 = 324 / 324 / 324. **After a CLAUDE.md rule-8 reversal the simulator scores almost no fight regardless of how well buffs are modelled.** Room 9 is the concrete case and answers the brief's question directly: `bloodthirsty` is statOnly so `ENEMY_BUFF` clears, but evasion 3 / block 1 / lck 2 / tenacity 2 keep `ROLLED_STATS` — it does NOT become modellable.

Quality by tier, controlled for room, is directionally positive in 4 of 5 within-room contrasts (room 3 Δ+4.21 meanOrb at n=25/10/2, room 4 Δ+0.66, room 5 Δ+1.30, room 6 Δ−1.53 at n=1, room 7 Δ+2.17) — **suggestive, not established**, on 24 non-Safe offers total.
2026-08-20 (session 56, §4) — **Rule 8's own evidence does not refute the reversal, and the reason should be stated rather than re-derived: the two claims are ORTHOGONAL.** Rule 8 measured `lootTable` identity IN THE ENEMY OFFER, 440/440. Reward-card quality and score payout are downstream of WINNING the fight, and an enemy offer's `lootTable` cannot show them. That is how a hard rule can be reversed without anyone having been wrong. The honest ceiling on §4's answer: the bot has taken the lowest tier in every unforced decision it has ever made, so every non-Safe row in the audit came from a room that offered no Safe option — forced, never chosen. There is ZERO data on what beating a deliberately chosen hard enemy yields; rule 8 created its own evidence base, and the first few flipped runs ARE the measurement.

So choosing a reward boon also chooses a Hard Core payout, and both `pickBoon` and `pickBoonWithPriority` are completely blind to it. This is the nearest thing in the data to the user's "harder cores payout" claim and probably the cheapest live gain available. Needs a user directive on how to trade orbs against boon quality before it can be implemented.
2026-08-20 (session 56, §4) — **Two corpus traps, both found the hard way, both now documented in `scripts/rewardTierAudit.ts` and `tests/rewardTier.test.ts`.** (1) A run DIRECTORY holds MULTIPLE ATTEMPTS and `entity.ID_CID` is literally `5` — the DUNGEON id, not a run id — so attempts must be delimited by `ROOM_NUM_CID` DECREASING; joining per-directory produced 5 bogus inheritance "exceptions", every one a cross-attempt join. (2) `ROOM_NUM_CID` lives on `data.entity`, NOT `data.entity.data`, where it reads `undefined` silently and collapses every room-controlled comparison into a single bucket.
2026-08-20 (session 56, USER DIRECTIVE, given mid-session) — **Never take the hardest enemy card when it carries a `Perpetual` condition; fall through to the next best by existing criteria.** Implemented as a strict WITHIN-TIER tie-break in `pickLowestTier` (`preferNonPerpetual`), so it CANNOT change which tier is fought and CLAUDE.md rule 8 is untouched. `perpetual_` is a DELIVERY difference, not an effect one — 23 of the 46 ids are effect-twins of a base id whose buff persists for the rest of the run. Measured: 47 of 134 offers (35%) put a perpetual on the top tier, so post-flip this fires on about a third of rooms — a substantial carve-out, not an edge case; only 4 offers are all-one-tier with a perpetual among them (the only shape reachable under rule 8) and all 4 have a non-perpetual alternative at that tier; **0 offers are entirely perpetual**. Fails OPEN if one ever is: a preference among equals must not strand a 60-energy run, unlike the tier rule itself.
2026-08-20 (session 56, §5) — **The final-room "no modifiers" exception is keyed on the SERVER's `maxRoom`, never the literal 16, and the index scheme was verified before encoding.** There is no `floor` field anywhere in the corpus, so "floor 4 room 4" cannot be cross-checked — but it does not need to be: `dungeon-today`'s container publishes `maxRoom` directly, and Forbidden Woods (ID_CID 5) publishes **16**, the user's number exactly. It is PER DUNGEON (Void Dungeon publishes 17), so `pickTierForRoom` takes it as a parameter. `isUnmodified` is STRICTER than "lowest tier" — no `enemyBuff` AND every rolled stat zero, so a stat-only buff still counts as a modifier here because the directive is about the card, not about whether the sim can score it. The test is `room >= maxRoom`, not `=== maxRoom`, because the failure directions are asymmetric: no-modifiers at the wrong room costs a little reward, the hardest card at the ACTUAL final room costs the boss fight. It never raises a tier to find a clean card. INERT under rule 8 and NOTHING is gated on it (CLAUDE.md rule 6) — the corpus has never seen room 16, the deepest run ever being room 10.
2026-08-20 (session 57, §1) — **CLAUDE.md rule 8 is REVERSED in code: `pickLowestTier` is DELETED and the live selector is `pickHighestTier`.** Not renamed-and-kept: a function called `pickLowestTier` that returns the highest tier is a trap a future reader falls into in thirty seconds, and the brief named naming — not logic — as this change's real risk. `chooseTier` became `lowestTierOption` for the same reason, with a `highestTierOption` sibling. The Perpetual clause is applied as a FILTER BEFORE the max, never "take the max then check": filtering first is the only ordering that does not raise a fallback question on the 35% of offers whose top tier is perpetual. `pickSafeTier`/`assertSafeTier`/`UnsafeTierError` survive unused and now say in their own doc comment that they are the exact opposite of the standing directive — kept only because SPEC, DECISIONS and the session logs cite them by name.
2026-08-20 (session 57, §1) — **An all-Perpetual offer now FAILS CLOSED, REVERSING session 56's fail-open decision on the same shape, and the asymmetry with the final-room path is deliberate.** Session 56 chose fail-open when the clause was a within-tier tie-break that could not change which tier was fought; under rule 8 it decides the tier, and choosing the hardest card is a deliberate act of taking on risk the user has forbidden in this exact form. `pickHighestTier` throws `PerpetualOnlyOfferError`. `pickFinalRoomTier` still fails OPEN on the identical shape, because there it is already reaching for the least dangerous card, there is nothing safer to fall back to, and stranding the boss room costs the whole 60-energy run. 0 of 134 corpus offers are entirely perpetual, so neither branch has ever executed — which is the reason the halting one must be loud rather than quiet.
2026-08-20 (session 57, §1) — **An UNREADABLE room or `maxRoom` resolves to the final-room no-modifiers clause, and is labelled `final-room-unreadable` so it can never make the flip silently inert.** The failure directions are asymmetric (no-modifiers at the wrong room costs a little reward; the hardest card at the real final room costs the boss fight), so conservative is right — but session 56 found `ROOM_NUM_CID` lives on `data.entity`, NOT `data.entity.data`, where it reads `undefined` silently and `liveRun.ts` defaults it to 0. If that field moves again, EVERY room would take the conservative branch and the flip would be off with no error anywhere. `tierRuleFor()` returns the third label and `liveRun.ts` prints a `⚠` naming `ROOM_NUM_CID`. `maxRoom` itself was verified against a LIVE response before it was allowed to govern anything (`scripts/checkMaxRoom.ts`, one free GET): Forbidden Woods 16, Void Dungeon 17, Dungetron 16, Underhaul 16, matching `config/discovered.json`. `liveRun` now takes it from `config.maxRoom` (server-published) rather than the `MAX_ROOM` literal.
2026-08-20 (session 57, §1) — **The retry re-locator must ROUTE THROUGH the tier rule, not re-implement it.** `locateLowestTierOption` held its own inline "lowest tier" scan, which was equivalent to the rule when it was written. Under rule 8 it would not have been: an inline max-tier scan skips the Perpetual filter and the final-room exception, so a RETRY could land on precisely the card the decision path had refused — and retries happen on the action-token path, where nobody is watching. Renamed `locateChosenTierOption(run, room, maxRoom)` and delegated to `pickTierForRoom`. `PerpetualOnlyOfferError` propagates out of it deliberately: an offer that turned all-perpetual between attempts is a new surprise and fails closed.
2026-08-20 (session 57, §2) — **`gigusOrbAmount` ships as a tie-break WITHIN one priority rank, and it is worth almost nothing: +16 orbs over 552 decisions (+0.029/decision), changing the pick on 0.7%.** Reported plainly rather than shipped as a gain, per the brief. The reason is not the field — payouts differ across options in 136 of 138 offers, mean spread 6.22 — it is that the winning priority rank TIES on only 16 of 552 decisions (2.9%). The wider reading the same directive does NOT authorise (orbs decide whenever no priority family matches, which is 56.5% of decisions) is worth **+1000 orbs, +1.81/decision, 35.5% of picks changed** — 62x the shipped rule, and comparable in scale to session 56's whole enemy-tier effect (+4.21 mean orbs at room 3). NOT shipped: the directive is tie-break only and widening is the user's call (QUESTIONS.md §24). `scripts/orbTieBreakReport.ts` prints all three policies; `tests/rewardTier.test.ts` pins the tie rate so a later reader does not assume the bot chases orbs.
2026-08-20 (session 57, §2) — **The orb tie-break refuses to fire on a PARTIAL capture rather than read an absent `gigusOrbAmount` as zero.** It requires a payout on EVERY option tied at the rank. Reading the absence as 0 would hand the pick to whichever option happened to be recorded, on no evidence — a silent wrong answer in exactly the direction the field was added to improve, and the same shape of trap as CLAUDE.md rule 10's back-compat default. All 138 corpus offers carry it on all three options today, so the guard is currently inert; it exists for the response shape that changes.
2026-08-20 (session 57, §4) — **Offline gating of DUNGEON strategy is over, and this is stated once here rather than discovered over five sessions of gates that quietly stop meaning anything.** Post-flip the simulator scores almost nothing (617 of 622 non-Safe paths carry unmodellable rolled stats), and it was already scoring 5.8% of exchanges and unable to separate two boon policies at n=2000. From here a dungeon strategy change is justified by being a USER DIRECTIVE or by being MECHANICALLY OBVIOUSLY CORRECT (reading an ignored field, fixing a double-count), and validated by LIVE OUTCOME over many runs — at rule 11's four juiced runs/day that is weeks, not sessions. Anything claiming an offline gate must name the arm that separated and at what n, or say plainly that it did not. **Fishing is now the only place in this project where an offline gate still means anything** (88 clean paired traces, bootstrapped, still separates arms) — a further reason QUESTIONS.md §19 has been worth waiting for. `src/sim/dungeonSim.ts`'s Safe-tier default is left alone deliberately: raising it would not close the gap, only make the sim refuse to score.
2026-08-20 (session 58) — **ENERGY IS NOT A CONSTRAINT AND MUST NEVER BE WRITTEN INTO A PLAN AS ONE. User directive, given with visible frustration at having to repeat it.** The account generates **~1368 energy/day** once the ROMs NFTs held by this character are counted, not the 432/day that `GET /offchain/player/energy`'s `regenPerHour: 18` × 24 implies. That endpoint reports the passive regen pool ONLY (`energyValue`, `maxEnergy: 420`); it does not report ROM yield, which is why reading it as a ceiling is wrong every time. Session 58 read `energyValue: 11` and declared both of the session's live items blocked for ~13h — a fabricated blocker that would have cost the whole session. `scripts/claimRoms.ts` and `scripts/claimAllRoms.ts` already exist for the claim side. **The real caps are the GAME's own daily ledgers and nothing else**: fishing casts (`dayDocs[pondId]`, 20/day) and dungeon run-units (`dayProgressEntities`, 12/day), both rolling over at 11:00 PT, plus this repo's own policy budgets in `config/bot.json`. Check those; do not check energy, do not report an energy number as a reason a task cannot run, and do not schedule around regen.
2026-08-20 (session 58, §1) — **QUESTIONS §24 RESOLVED: the wide orb reading SHIPS as `orbRule: "wide"`, the default in `boonPriority.ts` and a `config/bot.json` knob.** Where NO option matches a priority family (56.5% of decisions) the richest `gigusOrbAmount` wins and `rankBoons` breaks payout ties; it still cannot override a priority family, because it only runs where the priority layer matched nothing. Settled by an experiment against a decision rule the session-58 brief fixed BEFORE any number existed ("ship if the depth loss is under 0.15 rooms; unresolved 0.15–0.292; reject above the 0.292 break-even"): `scripts/orbDepthExperiment.ts` at n=8000 per arm with identical seeds measured the loss at **-0.002 rooms, paired 95% CI [-0.018, +0.014]** — the whole interval ~11x inside the bar — for **+6.3 Hard Core per run (+10.4%)**. The paired statistic matters: 78.9% of seeds produce an identical run in both arms, and an unpaired interval would have thrown that agreement away as noise. Session 57's "no offline experiment can settle it" was wrong in a specific and reusable way — §2e failed to DETECT a difference, while §24 needed one BOUNDED against a threshold, and a test whose precision exceeds the threshold is informative even when null. **Do not narrow this back without a user directive**, the same standing the rule it replaced had.
2026-08-20 (session 58, §1) — **A null depth result is only meaningful if the depth CHANNEL is open, which is a separate question from precision and is now checked first.** `applyBoon` moves player state for exactly six boon types — Heal, UpgradeRock/Paper/Scissor, AddMaxArmor, AddMaxHealth. The five `rolled` types write a stat `combat.ts` never reads; the six `latent` types hit `case "latent": break;`; the 36 unmodelled types return the player unchanged. Two arms that differ only on inert options are therefore BIT-IDENTICAL, and a 0.00-room difference would be a property of the instrument, not of the policy. `orbDepthExperiment.ts` measures this as stage 0 and reports UNRESOLVED regardless of stage 1 if the channel is closed. It was open here: C differs from B on 34.4% of decisions, 25.8% of those differences touch a state-moving option, and 21.1% of seeds diverged. **Any future sim comparison of two BOON policies must clear this bar before its null is read as evidence** — the six-type list is derived from `BOON_MODELS` in the script, not hand-copied, so it cannot go stale.
2026-08-20 (session 58, §1) — **A corpus reward offer's room is `ROOM_NUM_CID - 1`, and `scripts/orbTieBreakReport.ts` shipped in session 57 reading the raw wire value.** The reward phase is reached with the room counter ALREADY ADVANCED past the room whose clear produced the offer. Measured 135/135 with no exceptions against `OBSERVED_OFFERS`, whose room labels have used the correct convention for 50+ sessions. Corrected, and re-running gave A/B/C totals **identical to the orb**, so session 57's §24 numbers stand and the defect was inert on this corpus — but it would not stay inert on a deeper one, because `room` feeds `priorityOf`'s rooms-1..8 lifesteal window and `rankBoons`' `roomsRemaining` weighting. Pinned by `tests/orbOffers.test.ts`. Note the related trap it exposes: 17 of the 135 `OBSERVED_OFFERS` rows name a `source` file TWO STATES LATER than the one holding the offer, and those 17 are the corpus's DEEPEST offers — so a source-keyed join silently drops exactly the rows a depth experiment most wants. `src/sim/orbOffers.ts` joins on room+content instead and resolves all 135.
2026-08-20 (session 58, §1) — **`SimOptions.offers` has a second legitimate use, and the line is the offer DISTRIBUTION, not the hook.** Its doc comment reserved it for labelled counterfactuals ("never use it to generate a reported result"). `src/sim/orbOffers.ts` passes back exactly `OBSERVED_OFFERS` — same rows, order, room, source and `type`/`val1`/`val2` — with the recorded payout joined onto each option, because that field is one the hand-transcribed table never carried and an orb policy is blind without it. Nothing is invented, so the result is a real measurement. What licenses the claim is `assertDistributionPreserved()`, which the caller MUST run before reporting anything and which `tests/orbOffers.test.ts` proves rejects a tampered table. Enrich the offers; never change which ones a room gets.
2026-08-20 (session 58) — **A config knob with a code default MUST be listed in the zod schema** (`src/orchestrator/config.ts`). Zod strips unknown keys silently, so `orbRule` in `config/bot.json` would have read as absent and setting it to `"tie-break"` to revert session 58's change would have done nothing — a config that lies rather than fails. Added to both the schema and the `BotConfig` type at the same time as the knob itself.
2026-08-20 (session 58) — **CLAUDE.md rule 11's ENERGY derivation of the daily ceiling is DELETED, and rule 4 is retitled from "Energy is real money. Simulate first." to "Simulate first." — user-approved this session.** Rule 11 derived 4 juiced runs/day two ways, "240 energy / 60" and "12 run-units / 3", and instructed a STOP if they ever disagreed on the theory that one had been edited without the other. At ~1368 energy/day they disagree permanently and CORRECTLY (1368/60 = 22.8), so the rule carried a tripwire that fires on good data — a fail-closed check whose premise had become false. The ceiling now rests solely on **12 run-units / 3, which the SERVER enforces** (`maxRunsPerDay: 12`, `dayProgressEntities`; `scripts/checkDungeonToday.ts` reads it, and `liveRun.ts` refuses with "real server cap already reached today"). Rule 4's substance — no strategy code meets the live API before it passes against `fixtures/` — is unchanged; only its energy framing went, replaced by the run allowance, which is the actually scarce thing.

Session 58 instead read `energyValue: 11` off `GET /offchain/player/energy` and reported a thirteen-hour blocker that did not exist; the dry run, when finally run, printed the true cause immediately (`real server runs today: 12/12 — any start_run will be rejected server-side`) AND showed the energy preflight offering to claim one ROM out of a 2251-energy bank. This generalises past energy: the guards are the authority on whether something can run, and they are free to consult.
2026-08-20 (session 59) — **SPEC's Path B ("bot-owned EOA", `AUTH_MODE=eoa`) is DELETED, `viem` is dropped, and `scripts/probe.ts`'s private-key login is gone with them.** User directive via CLAUDE.md rule 3 (commit `e74fe3e`). The account is an Abstract Global Wallet, which exposes no user-held EOA private key, so Path B was **unreachable, not unfinished** — it would have authenticated a different, empty account. SPEC §1a was REWRITTEN rather than annotated, and §1b's EIP-191 flow (marked **[CONFIRMED]**) removed: a confirmed-and-unfinished feature is an invitation for a later session to helpfully complete it. **The portability brief justified the `viem` half as "a declared dependency imported nowhere in `src/`", which was true and incomplete** — `scripts/probe.ts` imported it and carried a full working implementation that read `~/.secrets/gigaverse-private-key.txt` and signed with `privateKeyToAccount`. That is exactly what falsifies the one sentence this repo can offer someone deciding whether to run it: **it asks for a session token, not custody of a wallet.** Do not replace it with AGW session keys either — those are for delegated ON-CHAIN signing and this bot does no on-chain work at all; adding a signer to solve a problem the program does not have would cost the safety story for nothing.
2026-08-20 (session 59, §1) — **All disk paths resolve through `src/profile.ts`; `--profile=<name>` (or `GIGA_PROFILE`) gives a second account full separation, and OMITTING it is byte-for-byte the old behaviour.** Wired through all four entry points. Four decisions inside it: (a) **`getJwt()` is a PROVIDER, not a loaded string** — one indirection now keeps refresh-on-expiry reachable without rewriting call sites later; (b) **defaults are pinned in tests as LITERALS**, never by comparing against the module's own constant, because a test that compares a constant to itself passes no matter what that constant becomes — which is the exact failure it guards; (c) **profile names are REJECTED, not sanitised** (`..`, `a/b`, empty, leading dash), since a name that silently becomes a different directory is how one person's spend ledger overwrites another's; (d) **the JWT stays in `~/.secrets`, never under `profiles/`**, which is inside a public repo and one `git add -A` from being committed. The brief's prediction that this would be mostly mechanical held: sessions 30/31 had already parameterised every I/O-owning module (to stop tests clobbering real ledgers), leaving only THREE real path literals in `src/`.
2026-08-20 (session 59, §1) — **`config/discovered.json` is SHARED across profiles; `config/bot.json` is PER-PROFILE.** Dungeon IDs, energy costs and room counts are properties of the GAME — Forbidden Woods is dungeon 5 and `maxRoom` 16 for everyone (confirmed on four dungeons, session 57) — so per-profile discovery would make every additional person re-run `probe.ts` to rediscover facts that cannot differ between them. Budgets are personal and go the other way. **That split is the entire reason these are two files**, and the first cut of the seam got it wrong by putting both under the profile; the brief had called it out. Pinned by `tests/profile.test.ts`.
2026-08-20 (session 59, §2) — **The client's surface is a checked safety property, not a promise: `tests/clientSurface.test.ts` enumerates every method and fails the build when one is added.** No sell, burn, list, transfer, or on-chain transaction exists; the 12 named endpoints are 9 reads plus `postDungeonAction`, `postFishingAction`, and `claimRomEnergy` (which moves the account's OWN accrued energy into its OWN pool — no recipient, no counterparty). **The honest complication, recorded so nobody "cleans it up":** `raw`/`get`/`post` are `private` in TypeScript, which is ERASED AT RUNTIME — they are on the prototype and `post` can reach any path. They are LISTED rather than filtered out, because filtering them would claim a smaller surface than exists; a separate test asserts no call site outside `client.ts` touches them, and another asserts nothing in `src/`/`scripts/` imports a signing library. If this test ever fails, do not add the method to the allowlist to make it green — ask whether the program should be able to do that to someone else's account, and land the README change in the same commit.
2026-08-20 (session 59) — **`profiles/` is gitignored, and the test asserts it via real `git check-ignore` rather than by reading `.gitignore`.** Gitignore patterns without a leading slash match at ANY depth, so `profiles/<name>/data` and `.../logs` were covered by `data/`/`logs/` — **by luck**. `profiles/<name>/fixtures` and `.../config` were NOT, so the first use of `--profile` would have committed a second account's captured game states and budget file to this PUBLIC repo. The test shells out to git because the pattern semantics are the thing under test; re-implementing them in the test would reproduce the same misunderstanding that caused the bug.
2026-08-20 (session 59) — **Pin a test to the PROPERTY, not to an identifier.** Session 54's `tests/orchestrator/dungeonArmClosed.test.ts` guards rule 11 by asserting the orchestrator takes the fishing lock and not the three dungeon locks — but it did so by matching the literal string `acquireGuardLock(FISHING_GUARD_STATE_PATH)`. Renaming that variable to a profile-resolved `fishingGuardPath` broke the test while the property was untouched, and worse, the inverse holds: someone reopening the dungeon arm could have slipped past the `not.toContain` assertions by renaming a constant. Re-pinned on the FILE NAMES actually locked (`guard-budget-fishing.json`, `guard-budget.json`, `opponent-model.json`, `play-counts.json`). An identifier is a spelling; a file name is the thing that gets locked.
2026-08-20 (session 59, §1) — **The no-hardcoded-paths invariant is ENFORCED for `src/` and the four entry points, and INVENTORIED for the 25 analysis/probe scripts that remain.** The brief asked for a grep that "fails on a hit"; taken literally it fails on ~60 sites, most of them developer tools (`reversalDispersion.ts`, `parseHar.ts`, `chargeTable.ts`, …) that read the author's own corpus by design and that nobody running the bot invokes. Converting them is a large refactor with no portability value. `tests/noHardcodedPaths.test.ts` therefore enforces (a) `src/` grows no NEW path literal, against a 9-file documented allowlist that is itself checked for staleness, and (b) each entry point resolves a profile AND passes an explicit `guardStatePath` — because leaving that undefined makes two profiles silently share one spend ledger, a failure that looks safe and is wrong. The 25 remaining files are counted as a ratchet so the set cannot grow silently. **This is debt, recorded as debt; do not read the passing test as the full invariant being met.**
2026-08-20 (session 59, §3) — **An agent must NOT create or push the distribution repo.** Drafted as a checklist in `handoff/DISTRIBUTION.md` instead. Ship from a FRESH repo with SQUASHED history rather than `git filter-repo`: the working tree is clean (session 54 redacted 2,726 fixture files to 0 raw occurrences) but the git HISTORY still carries the noob token and three handoff documents' identifiers — a deliberate decision that stays fine for a repo nobody is invited to read, and stops being fine the moment friends are pointed at it. A fresh squashed repo sidesteps force-push, stale clones and forks entirely. Three decisions remain the user's alone: whether `config/discovered.json` comes off `.gitignore` (it holds nothing per-account, but removing it edits a rule-3 line), which repo and whether public, and **a licence — there is none, and absent one nobody has permission to use it.**

So the 3x multiplier does **not** miscount — the alternative hypothesis is dead. Run-end accounting then read 345 → 286, `observedDelta: 59`, so 1 energy is credited back somewhere. **The new evidence that reframes it:** one of the same session's five fishing casts drifted identically (committed 12, observed 11; the other four exact). Two different run types, same day, same signature — so this is not "something inside a dungeon run", it is an account-wide event, most plausibly passive regen (18/hr ≈ 1 per 3.3 min) ticking across the measurement window. Both drifts sat on the longest-elapsed measurement of their batch. Six-session question closed; a smaller one replaces it.

Session 51's pre-registered rule fires: VERDICT DROP.** The first 5-cast batch played under session 51's instrumentation produced 7 real `matcherWeight` turns. π: n=7, min 0.130, median 0.138, max **0.255**, 71.4% at or below 0.15, **0% above 0.5**. The rule ("π never exceeds 0.5 → drop the tier") applies as written. **Nothing has been dropped** — that is a strategy change needing a user decision, and it is recorded here so the rule cannot be quietly renegotiated now the numbers are visible. Note the sample is 7 turns and the rule has NO minimum-n clause: adding one *after* seeing a DROP would be exactly the renegotiation pre-registration exists to prevent, so if more data is wanted it must be framed as a new pre-registered rule, not as a caveat on this one. Separately, the replay was **well calibrated** — it predicted median 0.135 / 70.5% below 0.15 against live 0.138 / 71.4%, which is rare evidence FOR the sim while rule 8 has made it blind elsewhere.

Orb rule fired 3/4 with `narrowed: true`, taking the max orb offer each time, run orb sum 88 — and critically did **not** override the one priority family on offer (room 3: AddBurnSword at 20 orbs chosen over AddIntuition at 23). **The accepted cost showed up as predicted and one unpredicted benefit did not:** depth 5 vs the lowest-tier mean of 6.6, but the single clean same-depth pair (run 24893156, also room 5, also juiced, lowest-tier, identical loot 141) scored 3456 against this run's **4224, +22%**. n=1 per arm — suggestive, not established. It was not. Opening spend over the last 20 casts is 1.400 and this batch contributed 3 points across 5 casts, so the preceding 15 average exactly (28−3)/15 = **1.667** — the running opening-spend mean immediately before the batch. Rule 9 says check the claim against the corpus; it does not license declaring a claim invented because the first similar figure found disagrees. 2026-08-20 (session 61, §3) — **QUESTIONS.md §19's pre-registered rule was RENEGOTIATED after its result was visible, deliberately, on the user's call, with the original verdict preserved.** That is the honest sentence and it is the one that goes here. Session 51 pre-registered "pi never exceeds 0.5 on any cast => DROP the matcher tier". Session 60 applied it as written to the first 7 instrumented turns and it returned **DROP**. The user then decided to gather more turns before acting, and session 61 encoded that as a new rule in `matcherVerdict.ts` with a minimum-n clause. This is not "refining the threshold" and must never be described that way — a rule was hit, its answer was seen, and the rule was then changed. What makes it defensible rather than merely convenient: (a) session 51's verdict is preserved verbatim in `SESSION_51_VERDICT` with its full sample, and asserted in the suite; (b) the replacement was written in a session that ran ZERO casts, with fishing paused on the user's oil crafting, so no new data was in hand and none was arriving — it could not be tuned to a result already seen; (c) the new rule ends in BOTH directions, which session 51's did not.
2026-08-20 (session 61, §3) — **N = 32 instrumented matcher TURNS, derived as a SENSITIVITY FLOOR, not as a prediction.** The parametric route was tried first and is rejected in the file: fitting the replay reference's two quantiles (median pi 0.135, P(pi<=0.15)=0.705) gives logit-normal sigma 0.228 -> P(pi>0.5) = 2.2e-16, lognormal sigma 0.196 -> 1.1e-11. Under either, a crossing is ~8 sigma out and NO achievable N could observe one. That extrapolates a far tail from two central quantiles and is not trusted — but it is recorded, because it says the prior strongly favours DROP being correct, so N is about being able to SAY so rather than about expecting a reversal. N is therefore chosen so that 1-(1-p)^N >= 0.80 at **p = 5%**, the assumed rate, stated explicitly: 5% is this repo's OWN floor of measurability (SPEC §4e puts rolled-stat procs at 1-5% and concludes they need hundreds of observations). A DROP at N=32 therefore means "if crossings happen at all they happen on fewer than ~5% of instrumented turns", NOT "pi never crosses". Denominated in TURNS and never converted to casts, because oils shorten casts.
2026-08-20 (session 61, §3) — **The minimum-n clause gates the DROP arm ONLY.** DROP is an absence claim and absence needs power; KEEP and EARNED_BUT_UNPAID are existence claims about pi and fire at any n, because a crossing that happened is not made less real by a small sample. KEEP's second half ("and it hit above base rate") IS a noisy sampled comparison, so the report carries `verdictIsPowered` and a KEEP below N must be read as unpowered on that half.
2026-08-20 (session 61, §4e) — **The 60% fishing target is DROPPED.** 60% was chosen because 3-of-5 is an observable value at batch size 5 (n=5 can only read 0/20/40/60/80/100%), not because 60% meant anything about this fishery. It never got its read: 24 post-fix casts against the ~93 needed for +/-10pp. Oils change the spending policy underneath it, so carrying it forward would mean measuring a target chosen for one policy against a different one. A replacement target gets derived once the oil policy's actual range is visible — by the USER, after oil casts exist, not by an agent.
2026-08-20 (session 61, §4e) — **The 15-cast zero-catch tripwire SURVIVES the 60% drop and is now CODE.** `src/strategy/fishing/zeroStreak.ts`. It was prose for many sessions and that is exactly what it bought: the dead era ran 40 casts before a human noticed, so the rule whose justification is "it would have caught the dead era early" is a rule that did not fire, because nothing computed it. "Armed" cannot mean "still written down". It spans the oil boundary deliberately — a streak does not reset because the spending policy changed, since the transition is precisely when a safeguard earns its keep. This is the ONE outcome metric §4b's oil split does not apply to, because it is a SAFETY metric and per-arm streaks would halve its sensitivity at the worst moment.
2026-08-20 (session 61, §4b) — **The oil-era flag is DERIVED from the capture, not written by the live loop.** `consumablesUsed` and `fishingConsumableSlotUsed[3]` are on every captured board state already, so `FishingCast.oilEra` reads them instead of adding a field the live loop must remember to set. It cannot be forgotten, it applies retroactively, and it is a flag rather than a deletion so an excluded cast can be reconsidered. **It immediately found a cast the brief said could not exist**: `12975152` (2026-08-19) carries `consumablesUsed: 1` with slot 0 marked on its FIRST captured state — a consumable spent at or before cast start by someone other than this bot. So "the corpus contains zero oil casts" is false; it contains one, with the item unidentifiable (both oil-boost percents read 0, ruling out only Fintuition and Dual Yield). A written flag would have started at zero and never found it. Item IDENTITY is recorded separately at spend time on `RingPredictionRecord.oilItemIdsUsed`, since the board state names no item.
2026-08-20 (session 61, §4c) — **Oils are spent AUTONOMOUSLY within `config/bot.json`'s `dendren.oils` budget, but `policyApproved` gates the TIMING separately and ships FALSE.** User decision: oils mirror `forbiddenWoods.potions` — the user sets the count, the bot spends inside it and stops; fishing does not become per-cast approval. CLAUDE.md's "Ask first" list gains an explicit line saying so, because "Sell, burn, or list any item" could reasonably be read as covering consumption of a hand-crafted item and a careful agent would block on it. The third config field has no potions analogue and is the important one: authorising the BUDGET is not authorising the TIMING (rule 4). This is also what stops `liveFishing.ts`'s Relaxing-Oil call site — LIVE since session 44, inert only because the balance has always been 0 — from firing by itself the moment crafting finishes. `mayConsumeOil` takes every condition as a REQUIRED field so a caller cannot gate on fewer, which is the structural fix for `resolvePotionLoadout`'s bug (that function was deleted in session 54; the lesson survives it, the referent does not).
2026-08-20 (session 61, §4d) — **The sim CANNOT score the turn-cost branch, and that is the reported result.** The brief asked for the oil timing policy derived twice, once assuming consumption is free and once assuming it costs a turn. Both were run; the second is not a model of a turn cost. Turns are not scarce in this fishery (`maxTurns` 40, mean turns 2.95, `stalled` 1 in 8000); what is scarce is MANA (`escaped_mana` 2003/8000) and MISSES (`escaped_meter` 499/8000, a miss pushes fishHp back toward fishMaxHp). A consume turn plays no card, so it spends no mana, takes no miss, and hands the matcher a free observation — a BENEFIT. Visible directly: `start` at costsTurn=true cuts escaped_mana 868->270 and raises its catch rate 74%->93%. An added cost that improves the outcome is an artifact. **This also inverts the brief's framing of the mechanic**: the brief reasoned a turn-costing +2 is a net loss whenever an ordinary attack deals more than 2, which assumes the forgone turn was a free guaranteed attack — it is not, it costs mana and risks a miss that undoes damage. The load-bearing open question for the first live oil cast is therefore whether `use_fishing_item` costs MANA, not whether it costs a turn.
2026-08-20 (session 61, §4d) — **RECOMMENDED oil policy: `on-demand` — Relaxing Oil only when LETHAL, Focus Oil only at meter zero. AWAITING USER APPROVAL, nothing consumed.** `handoff/OIL-POLICY.md`. +19.40pp vs the never-oil control at n=8000 paired, robust at effect amounts 1/2/3 within the branch the sim can model. The causal story matters more than the ranking: the Focus Oil carries almost all of it (+17.74pp alone vs the lethal trigger's +4.47pp, near-additive) because the focus meter never regenerates within a cast, so at zero the policy is frozen onto one cell and +2 is the difference between still being able to aim and not. The lethal trigger is worth its smaller share for a reason its delta does not show: it ends the cast, so it is PROVABLY indifferent to the unresolved turn-cost mechanic. `start` loses honestly (16000 oils for +5.66pp, 0.028pp each). **Session 43's shipped heuristic (c) is DOMINATED and should be replaced**: +4.51pp for 2630 oils against the lethal trigger's +4.47pp for 1821 — indistinguishable benefit, 44% more oil.

Rationale: it is the shape in which rule 8's flip goes silently inert (session 56 found ROOM_NUM_CID had moved, and `liveRun.ts` defaults an unreadable room to 0, which takes the LOWEST tier every room and comes back looking exactly like the fifty lowest-tier runs before it). `maxRoom` cannot be the unreadable half, so an unreadable rule always means the ROOM number moved. The legitimate alternative reading needs room 16 and the deepest run ever is room 10. Halting can cost 3 run-units and that is the accepted trade: a run that silently took the lowest tier is worth LESS than no run at all, because it looks like evidence and is not.

2026-08-21 (session 63, §2) — **`onEnemyWinExchange_corrode` is MODELLED in the combat core, and it was CONFIRMED against the corpus before it was implemented rather than after.** `corrodeOnEnemyWin` (`src/sim/enemyBuffs.ts`) reduces the player's `armorMax` (the wire's `shield.currentMax`) when the enemy wins an exchange with the buff's own declared move; `resolveExchange` applies it and reports it as `ExchangeResult.corroded`. **The natural experiment**, found by scanning every consecutive same-room state pair in `fixtures/dungeon-runs/` for a `currentMax` decrease: foe-won × move-matches = 4 exchanges, all −3; foe-won × no-match = 19, all 0; foe-lost × match = 8, all 0; neither = 25, all 0. **So BOTH gates are measured, not declared** — the move gate by the 19, the win gate by the 8. 2026-08-21 (session 63, §2) — **A state boundary is NOT an exchange boundary, and reading deltas across one manufactures counter-examples.** Scanning for corrode initially put five spurious zero-deltas in the firing cell, which looked like the model failing 4-of-9. Dedupe on the `(myLastMove, foeLastMove)` pair before reading any per-exchange delta. This is session 56's cross-attempt trap in a different costume: there the fix was delimiting attempts on `ROOM_NUM_CID` decreasing, here it is delimiting exchanges on the move pair changing, and in both cases the naive per-file join produced confident garbage.
