2026-08-21 (session 65, §1) — **`slotIndex` IS A CURSOR OVER THE SERVER'S THREE-SLOT LEDGER, NOT A CONSTANT — and the hard-coded `0` survived from session 44 to session 65 only because no cast had ever wanted a second oil.** Cast 13019682 consumed a Focus Oil at slot 0, hit `focusMeter: 0` again two turns later, sent a second consume at slot 0 against `fishingConsumableSlotUsed [T,F,F]`, and got HTTP 400. `nextConsumableSlot` (`scripts/liveFishing.ts`) now reads the live ledger and fails closed in BOTH directions: an absent field returns `null` rather than guessing 0, and a full ledger returns `null` rather than wrapping onto a used slot. `fishingConsumableSlotUsed` and `consumablesUsed` were promoted out of the schema's `passthrough()` because the loop now READS them rather than merely recording them. CONFIRMED after the fix, and by THE SAME CAST: 13019682 was abandoned mid-play by the token desync the rejection caused, persisted server-side, was resumed by the next invocation against fixed code, and went on to consume THREE oils in total walking slots 0, 1, 2 — one before the bug bit and two after the fix. **The general lesson: a constant that is correct for the first occurrence of a thing is not evidence about the second, and "confirmed live" on n=1 of a repeatable action means confirmed for the first one only.**
2026-08-21 (session 65, §1) — **A REJECTED `use_fishing_item` ADVANCES THE SERVER'S ACTION TOKEN, so it is NOT a free optional action.** The call site's comment claimed a wrong slot "fails closed via the catch block, not a GuardTrip: this action is an optional rescue, not a required step in playing the cast." Live play falsified it: after the HTTP 400 above, the next `play_cards` died on `Invalid action token 1787330936730 != 1787330937735`. `postFishingAction` throws before assigning the response's token, so the client never learns the advanced value, and the failure surfaces ONE TURN AWAY from its cause. **There is no resync — `GET /fishing/state` carries no `actionToken`** — so the loop now fails closed AT the rejected consume, naming it. **Not destructive:** the cast persists server-side and the next invocation RESUMES it with no `start_run`, no energy and no ledger entry — the abandoned cast is the one that went on to consume three oils and catch. This is the general shape of session 63's `shield.currentMax` and session 64's ITEM_MESSAGE traps: a failed or repeated response that looks inert but has already moved server state.
2026-08-21 (session 65, §3) — **THE OIL TURN COST IS NO LONGER SWEPT. `MEASURED_CONSUME_COSTS_TURN = false` is the single home for the answer, and `oilTimingSweep.ts` reports only that arm by default** (`--include-artifact-branch` restores the other, which is retained as the evidence for WHY it is an artifact, not as an alternative answer). **A CORRECTION TO THE SESSION-65 BRIEF, which requested this work on a false premise:** the brief said the +19.40pp headline "was computed across both arms and should not be quoted again until it has been recomputed." It was not. `handoff/OIL-POLICY.md`'s headline table is labelled `costsTurn=false, amount=2, n=8000`, and `main()` has always judged robustness on the `costsTurn=false` rows only, printing "ARTIFACT BRANCH" over the others. **The recommendation never depended on the unresolved mechanic**, so resolving it invalidates nothing and corrects no published number. The re-run reproduces the published table EXACTLY — on-demand +19.40pp [+19.39, +19.41], robust at amounts 1/2/3 (+13.89 / +19.40 / +21.99) — so **the +19.40pp figure is CONFIRMED and may be quoted again.** What was genuinely overdue is narrower: stop sweeping a resolved parameter, because a sweep that keeps sweeping one re-opens a settled question every time it runs. CLAUDE.md rule 9, fifth occurrence.
2026-08-21 (session 65, §1c) — **PARTIAL DRY (one oil exhausted, the other plentiful) is handled, tested BEFORE it could occur live, and then occurred live the same session.** Two silent failure modes, both pinned by `tests/fishing/oilPartialDry.test.ts`: (1) `onDemandTriggers` returns `["relaxing","focus"]` — Relaxing FIRST — so a loop that `break`s on a refusal instead of `continue`ing lets a DRY Relaxing trigger suppress a fully funded Focus consume, a bug that hides behind whichever oil is scarce; (2) `stock_dry` is an `&&`, and an `||` there would end a seven-cast batch the moment the scarce oil ran out with 22 of the other still held. Demonstrated by breaking each branch (2 tests fail, then 3), then restoring. **It then ran for real**: the Relaxing Oil was spent on cast 1, and casts 2-7 executed partial dry — recording OIL-POLICY-DRY for Relaxing while Focus consumed normally and the batch did not halt. This is the answer to session 64's "a component that is shipped, gated, tested and inert because nothing hands it its dependency": the fix is to test the state BEFORE the first thing that will execute it, not after.
2026-08-21 (session 65, §1b) — **A batch's INTENDED EXIT is a shape, not a constant, and the two shapes are named rather than retuned.** Session 64's batch existed to see whether an oil could be consumed at all, so the first consume ENDED it (`stopOnOilConsume: true`). Session 65's existed to accumulate instrumented turns, so a consume is a CAPTURE and `castCap: 7` is the exit. `SESSION_64_LIMITS` stays exported and tested so its halts stay demonstrable. `stock_dry` is gated on `stopOnOilConsume` because that halt's entire justification is that the consume exit can no longer be met — a batch whose objective is turns still gets every turn out of a dry cast. **§2c is NOT retired:** `cleanCastCap: null` suppresses the HALT, not the pre-registration, and `main()` prints the clean-cast tally against the threshold at batch end so "reports without halting" is enforced rather than claimed.

2026-08-21 (session 66, §1) — **THE `nextPosition` OVERRIDE STAYS ARMED AND GAINS A FIRST-MISS TRIPWIRE THAT CAN ACTUALLY FIRE.** User decision 2026-08-21, taken because the override became a live input to card choice in session 65 without a decision being taken (12 validation entries, 12 hits, 9 casts, ~1-2% of turns). **The Wilson gate could never have protected anything, and that is a structural fact about the gate, not a threshold that needs tuning:** a lower bound computed from an unbroken streak ONLY EVER CLIMBS (12/12 ≈ 0.76, 20/20 ≈ 0.84, 50/50 ≈ 0.93), so there is no value the streak can reach that lowers it, and the moment the override stops behaving is the moment nobody is watching a number that has spent its whole history going up. The fix is an EVENT THAT CAN OCCUR. **Three prediction cases, distinguished in code (`classifyPredictionOutcome`, `src/strategy/fishing/nextPositionArm.ts`) and in the record (`NextPositionValidation.overrideActive`):** `absent` — no `nextPosition` volunteered, ~98-99% of turns, cannot trip; `not_acted` — present but it did not steer the card choice, logged and NEVER trips, because disarming over a decision the bot did not take retires a safeguard on a counterfactual; `acted_hit`/`acted_miss` — present AND steered, and `acted_miss` is the only case that disarms. Conflating "no prediction" with "prediction correct" is how 12/12 becomes 12/12 forever. **The disarm is a VETO over the Wilson gate, not a term in it** — folded into hits/attempts one miss is swamped by the next handful of hits and the override re-arms itself inside the same batch, which is the "a safeguard that resets is a log line" failure; pinned at 200/200 + a disarm, where the bound stays above 0.97 and `ready` is false. **Nothing re-arms automatically:** the module exports no re-arm function, a test pins that absence, and a human deletes `data/nextPositionOverrideDisarm.json`. Fails closed both ways (missing file = ARMED, the normal state; unreadable or wrong-shape = DISARMED) and is write-once so the FIRST miss stays the recorded one. `overrideActive` is optional and ABSENT MEANS UNKNOWN, never "acted on" — the 12 rows written before the field existed cannot be re-attributed.

2026-08-21 (session 66, §3) — **THE "STRUCTURAL, NOT SAMPLING NOISE" READING OF THE 14-CAST FOCUS GAP IS WITHDRAWN, AND THE MEMBERSHIP QUESTION IS ANSWERED.** The session-65 recap said the lax-vs-strict Focus gap being "STILL exactly 14 across three independent batches" showed a structural feature of how casts end. Two independent problems. **(1) The count is not evidence.** Seven casts were added between the readings; at the observed rate 14/102 = 13.7% they were expected to add ~0.96 members and adding ZERO has probability 0.863^7 = **0.36**. A one-in-three outcome is the most ordinary thing that could have happened. **(2) The "same 14 casts" is arithmetic, not a finding.** Gap membership is a PER-CAST property (`castReachability` reads one cast and nothing else) and the corpus only grows, so gap(109) is necessarily a superset of gap(102) — equal counts FORCE equal membership, and observing it teaches nothing. The ids are recorded anyway (12923189 12942026 12942155 12944922 12945306 12945313 12956660 12956727 12957029 12957061 12957129 12975708 12975724 12991326; none among the seven newest). **The property they share is real, exactly characterised, and is clause 2 restated rather than a fact about the meter:** a CAUGHT cast can never be in the gap (terminal `fishHp: 0` fails clause 1 — 23 caught, 0 in the gap, structurally), and the gap is NOT "escaped with an empty terminal meter", which is 66 casts, of which 52 had ALREADY hit zero with a turn still to play and are strict-reachable too. 66 − 52 = 14: the gap is precisely the casts whose meter emptied for the FIRST AND ONLY time on the state that ended the cast, which is exactly the case the "with a turn remaining" clause was invented to exclude. `npx tsx scripts/oilReachability.ts --gap`. CLAUDE.md rule 10's neighbour: a number that has not moved across two readings is not thereby a constant.

2026-08-21 (session 66, §2) — **HOLDING ZERO MID RELAXING OIL (937) COSTS AN EXPECTED +1.83pp OF CATCH RATE, 95% Wilson [0.5pp, 6.4pp] — about SIX OILS PER EXTRA FISH.** `handoff/reports/session-66-relaxing-cost.md`, `npx tsx scripts/oilReachability.ts --relaxing-cost`. **The finding is not the 2, it is the 10:** the lethal trigger is reachable on 12 of 109 casts (11.0%, 14 decision points) and **ten of those twelve were CAUGHT ANYWAY**, so only two casts could ever have been converted. The trigger fires at `fishHp <= 2` with a turn still to play, and a fish that low usually dies to the next card, which deals far more than the oil's 2 — so the lethal trigger is a proxy for "this cast is nearly over", not for "this cast is about to escape", and five of six oils spent on it buy nothing. At the 20-cast daily cap that is ~2.2 oils/day for ~0.37 extra fish/day (95%: 0.10–1.29). **The sim's `lethal-relaxing-only` +4.47pp must NOT be quoted as this cost.** Per oil the two sources agree closely (0.196 sim vs 0.167 corpus); the headline differs 2.4x purely because the sim reaches the lethal band on 22.8% of casts against this corpus's 11.0%. **EXPECTED, not observed** — the numerator is two casts (12975713, 12991353), and the single live Relaxing consume on record (session 65, `fishHp 1 -> 0`, CAUGHT) confirms the MECHANISM while calibrating no rate. Focus is where the value is: reachable in 55.0% of casts, +17.74pp of the policy's +19.40pp, stock 18.

2026-08-21 (session 66, §4) — **§19's BATCH SHAPE IS HISTORY, NOT A STANDING AUTHORIZATION.** §19 closed in session 65 as a POWERED KEEP at n=35 of 32, so the objective `SESSION_65_LIMITS` was chosen for — accumulating instrumented matcher turns — no longer exists. The constant stays exported, tested and numerically unchanged (same reason `SESSION_64_LIMITS` does: a shape is history, not a setting), but its RATIONALE is retired in both its own doc comment and at `main()`'s `batchLimits` line. **A future batch that wants seven casts must state what they are for in its own brief.** Do not budget casts for §19 and do not report turn accrual; if a brief asks for §19 turns, this entry is the answer.

2026-08-21 (session 67) — **OILS ARE A BACKUP, NOT A ROUTINE SPEND. User directive.** *"Keep crafting, but use oils only on an as-needed basis. If the autofisher believes it can catch the fish without oil, don't use the oil — conserve inventory for future casts. The priority is to use mana to get the fish as close as possible to caught, with the oils as a backup to guarantee a catch if need be."* Derived and scored offline; **NOT SHIPPED** — `liveFishing.ts` still plays `onDemandTriggers` and `dendren.oils.policyApproved` still ships false. The recommendation is `conservingOil({relaxing: 1, focus: 1})` in `src/strategy/fishing/oilTiming.ts`: on-demand's two triggers unchanged, each with a necessity condition — skip the oil when an affordable card already achieves the outcome with CERTAINTY. Write-up and every number: `handoff/OIL-CONSERVE.md`.

2026-08-21 (session 67) — **THE NECESSITY THRESHOLD IS NOT A FITTED PARAMETER, AND MUST NOT BE "TUNED" LATER.** Both quantities the gates read are BIMODAL at the moments the triggers fire: `bestKillProbability` is 34.3% exactly 0 / 55.8% exactly 1 / 9.9% strictly between; `bestConnectProbability` is 59.8% / 27.8% / 12.5%. So every threshold from 0.25 to 1 lands on one plateau (catch 88.29–88.46%, 2.18–2.42 oils per extra fish), and the best tuned pair buys ~0.08pp over the zero-parameter value of 1 — on a simulator whose control arm catches 68.71% against the real fishery's 25.9%. **A constant that cannot be distinguished from 1 on the sim that fitted it is not worth shipping.** `tests/fishing/oilNecessity.test.ts` pins the value at exactly 1 so a retune has to argue against this paragraph rather than around it.

2026-08-21 (session 67) — **"SWITCH TO FOCUS-ONLY" WAS THE WRONG ANSWER, and the free re-rank alone would have given it.** Under oils-per-extra-fish, `focus-when-empty-only` (2.48) does beat the shipped `on-demand` (3.59), and the Relaxing trigger costs **15.51 oils per extra fish at the margin**. But `conserve(r=1,f=1)` scores 88.38% / +19.66pp / 3809 oils = **2.42**, beating both on both axes, and it dominates `on-demand` at every stock level in the 20-cast finite-stock day. Switching to focus-only would have discarded 1.9pp of catch rate for nothing. **The lesson is about the method, not the oils: re-ranking an existing menu under a new objective answers "which of these" and never "is a better one available".**

2026-08-21 (session 67) — **THE SIX `fakeDoc` COPIES ARE ONE BUILDER, and the guard is the deliverable, not the consolidation.** `tests/helpers/fishingDoc.ts` is the single builder; `LIVE_PATH_FIELDS` names what the live path reads and `tests/fishing/fishingDocGuard.test.ts` requires removing each one to be OBSERVABLE in a real `runOneCast`. A `toContain`-style assertion on the key set was rejected as worthless — it passes for a field nothing reads. **Do not loosen the observability assertion.** If a field ever comes back unobservable the answer is to widen the observable or to drop the field from the list deliberately, in a commit that says so. That is not hypothetical: `fishMaxHp` and `focusMeterMax` failed on the first run, because under `onDemandTriggers` neither enters a decision — they are read only to be PRINTED. The observable was widened to include console output; the list was not shortened.

2026-08-21 (session 67) — **THE SHIPS LIST WAS MISSING `vitest.config.ts` AND `package-lock.json`; both are now on it.** Found by rehearsing DISTRIBUTION.md steps 5 and 6 locally. Also settled: `CODEXAUDIT`/`CODEXIMPROVE`/`CODEXREVIEW`/`config/.gitkeep`/`.claude/` do not ship — they were on neither list and the rehearsal had to decide.

2026-08-21 (session 67) — **THE BOT IS PORTABLE; ITS TEST SUITE IS NOT. Four shipped test files assert against unshipped data.** `rejectionAudit.test.ts` (`logs/`), `redact.test.ts` (`handoff/`), `matcherVerdict.test.ts` (`data/ringPrediction.jsonl`), `reversalDispersion.test.ts` (`data/fish-patterns.jsonl`). A friend's first `npx vitest run` is red, and `rejectionAudit.test.ts` throws at COLLECTION so it contributes **0 tests instead of 11** — a silently vanishing count, visible only by diffing reporter output. **None of these is a ships-list bug**: every path is correctly excluded. `fixtures/` was NOT trimmed, which is the thing step 6 existed to catch. Not fixed in session 67 — the fix is judgement about what a stranger's suite should assert (skip-guards, not loosened assertions), and the brief scoped the session to finding them. `handoff/reports/session-67-distribution-rehearsal.md`.

2026-08-21 (session 68, §1) — **The oil shadow is INERT BY CONSTRUCTION, and the guarantee is a byte-identity test with three anti-vacuity companions, not a comment.** `snapshotOilDecision` hands the gate a frozen DEEP COPY (the copy is what gets frozen — freezing the live `deckCardData` in place would itself be a side effect on the live path, which is the trap the ordering avoids); `evaluateOilShadow` cannot throw; the record is inert by type. Byte-identity alone is easy to pass while proving nothing, so three tests close the vacuity routes: the shadow really ran (records with a real probability, no swallowed throw), it really DISAGREES with the live policy on the test cast (so a leak must surface as a missing POST), and the comparator really can fail. Demonstrated failing with the shadow permitted to influence the decision — one test red, the right one, `oilsConsumed` 2 → 1 — then restored.

2026-08-21 (session 68, §1) — **`NECESSITY_EPSILON = 1e-9` is a FLOATING-POINT FIX, not a threshold tune, and CLAUDE.md's "do not tune the necessity thresholds" is untouched.** Both gate inputs are sums of probability mass, so a genuinely certain outcome arrives as `0.9999999999999999` whenever summation order does not cancel; under a bare `>=` against the threshold of `1` the gate FIRED on a certain kill — the exact decision its own thesis forbids — and its behaviour was decided by float representation. `RECOMMENDED_NECESSITY_THRESHOLDS` is still `{1, 1}`, both degenerate endpoints still degenerate, and session 67's sweep reproduces BYTE-FOR-BYTE at n=8000 (88.38% / 3809 oils / 2.42 per extra fish; buckets 34.3% at 0, 55.8% at 1). The sim's distributions sum exactly, so the defect is LIVE-ONLY — which is precisely the class of thing shadow mode exists to surface.

2026-08-21 (session 68, §1b) — **The Relaxing arm of the necessity gate cannot be observed in shadow at all, and the reason is ORDERING, not stock.** The session-68 brief said the arm was unexercisable because stock was Relaxing 0; live stock was Relaxing 56 / Focus 19, so that reason was simply wrong. The real cause is structural: the shadow evaluates in the card-choice phase (it needs `dist`), the Relaxing trigger fires only on a lethal fish, and a lethal Relaxing Oil ends the cast inside the oil block before that phase is reached. Measured over five casts: 13 shadow records, exactly ONE at a firing moment (Focus arm, `bestConnect` 0.074, gate agreed), `bestKillProbability` null on all 13, 0 sanity violations, 0 throws. **The gate must not be reported as validated live.** Fixable by hoisting the `dist` pipeline above the oil block — `dist` depends only on `matcher.history`, `pendingPrediction` and the mined tables, none of which a consume changes — but that is a live-loop restructure and belongs in a brief.

2026-08-21 (session 68, §2) — **A lethal Relaxing Oil ends the cast, so the Focus Oil that triggered on the same turn must not be sent.** LIVE-FOUND and it cost a cast: `onDemandTriggers` is evaluated once per turn and can return BOTH oils, and `use_fishing_item(942, slot 1)` went to a cast the server had already completed → HTTP 400 → the token desync of session 65 → unrecoverable. The pre-existing `if (doc.COMPLETE_CID) continue;` could never catch it because it sat AFTER the loop; the damage is done by the loop's own second iteration. Pinned by `tests/fishing/oilLethalCompletes.test.ts`, whose mock REJECTS post-completion actions — without that the test passes on the unfixed code, because a mock that does not care is not the server.

2026-08-21 (session 68, §2) — **A pending `cardsToAdd` offer is resolved BEFORE `start_run`, not only at the end of a cast.** The end-of-cast `loot` block carried a comment claiming the bot's own catches never leave the account stuck; that was only ever true of casts that reach the end. A guard trip taken between the catch and the resolution left the account rejecting every `start_run` with "Player is already in a game". `resolvePendingCardOffer` is now shared and called from both places, so recovery is automatic after ANY abort path — verified live (`loot sent — cardChosenId 34`, then a clean `start_run`).

2026-08-21 (session 68) — **`CRIT_HIT` can come from an equipped LURE and owes nothing to the card's `critZones`.** Live, cast 13022874 turn 4: `CRIT_HIT` on card 76 (`critZones []`, `critEffects []`, `hitEffects 3`) took the fish 5 → 0. User-stated the same day: a "Steady Lure" is equipped, 3% crit chance. `cardChoice.ts` models only the CARD crit source, so it now describes one of two sources in play. **The crit DAMAGE rule stays OPEN at n=1** — `hit + 2`, a flat 5, and "lethal, and the server reports remaining HP" (also exactly 5) all fit; do not encode one. **Do not compute a crit rate over the whole corpus**: 1 in 484 plays spans ~60 sessions and the lure's equip date is unknown, which is CLAUDE.md rule 10's trap in another costume.

2026-08-21 (session 68) — **Two `castTrace.ts` audit bugs, both exposed by the first CRIT_HIT and the first oil-killed fish, both fixed; the LIVE path was never affected.** `CRIT_HIT` was not counted as a hit (only `type === "HIT"` was), so the crit scored as a MISS in every offline audit; and terminal events arriving on a `use_fishing_item` response were dropped WITH the response, so a fish killed by a lethal Relaxing Oil was never marked caught in a trace (23 traces vs the corpus's 26 — it read 22 vs 23 before this batch and the one-cast gap was wrongly attributed to the known incomplete cast). Both views now say 26, and that reconciliation is the evidence. `liveFishing.ts` derives `realizedHit` from the HP delta, so `data/ringPrediction.jsonl` and §19's verdict never saw either bug.

2026-08-21 (session 68) — **"A caught cast can never be in the gap" is FALSIFIED, by cast 13022748.** The derivation was sound and its premise was dated: it assumed the only state the lax reading ADDS is the terminal one, which holds only when a cast ends on a CARD. A lethal Relaxing Oil ends it without a following `play_cards`, so the added state is the PRE-oil one — fish alive at 2 HP, meter 0, no turn remaining. The oil era gave casts a new way to end and this is the first structural claim it has broken. Related: the focus gap moved 14 → 15, which is session 66's withdrawal of the "stable structural feature" reading being confirmed rather than contradicted.

2026-08-21 (session 68, §3) — **Author-data tests get a LOUD skip and program-logic tests always run; the probe must never execute at module or describe scope.** `tests/helpers/authorData.ts` is the one way to declare the distinction. A file that throws while being COLLECTED cannot report that it was skipped — it reports nothing, and `rejectionAudit.test.ts` was contributing 0 tests instead of 11 for exactly that reason. Demonstrated with only the load location differing: old shape → `Tests no tests`; new shape → `3 passed | 8 skipped (11)` plus a stderr banner. §19's closed rule now also has a SYNTHETIC powered-KEEP pin so it ships with the code it guards.

2026-08-21 (session 68, §4) — **`npx tsx scripts/preflight.ts` is DISTRIBUTION step 5, and it must be run before every invite.** It exports the ships list, runs `doctor.ts` under a HOME with no `~/.secrets`, runs the suite, and secret-scans THE EXPORT rather than the working tree. It never creates a repo, adds a remote, or pushes — steps 3, 4 and 6 remain the user's by standing decision. First passing run: 1 ✗ (the JWT), `1279 passed | 13 skipped (1292)`, secret scan clean, which closes session 67's "its test suite is not portable".

2026-08-21 (session 68, §4b) — **`ls fixtures/fishing-casts/live | wc -l` IS NOT THE CAST COUNT, and there is no missing cast.** The session-67 brief asked which of "110 directories vs 109 casts" fails to load; nothing does. A directory is created per INVOCATION of `liveFishing.ts`, not per cast (7 hold more than one, one holds six), and every run that starts no cast — every `--dry-run` — still creates one containing only `raw` (23 of them). 118 dirs, 95 with state files, 114 distinct docIds, and 114 is what the corpus loads. Pinned as RELATIONS in `fishingCorpus.test.ts`, never as literals, so the question cannot be asked a third time.

2026-08-21 (session 69, §1) — **A SHADOW EVALUATED IN THE WRONG PHASE OF A TURN IS BLIND TO EXACTLY THE DECISIONS THAT END THE TURN, and it reports that blindness as an ordinary run of quiet records.** Session 68's oil shadow produced 13 records over five live casts with 0 sanity violations and 0 throws, and exactly ONE at a firing moment — because it was evaluated in the card-choice phase (that is where `dist` first existed), the Relaxing trigger fires only on a lethal fish, and a lethal Relaxing Oil ENDS the cast inside the oil block. The one turn the arm was observable on was the one turn no record was written. **Nothing about "13 records, 0 violations" says half the gate was never asked; only counting FIRING MOMENTS did.** The fix is the `dist` pipeline hoisted above the oil block, with the shadow evaluated there — `dist` reads `matcher.history`, `pendingPrediction`, `switchEstimate` and the mined tables and nothing off the doc, while the block writes only `doc`, `oilHeld` and its own tallies. `hand`/`mana`/`fishHp` deliberately did NOT move: they are read off the POST-consume doc and belong to the card policy. **The argument is not the guarantee, and this repo has shipped two restructures justified as "only moving a computation" (sessions 64, 65).** `tests/fishing/hoistInvariant.test.ts` captures the full POST sequence and result across six scenarios into a golden GENERATED BEFORE THE HOIST and committed with it; rewriting it needs `UPDATE_LIVE_DECISION_GOLDEN=1`, so a future restructure that changes play fails there instead of re-blessing itself. The bodies carry the chosen card index and focusPoint, which is what `dist` drives. `tests/fishing/oilShadowRelaxingArm.test.ts` is the other half — demonstrated failing 3-of-6 on the pre-hoist placement, where the record simply did not exist.

2026-08-21 (session 69, §2b) — **THE USER'S CAST IS 13022748, AND NO CARD COULD HAVE KILLED WITH CERTAINTY — BUT ONE CAME WITHIN 2.5 PERCENTAGE POINTS.** At the consume: fish 2/18, player 7/10, **focus meter 0/3 frozen at [4,3]** so exactly ONE placement was available, draw pile 4, hand [6, 75, 4]. All three cards were lethal on DAMAGE (weakest deals 5 against 2 HP); the constraint was CONNECTING. `bestKillProbability` **0.975217** (card 75, covering 3 of 16 cells). **So `conserve(r=1,f=1)` — the certainty gate already shadowed — would have spent this oil too, and the user's concern lands on the probabilistic band, not on the certainty rule.** Two facts the brief did not have: the bot had cards but no ability to AIM, having spent the meter on turn 2 to set up the shot that took the fish 7 → 2; and a miss heals the fish by 3-6, lifting it clear of the oil's 2 damage, so **a miss destroys the oil's lethality for the rest of the cast** — holding it saves it for a later CAST, not for later in this one. `scripts/oilMomentAudit.ts` (new, profile-resolved) rebuilds the board and the live distribution at every consume in `logs/`; it excludes the audited cast's own corpus rows by default, because "could it have known" must not be answered with evidence the cast itself produced.

2026-08-21 (session 69, §3) — **THE PROBABILISTIC THRESHOLD IS `1 - v` WHERE `v` IS A MEASURED EXCHANGE RATE, AND THE SIMULATOR CANNOT SEE IT AT ALL.** Spending a lethal-band oil gains `(1 - p)` fish and costs one oil worth `v` fish, so hold when `p >= 1 - v`. Session 66 measured `v` from the corpus at ~6 oils per extra fish, giving `holdThresholdFromExchangeRate(6)` = **0.8333**, interval **[0.33, 0.95]** from the measurement's own 1.5-20 — and the interval must travel with the point estimate, whose numerator is TWO casts. PRE-REGISTERED as `PREREGISTERED_EXCHANGE_THRESHOLDS` before the session-69 batch. **Nothing was swept**, which is how this coexists with CLAUDE.md's standing "do not tune the necessity thresholds": the only way to argue with it is to argue with the measurement. **THE NEGATIVE RESULT IS THE IMPORTANT HALF:** at n=8000/arm the derived threshold is byte-identical to `conserve{1,1}` (3809 oils, 88.38%) because the sim puts NO `bestKillProbability` mass in [0.833, 1) — while the four Relaxing firings on the entire live record read 0.400, 0.580, 0.587, 0.975, **not one exactly 0 or exactly 1**, so the sim's measured bimodality (34.3%/55.8%/9.9%) does not reproduce live. `oilExchangeRate.test.ts` pins the absence of a sim saving so a report cannot invent one, pins that the dial is nevertheless not inert (3618 oils at the interval's low end), and pins both degeneracies at STATE level — an aggregate over a distribution with a hole in it proves nothing about the hole. **The FOCUS arm stays at 1 and that is a finding:** the derivation prices an oil converting an uncertain catch into a certain one, a Focus Oil restores meter, and no corpus-measured exchange rate exists for its trigger — borrowing the Relaxing number would be the fitted constant the construction exists to avoid. **`p` is a proxy that ERRS TOWARD SPENDING:** `bestKillProbability` is this-turn, the derivation wants eventually, so the gate holds LESS often than the rate says. NOT SHIPPED.

2026-08-21 (session 69, §4) — **RELAXING CAPPED AT 2 PER CAST, FOCUS UNCONSTRAINED — AND "unconstrained" IS EXPRESSED BY SILENCE.** User directive 2026-08-21: *"Continue using Focus oil until supply naturally depletes, then only use 2x Relaxing oil per fishing run."* `OilBudgetConfig.perItemMaxPerCast` is keyed by item id; **942 is ABSENT from the map** rather than given a generous number that would read as a limit somebody later tidies. **"per fishing run" is read as PER CAST because `start_run` is the server's own action for starting one cast** — the directive's word is the API's word; a per-SESSION reading would be strictly tighter and is not what shipped. A per-item number can never exceed `maxPerCast`, which is floored over it. **A cap hit is the THIRD CAST STATE, not an ordinary refusal:** the policy wanted the oil and the account HELD one, so the cast was played by the oil policy running dry on that arm and is flagged OIL-POLICY-DRY with the new reason `per_cast_cap` — deliberately distinct from `empty`, because reading a user ceiling as an empty bag would understate the account's holdings in exactly the reports that track them. The cast continues and the batch does not halt. **A ceiling never causes a spend**, which is why applying it while Focus stock is healthy is safe rather than premature — pinned, along with the shipped `config/bot.json` really carrying it, because a test of the code alone passes with the directive unconfigured.

2026-08-21 (session 69, §5) — **THE CRIT RATE IS 1/73 = 1.37% ON A DATABLE DENOMINATOR, CONSISTENT WITH THE STATED 3%.** *User-stated 2026-08-21: the Steady Lure was equipped BEFORE that day's casts* — which is what makes a rate computable at all, and session 68 was right to refuse to divide 1 crit by 484 plays spanning ~60 sessions of unknown lure state. Scoped to 2026-08-21: **1/73 all plays = 1.37%, 95% Wilson [0.24%, 7.36%]**; **1/39 connecting plays = 2.56%, [0.45%, 13.18%]**. Both contain 3%, so the anomaly needs no explanation beyond the lure. **BOTH denominators are reported because n=1 cannot choose between them** — "3% crit chance" could mean 3% of plays or 3% of plays that connect, and those are different mechanics; picking one would be an assumption dressed as a measurement. The **damage rule stays OPEN at n=1**. Separately confirmed per play over the full day: HIT and CRIT_HIT are DISJOINT — 38 HIT-only, 1 CRIT-only, 34 neither, 0 both — which is exactly what session 68's `castTrace.ts` fix assumed.

2026-08-21 (session 69, §6) — **A BATCH SHAPE CARRIES A HALT FOR THE FAILURE THAT SHAPE EXISTS TO AVOID.** `SESSION_69_LIMITS` (castCap 10, cleanCastCap null, stopOnOilConsume false, zeroStreakCap 15) is a distinct shape rather than `SESSION_65_LIMITS` with a bigger number, and the reason is `haltOnShadowBlind`: the batch stops on the FIRST shadow record taken at a Relaxing firing whose `bestKillProbability` came back null — i.e. on the first sign the §1 hoist works in test and not against a real server. Without it the remaining casts would accumulate exactly the unobservable data the batch exists to stop accumulating, which is the state session 68 spent five casts in without knowing. Ranked BELOW `ledger_exhausted` and `cast_cap` (running out of casts is not evidence about the shadow) and ABOVE `zero_streak` (a streak is a statement about the fishery; a blind shadow means the objective is unreachable). OFF for the pre-hoist shapes — arming it on constants that predate the hoist would make them describe a batch that never ran. Per session 66 §4, the ten casts' justification lives in the constant's own doc comment, beside the number, and not in a brief that will not be read again.

2026-08-21 (session 69, §6 result) — **THE HOIST IS VALIDATED AGAINST A REAL SERVER, AND LIVE PLAY CONTRADICTS THE SIMULATOR'S INPUT DISTRIBUTION OUTRIGHT.** Ten casts, 8 caught / 2 escaped, halted on `cast_cap`. Shadow: **42 records, 10 firing moments, 5 of them RELAXING, and `bestKillProbability` populated on every one** — against session 68's 13 records, 1 firing moment, null on all 13. 0 sanity violations, 0 throws, `haltOnShadowBlind` never fired. **The contradiction is the bigger finding.** Relaxing `bestKillProbability` over all NINE firings on the entire live record reads 0.400 0.481 0.505 0.506 0.580 0.587 0.690 0.964 0.975, and Focus `bestConnectProbability` over this batch's seven reads 0.049 0.231 0.413 0.481 0.563 0.690 0.906 — **every single value strictly between 0 and 1, zero at either endpoint.** The sim's measured bimodality (34.3% exactly 0, 55.8% exactly 1, 9.9% between) is the evidence session 67's "threshold 1 is zero-parameter, everything else is a plateau" argument rests on, and live play has no mass at either endpoint. **Consequence, stated so it is not softened later: the CERTAINTY GATE HAS NEVER ONCE HELD A RELAXING OIL LIVE** — 0 of 9 firings, and `wouldSkip` on 0 of 42 shadow records over ten casts. Shipping `conserve(r=1,f=1)` on this evidence would change nothing at all. The exchange-rate threshold would have held 2 of 9 (the 0.964 and 0.975 firings), accepting 0.061 expected fish to save 2 oils — favourable ~5x at the measured 6 oils/fish, and UNfavourable at the interval's low end of 1.5, where the threshold would be 0.333 and eight of nine would be held. That is what the interval is for. Neither gate is shipped.

2026-08-21 (session 69) — **THE FISHING LEDGERS DISAGREE BY ONE AND THE GAME IS THE MORE GENEROUS SIDE: game `dayDocs[pond 2]` = 14, repo = 15, after a batch that sent exactly 10 `start_run`s with 10 distinct docIds and no resumes.** Both agreed at 5 before the batch, so the game counted +9 where the repo, the log and the corpus (114 → 124 casts) all counted +10. Read twice a minute apart, stable, so not lag. **Reported rather than reconciled, per rule 13's "report it, do not absorb it".** The direction is the safe one — the authoritative game ledger says SIX casts remain where the repo says five, so nothing was overspent and the repo is the conservative side. **Do not spend casts investigating this**; the next session should read both ledgers at start and see whether the gap persists across the 11:00 PT rollover, which is the cheap discriminator between a miscount and a one-off.

2026-08-21 (session 70, §4) — **THE MISSING CAST IS docId 13024510, THE CAUSE IS SERVER-SIDE, AND THE REPO LEDGER NOW DEFERS TO THE GAME'S IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.** `liveFishing.ts` logs `ledgerRemaining` — a live read of the game's own ledger — after every cast in a batch, and session 69's ten read `14 14 13 12 11 10 9 8 7 6`: `dayDocs` went 5 → 6 → **6** → 7 → … → 14, so exactly ONE cast failed to tick it. That cast is ordinary in every client-visible respect — `"Game started successfully."`, three `play_cards`, a lethal Relaxing Oil, caught, looted — and **the server DID charge its energy** (408 → 396, `observedDelta` 12, `drifted: false`). Ruled out from the same logs: not a double-counted doc (15 successful `start_run`s returned 15 DISTINCT docIds, and the repo counter of 15 matches them exactly), not a resume, not a rejected start counted as a success (the one rejection, "Player is already in a game", correctly recorded nothing), not read lag (the settled value held at 14 across two later reads), not miscounted into another pond (`dayDocs[pondId 1]` = 0). Ending on an oil is not the discriminator either: casts 5, 6, 8 and 10 also ended on `use_fishing_item` and all four ticked. **So the repo cannot predict the server's counter and must stop trying to.** `reconcileFishingLedger` + `GuardState.adoptServerRunCount` — the latter **deliberately NOT monotonic**, which is the whole difference from `recordServerCapReached` beside it, because the gap has now pointed BOTH ways inside a day (game 14 / repo 15 at session 69's close; game 16 / repo 15 at session 70's open, after two browser casts). Repo HIGH refuses casts the account has; repo LOW plans a batch the server rejects. Reconciled inside `runOneCast` at the `getFishingState` every cast already makes, NOT in `main()` — session 64's headline was an inner hop that was tested and an outer wire nobody called. Energy is deliberately NOT reconciled: the two ledgers count casts, and inferring energy from casts this process did not send would be inventing a measurement. `checkFishingCaps.ts` printed "A gap means casts this process did not send" on ANY disagreement, a sentence describing only one of the two directions; it now shares the reconciler so the report cannot describe a rule the loop does not follow.

2026-08-21 (session 70, §2) — **GATE FAIL, AND THE FAILURE IS THE RESULT: NEITHER INSTRUMENT CAN SCORE A FOCUS-SPEND POLICY, BECAUSE BOTH UNDER-SPEND FOCUS BY HALF.** Corpus recomputed at 123 clean traces — session 49's numbers, quoted by the brief AND by `focusBudget.ts`'s own header, are all stale: meter-out 80.8% → **64.2%**, turns at focus 0 50.4% → **43.9%**, opening spend 1.62 → **1.40** [1.23, 1.56], profile `3.00 1.38 0.72 …` → `3.00 1.60 0.92 0.51 0.29 0.09 0.10`. The meter-out premise survives; the numbers behind it moved. The sim (live config, n=4000) reads `3.00 2.23 1.31 0.56 0.26 …`, opening spend **0.77** [0.75, 0.79], meter-out **32.5%** — intervals nowhere near overlapping, and the Δ row localizes it: **turns 3 onward agree to within 0.11 and the ENTIRE divergence is the opening move**, which is exactly what every candidate policy constrains. **`focusBudgetSweep.ts` fails its own precondition too and says so unprompted** — it does not use `castSim` at all (it replays real trajectories, which was the brief's proposed remedy), yet under `--matcher=loo` (session 50's fix for this same shape) it spends **0.73** against live's **1.08** [0.82, 1.34]. The table is what that predicts: `costCap(2)` and `threshold(0.1…1)` are byte-for-byte inert (+0/−0), and every arm that moves anything moves it DOWN (`costCap(0)` −31 p=0.000; `schedule(8)` −19 p=0.001). **An inert arm there means NOT EXERCISED, not NO EFFECT** — a cap of 2 cannot bind on a policy spending 0.73. **THE LOCALIZATION IS THE USEFUL HALF:** the replay's fish is the real recorded trajectory, so the fishery is not modelled there, and on the SAME 123 traces the RECORDED policy spent 1.40 where today's replayed policy spends 0.73. `castSim` independently lands at 0.77. Two instruments sharing no fish model agree with each other and disagree with live, so **the shortfall is in this repo's model of its own POLICY, not of the fishery** — and corrected-map era 1.07 vs live 1.08 confirms the data has the phenomenon both models lose. NO FOCUS POLICY IS RECOMMENDED and `focusBudget.ts` is untouched. `handoff/reports/session-70-focus-profile.md`.

2026-08-21 (session 70, §2a) — **AN `observeTurn` THAT RECORDS ONCE PER LOOP ITERATION IS NOT ONE STATE PER TURN, AND THE ARTEFACT IS INVISIBLE IN THE AGGREGATE.** A redraw and a turn-free oil consume both `continue` WITHOUT advancing `turn`, so the first version emitted `0 1 2 2 2 3` and every profile built from it was shifted from the first repeat onward — reading the sim's opening spend as **0.53** where the correct figure is **0.77**. The verdict was FAIL at both, so the conclusion never moved, but a recap would have quoted a number wrong by 45%. Caught by `tests/fishing/focusProfile.test.ts`'s "consecutive turn indices, no gap and no repeat" assertion, which exists precisely because the sim and corpus profiles are averaged SIDE BY SIDE and an instrumentation artefact would be indistinguishable from a finding. The hook is pinned inert the way session 68's oil shadow established: byte-identity over 60 seeds, plus three anti-vacuity companions (it really ran, it hands out a fresh object a caller cannot mutate back through, the indices are consecutive).

2026-08-21 (session 70, §5a) — **THE CRIT DENOMINATOR IS READ OFF EACH CAST'S OWN `GEAR_CID_array`, AND THE CONTROL IS THE REAL ADDITION.** Session 69 scoped the one `CRIT_HIT` to all of 2026-08-21 (1/73, 1/39) on a user-stated recollection that the Steady Lure was equipped before that day's casts. Every fishing document records the gear it was played with, so nothing had to be recalled: **the Steady Lure (951) first appears on a cast at 2026-08-21T16:47:53Z (09:47 PT)** and the eight earlier casts that day carried no lure at all. Classification is EXACT rather than bounded — gear changes only between casts and plays only happen during casts, so no play falls in an unclassifiable gap — and the buckets reconcile to the corpus (443 + 78 = 521 `playTurns`). **No lure, 102 casts: 0 crits in 443 plays, 95% Wilson [0.00%, 0.86%]** — an upper bound BELOW the stated 3%, and the first positive evidence that the crit source is the GEAR rather than something `cardChoice.ts`'s model misses; session 69 could only say the lure was "not contradicted". Steady Lure equipped, 22 casts: 1/78 = 1.28% [0.23%, 6.91%] all plays, 1/44 = 2.27% [0.40%, 11.81%] connecting. **BUT ATTRIBUTION IS NOW AMBIGUOUS AND MUST NOT BE RESTATED AS THE STEADY LURE:** a **Sticky Lure (952)** joined at 19:58:29Z, the single crit (cast 13022874, 20:11:19Z) falls inside that window, and the 27 Steady-only plays hold none. **`/offchain/static` does NOT carry the 3%** — `gameItems` 951 and 952 have `NAME_CID`, `RARITY_CID` 1, `TYPE_CID: "Gear"` and image URLs and **no effect field of any kind** — so the figure stays user-stated and API-unverifiable, neither confirmed nor contradicted. Both denominators are still reported, for session 69's unchanged reason: at n=1 "3% of plays" and "3% of plays that connect" are different mechanics.

2026-08-21 (session 70, §5a) — **A ROD GRANTS THE STARTING DECK — CONFIRMED — AND `REAL_DECK` IS NOW STALE, DELIBERATELY LEFT SO.** `/offchain/static`'s `gameItems` carries a field `/gear/items` does not: **`CARD_CID_array`**, present on every rod and on no lure. 922 Makeshift Rod `[1,2,3,4,5,6,7,76,77,79]`; **811 Shroom Rod `[1,2,3,4,5,6,74,75,76,78]`**; 50 Stone Rod `[5,7,8,9,10,32,35,2,34,37]`. Confirmed against PLAY and not just the payload: every cast's `fullDeck` matched Makeshift's list until **2026-08-21T19:58:29Z** and is Shroom's from that cast on, with `GEAR_CID_array` swapping 922 for 811 at exactly the same cast — and session 69's audited cast 13022748 held card **75**, which exists only in the Shroom set. **Session 15's "every rod's `itemEffects` are empty at every durability tier" result STANDS**: `/gear/items` was the wrong place to look, not the wrong question. Consequence: `REAL_DECK = [1,2,3,4,5,6,7,76,77,79]` in `fishingEmpiricalAblation.ts`, `focusReserveAblation.ts` and `focusProfileCheck.ts` is the MAKESHIFT deck and the account unequipped it on 2026-08-21 at 12:58 PT. **Not repointed**, and annotated at all three sites saying why: every historical sim number was computed on it and 110 of the corpus's 123 clean traces were played on it, so repointing would make old and new numbers incomparable without making either one right. Any future comparison against recent live play must STATE which deck it used.

2026-08-21 (session 70, §5a) — **THE `GearInstance#<id>_<n>` SUFFIX IS A MINT STAMP, NOT AN EQUIP STAMP, AND DATING A GEAR ERA FROM IT LANDS TOO EARLY BY A VARIABLE MARGIN.** The session-70 brief read it as equip time. `#951_1787254688` decodes to 2026-08-20 12:38 PT and first appears on a cast **21 hours later**; `#811_1787332895` and `#952_1787332903` decode to 2026-08-21 10:21 PT and first appear **2h37m later**. A suffix that precedes first appearance by an inconsistent gap is a creation timestamp. The observable is FIRST APPEARANCE in a cast's own `GEAR_CID_array`, and it is exact at the granularity that matters. Rule 9 applied to a user-stated claim exactly as to a brief's, per the brief's own instruction.

2026-08-21 (session 70, §1) — **REDRAW IS CONFIRMED — `play_cards` WITH AN EMPTY `cards` ARRAY — AND THE INTENT GUARD IS THE DELIVERABLE, NOT THE FEATURE.** User-captured 2026-08-21: `{action:"play_cards", data:{cards:[], nodeId:"", focusPoint:[2,3], itemId:0, slotIndex:0, tierId:0}}`, `focusPoint` supplied rather than omitted. No fifth action and no new endpoint — **the distinguishing signal was never in the `action` string**, which is why SPEC-fishing §7 called it "genuinely uncaptured" for so long and why no amount of looking for a fifth action was going to find it. The response reads `"Cards played successfully."` with `FISH_MOVED → CARD_PLAYED → FISH_HP_DIFF → NEW_HAND` carrying THREE cards (in ordinary play the hand SHRINKS turn over turn), so **the server charges a redraw as a turn and the fish moves**. **THE HAZARD: a redraw is indistinguishable on the wire from a play that failed to choose a card, and it spends 1 mana per card held.** `buildFishingEnvelope` DEFAULTED `cards` to `[]`, so any bug, fallback or `chooseCard` returning nothing that still reached the builder would have sent one — silently, looking like an ordinary turn in the log, with session 65's precedent saying a rejected action advances the server's token and surfaces a full turn after its cause. So intent is carried by WHICH BUILDER IS CALLED: `buildFishingEnvelope` now THROWS on a card-less `play_cards` (naming `buildRedrawEnvelope` and the mana cost), `buildRedrawEnvelope` is the only producer of `cards: []`, and `redraw_indicated_not_sent` is replaced by the two distinct events `redraw_sent` / `redraw_suppressed`. **STILL OFF:** `LiveFishingDeps.redrawEnabled` ships false, `main()` never sets it, there is no CLI flag, and a source assertion fails if anything ever writes `redrawEnabled: true`. The reason changed but did not weaken — `REDRAW_THRESHOLD` has never been calibrated against a redraw that could actually be sent, and the one calibration in this repo's history (`cardChoice.ts` §5) produced casts averaging **1.29 turns** with the loss mix flipping 89% `escaped_meter` → 78% `escaped_mana`. A deliberately dead wire is the one case where session 64's "a dep `main()` never sets is a dead feature" is the point rather than the bug, so it is pinned from BOTH ends: tests force it true to exercise the send path. **Two blockers found while wiring, recorded in SPEC §7a rather than left to be rediscovered:** a redraw does NOT advance `turn`, so `MAX_TURNS` cannot bound it and the branch would spin forever burning mana — `MAX_REDRAWS_PER_CAST` is a fail-closed cap, not a policy; and a redraw carries `FISH_MOVED` but the send path does not hand the new position to the matcher, and neither skipping it nor feeding it in has been measured.

2026-08-21 (session 70, §3) — **THE CERTAINTY GATE IS A PROVEN LIVE NO-OP AND HAS BEEN TAKEN OFF THE SHADOW; THE EXCHANGE THRESHOLD REPLACES IT.** Session 69 ran `conserve(r=1,f=1)` in shadow against a real server for ten casts and it **never once held a Relaxing Oil** — 0 of 9 firings on the whole live record, `wouldSkip` on 0 of 42 records. That is forced by its inputs rather than by luck: it holds only when a card already kills with probability >= 1, and every Relaxing `bestKillProbability` ever observed live (0.400 0.481 0.505 0.506 0.580 0.587 0.690 0.964 0.975) is strictly between 0 and 1. It was chosen in session 67 because the SIMULATOR's inputs are bimodal at 0 and 1 (34.3% / 55.8%); live has no mass at either endpoint. So the shadow was spending every record it took on the one rule known to do nothing. `SHADOWED_OIL_POLICY` is now `conservingOil(PREREGISTERED_EXCHANGE_THRESHOLDS)`, which would have held 2 of those 9. **A SWAP RATHER THAN A SECOND ARM, AND NOTHING IS LOST:** `conserve(r=1,f=1)` holds an arm exactly when that arm's probability is `>= 1`, and `bestKillProbability`/`bestConnectProbability` are recorded on EVERY firing record, so the retired gate's verdict is reconstructable offline from the same rows for free — asserted rather than narrated, so the claim cannot quietly stop being true. Anti-vacuity in the settled shape: "the shadow ran the new policy" would pass on a rename, so the tests also pin that the two rules genuinely disagree AND that they disagree in the band live occupies ([0.964, 0.975] held by the exchange gate, [] by the certainty gate, both as literals off the live record). `oilMomentAudit.ts` now prints BOTH gates, labelled off the shared constant so they cannot drift. NOT SHIPPED — `liveFishing.ts` still plays `onDemandTriggers`.
