2026-08-26 (session 99, recap) — **`tests/rejectionAudit.test.ts`'s NUMERIC arm
no longer asserts a MAXIMUM, and this is a re-expression rather than a
relaxation.** `Math.max(numeric) < 2500` went red on ONE `scissor` POST at
3810ms — 1 of 1308, next slowest 2259ms, a first attempt that SUCCEEDED. A max
over an ever-growing machine-local log corpus eventually fails for reasons
unrelated to what it checks, and raising the bound would only defer that while
widening the gap a real leak could hide in. It now asserts the median < 2500 and
the share at/above the 3600ms pacing floor < 1%, which is **strictly more
sensitive** to the actual failure mode: a pacing leak is a floor affecting
essentially all POSTs, which a max could pass.
2026-08-26 — Rod durability preflight is FAIL-CLOSED only, never predictive, until paired readings exist — QUESTIONS.md §52; one unpaired data point cannot support a decrement rate, and a made-up rate is worse than no rate.
2026-08-26 — The equipped rod is identified by GAME_ITEM_ID_CID matching CURRENT_ROD, never by slot number — makes the preflight self-validating: a rod the simulator does not model becomes a halt rather than a silently-wrong simulation (the sessions 89-91 failure, caught forward).
2026-08-26 — `triggeredBoons` is CLOSED as an evidence channel; no runs to be spent on it — 0 non-empty of 10,616 occurrences across the whole corpus, and a capture-path gap was ruled out separately by `focusBuffs` populating at 0.25% on the same object.
2026-08-26 — `data.events[].use_move.data.*Proc[01]` is the dungeon proc-evidence channel; CAPTURE-1's proc-RATE half is unblocked, its effect-SIZE half is not — 1919 exchanges per side already committed, rates 0.31-4.69%, and no flag has ever fired while its own stat read 0.
2026-08-26 — `lck` is CRIT CHANCE — established by the zero-stat control (critProc never fired in 1012+943 exchanges at lck 0), not inferred from the name, per DECISIONS 2026-08-15.
2026-08-26 — CAPTURE-1's "do not stub, default, or flag-hide the proc branches" prohibition is UNCHANGED by the rate discovery — having rates makes one input obtainable; effect sizes are still missing and a branch filled with guessed sizes is still an honest "unscorable" turned into a confident wrong number.
2026-08-26 — tests/noHardcodedPaths ratchet raised 25 -> 26 rather than converting scripts/procEvidence.ts to the profile seam — it names the COMMITTED CORPUS, which per the rodDeck.ts precedent must not be redirectable by --profile.
2026-08-26 — Run the suite as `vitest run --maxWorkers=4` on this machine — the default worker count over-subscribes it and produces TIMEOUT-only false failures in heavy sim tests; the control is that session 99's own 107-file set also fails 4/1967 under load while passing 1988/1988 bounded at load 31.6.
2026-08-26 — `data.events` absence is EXPECTED, not a capture gap; §57's n=1919 stands — the 5308 canonical states partition exactly (2687 GET reads carry events 0/2687; 265 enemyPath offers; 263 path-selection responses with a fresh un-acted enemy; 66 dungeon_started; 108 use_item; 1919 exchanges), and every response in which an exchange resolved carries its events, 1919/1919.
2026-08-26 — `OnDamage.playerId` names the VICTIM, not the dealer — verified against a state diff (0 shield + OnApplyShield 12 - OnDamage 10 = shield 2 in the response), not inferred from the field name.
2026-08-26 — `data.prevent` is NOT the block instrument — it reads 0 on all 2591 combat damage rows including all 76 blocked ones; the effect is in `value`. Recorded so the obvious field is not re-tried.
2026-08-26 — block = floor(ATK/2), evasion = full negate, lck = crit at exactly 2x ATK — measured against a null exact on 2211/2285 no-proc exchanges, with a matched control (same stat non-zero, flag unfired) that matched the rule 0 times in 3577 exchanges.
2026-08-26 — tenacity and intuition are RULED OUT as damage mitigation, with no positive mechanic — tenacity tracks the null and associates with OnHeal (n=6 heals, unbounded); intuition took full ATK on 5 of 5 non-blocked fires and denies a MOVE via `blockedMove`.
2026-08-26 — CAPTURE-1's status list was wrong in both directions: SecondWind (223) and Steadfast (65) exist and were absent from it; lifesteal was in it and DOES NOT EXIST — no such status and no proportional heal in the corpus (ratios 0.20-0.80, one heal with 0 damage dealt). lifesteal comes off the list.
2026-08-26 — Burn = its amount every exchange, matched to the AFTER-state amount (apply, then tick), 522/522 — against the before-state it reads 303/522 with two exception families (applied same exchange; stacking 4->8, 6->12).
2026-08-26 — Weak = floor(ATK*0.75) and Vulnerable = floor(ATK*1.25), both INDEPENDENT of `amount` (identical at 1,2,3,4) — so `amount` is a countdown for these two, not a magnitude.

2026-08-27 (session 104, user directive in-session) — **STATE.md now carries a `## Settled — do not re-open` digest, directly after `## Status`, CARRIED FORWARD each session rather than rewritten.** The failure it fixes is a one-hop loss: session 101 answered the proc effect-size question and recorded it in QUESTIONS.md §58 + DECISIONS, but session 103's recap never mentioned it (different topic), and Claude (chat) writes each brief from the last recap — so the answer was invisible exactly where the next task is chosen. The fix belongs in the recap, not in DECISIONS.md, which already held the answer and simply gave no reason to look. Three design rules: **pointers only** (restating evidence creates a second source of truth that drifts); **the `Re-opens as:` clause names the phrasing a brief would actually use**, because matching a phrase is what works and recognising a theme is what failed — this session's brief cited §58 by name and still got it backwards; and **`[USER]` marks directives an agent may not re-open at all**, as against settled evidence it may re-examine with better data. Capped at ~15 entries, dropped once folded into CLAUDE.md/SPEC.md/a failing test. `/handoff` now tells the next agent to check the brief against it before starting. STATE.md's stated cap was RAISED (~150 body + digest) rather than letting a new required section silently eat the old budget.
2026-08-28 (session 105) — **Tenacity PICK-ORDER is RETIRED, and the reason is structural rather than statistical: it is REDUNDANT given the stat.** `boons` and `stat` are read off the SAME preceding state, so the per-exchange `tenacity` value already encodes what `AddTenacity` did at the moment it applied — picking it 5th rather than 1st gives the same stat later, which the per-exchange reading tracks exactly. Any pick-order effect must therefore appear as a residual after conditioning on the stat, and none exists: 26 of 77 runs picked it at positions 1-9, but **13 of 16 (stat, pick) cells are a SINGLE RUN**, only 3 of 8 stat strata vary in position, and those carry 7 procs in 269 exchanges (Fisher p = 0.488 / 0.196 / 0.566, pooled 0.677). Session 103's raw shape reproduces at 5x volume and then dissolves — pick 5's 73 exchanges are stat 13 + stat 8 + stat 2. **This is a DIFFERENT verdict from SecondWind/Steadfast**, which are real mechanics ordinary volume cannot reach; pick order is not waiting for volume. QUESTIONS.md §63.

2026-08-30 (session 111) — **The recap secret scan is now ONE instrument, `scripts/secretScan.ts`, and the defect it fixes was never a lazy scan — it was that SCOPE WAS IMPLICIT.** Three sessions ran three incomparable scans: 108's method cannot be reconstructed, 109's read zero bytes and reported 0/4 (the origin of "prove the file count"), and 110's printed `files in session diff: 117`, which reads as repository coverage and is **1.3% of the 9,116 tracked files**. 110's scan was CORRECT for what it measured; the recap line was the problem. A diff-scoped scan cannot see a secret that landed in an earlier session, which is exactly how 108's unreconstructable method went unchecked for two sessions. The script names the scope beside the count, defaults to `--scope=tracked`, and fails closed on an unexplained hit, a zero-file sweep, or a broken matcher. CLAUDE.md's working style now points at it and forbids hand-rolling another pipeline.
2026-08-30 (session 111) — **A secret scan has TWO ways to measure nothing and the repo had only ever checked one.** Session 109's rule catches "the reader read no bytes" (control A: a pervasive token, `docId`, must be found in >0 files). It does not catch a REGEX that stopped matching, which produces the identical comforting zero. Control B runs every rule against a synthetic positive sample it must hit and against `redact.ts`'s placeholder it must miss; either failing fails the whole scan. Writing control B immediately found a live crash — `verifyMatchers` recorded a non-global pattern as a failure and then handed it to `matchAll`, which throws, so the reportable failure would have taken the scan down instead of being reported.
2026-08-30 (session 111) — **The scan's rules are the inverse of `src/api/redact.ts`, and its samples are BUILT AT RUNTIME rather than written as literals.** A scanner holding literal example secrets flags itself, and the obvious remedy — allowlisting the scanner — would create the one file where a real secret could hide behind a legitimate exemption. Both `scripts/secretScan.ts` and `tests/secretScan.test.ts` are asserted clean under their own rules, and that assertion caught the test file twice: splitting only the VALUE is not enough, because a rule keyed on a LABEL is matched by the code that builds the label. Also pinned: neither file may contain a literal NUL (the first draft did, from splitting `-z` output on a raw NUL instead of the `\x00` escape), because a NUL makes git and grep treat the source as binary and the scanner would skip its own file.
2026-08-30 (session 111) — **`usernameQuoted` was tightened three times against REAL false positives from the tracked tree, not tuned against imagination.** (a) In JSON the field is `"username": "<USER>"`, and without consuming the key's own closing quote the rule read it as the value's opening quote, matched the nonsense `username": "`, and so never tested the actual value against the placeholder lookahead — a correctly-redacted fixture flagged. (b) The value must start alphanumeric, dropping documentation ellipses. (c) The value must be two-plus characters, dropping one-letter stand-ins in `redact.ts`'s own comment; the accepted cost is that a genuine one-character username would not be caught. Remaining exemptions are five files of synthetic redaction vectors, each with its reason, and every exempted hit is PRINTED so an exemption cannot quietly widen.
2026-08-30 (session 111) — **Allowlist staleness is only computed on an EXHAUSTIVE scope.** On `--scope=diff` an exemption's file is usually just absent from the diff, and a naive check flagged all six as stale and told the reader to delete live exemptions. Out-of-scope and stale are indistinguishable from a narrow sweep, so the report says it did not check rather than guessing — same discipline as DECISIONS 2026-08-28's ruling that a threshold on a composition-bound statistic is not an invariant.
2026-08-30 (session 111) — **The guard-budget day-key straddle is FIXED IN CODE, closing QUESTIONS §65 and the repo's one concrete carried task.** `saveGuardBudget` evaluated `todayKey()` at WRITE time and wrote counters seeded at PROCESS START, so a process crossing 11:00 Pacific stamped the whole invocation's totals onto the new day. `DAY_MEMO` in `guardPersistence.ts` rebases at the boundary, keyed by guard-state path (one process legitimately accounts for two arms). Both `loadGuardBudget` and `saveGuardBudget` now take an optional `now: Date`, matching `todayKey`'s existing shape, which is what makes the boundary testable without waiting for 11:00. Ten cases replay session 108's real timestamps (2026-08-29T17:53Z start, 18:00Z crossing) and land on `{date: "2026-08-29", energySpent: 120, runsStarted: 6}` — the value session 109 wrote by hand to unblock the day. 2026-08-30 (session 111) — **Three corrections to §65's own fix sketch, each found by writing the case rather than the code.** (a) The memo must be seeded at LOAD, not lazily at the first save — a process that loads a non-zero seed before 11:00 and does not write again until after it has no pre-rollover save to learn the boundary from, and a save-time memo reproduces the original bug exactly. (b) FIRST LOAD WINS: `liveRun.ts` and `liveFishing.ts` each load twice, and `doctor.ts`/`checkFishingCaps.ts` load the same paths read-only; a second load re-seeding the memo after a rollover zeroes the baseline and reintroduces the bug in full. (c) A backwards move must NOT throw — the first draft did, and it was reachable on a LIVE self-healing path.
2026-08-30 (session 111) — **`guards.adoptServerRunCount()` assigns the server's count ABSOLUTELY and can LOWER it, which makes "the counters are monotonic within a process" FALSE on the autonomous fishing path.** `liveFishing.ts` calls it after `reconcileFishingLedger`; post-rollover that can put the cumulative below the rebase baseline. Throwing there would have crashed a straddling autonomous batch at the exact moment it was correcting itself. The baseline is what stops applying once the counters are re-seeded, so it is dropped and the raw cumulative is written — always `>=` the rebased value, keeping the error direction at "over-count, block, never over-spend", and in the adopt case it is exactly the game's own number because the reconciler guarantees that equality. **Do not "restore" the throw as a fail-closed improvement.**
2026-08-30 (session 111) — **The fix rebases the PERSISTED ledger only; `GuardState`'s in-memory counters stay cumulative across the boundary.** A straddling process therefore still counts the old day's spend against the new day's cap and stops early — fail-safe, and the next process reads a correct ledger. Recorded so a future session does not read §65 as fully closed and does not rediscover the in-memory half as a new bug: it is known, it is safe, and re-seeding a live `GuardState` mid-batch is a larger change than §65 asked for.
2026-08-30 (session 111) — **§65's own text mis-cited the second call site and the correction is recorded rather than silently fixed.** It said `liveFishing.ts:1799` "uses the identical `saveGuardBudget(...)` pattern"; 1799 builds the `PersistedGuardBudget`-shaped INPUT to `reconcileFishingLedger`, and the writes are at 1804, 1903 and 1969. 2026-08-30 (session 112) — **The double-lethal override does NOT intercept the on-demand trigger's decision point, and this is provable three ways** — but it DOES consume the trajectory upstream, which is the actual reason on-demand never fires. (c) The suppressor is IDENTIFIED and is the necessity gate. **The real interception is TEMPORAL.** `fishHp` at decision, histogram: **1:1, 2:0, 3:4, 4:5**. The override fires at 3-4 and its two oils deal 4 — a certain kill — so the fish is destroyed one band ABOVE where on-demand lives and never descends into it. 7 of the 9 band turns fired; the other 2 were held by the band's own `bestKillProbability >= 0.85` check, saving 4 oils.

2026-08-30 (session 113) — **A PINNED CLAIM WAS FALSIFIED BY THE CORPUS, and the direction of the failure is the finding.** `tests/fishing/redrawCounterfactual.test.ts` asserted that at BOTH sweep thresholds the conditioned redraw arm nets positive where the unconditional arm nets ~nothing, with the note *"Asserted as an inequality so a future corpus can falsify it loudly instead of by a number sliding."* The 20-cast batch did exactly that. **`b6` (K=6, conditioned) did not move at all** — its whole `toMatchObject` is byte-identical, net 11 — while **`all3` (unconditional) went 8 → 12** (rescues 19→23 against sacrifices 8→11). So the inequality inverted **not because the conditioned arm got worse, but because the unconditional arm caught up and passed it**, which is the more interesting of the two ways it could have failed. **K=10 still carries the claim and by a WIDER margin (23 vs 12)**, so the conditioning thesis is not dead — it is threshold-dependent in a way nobody had measured, and K=6 has stopped being evidence for it. Re-pinned as EXACT values on both sides plus an explicit `toBeLessThan` marking the inversion, rather than as a smaller inequality: the two halves move independently and collapsing them into one comparison is what hid this until it flipped. Re-opens as: *"the conditioned redraw arm beats the unconditional one at every threshold."* At K=6 it no longer does.

The headline claim was ALSO re-derived independently rather than trusting the test the same offline session wrote: loading the real 80-card catalog and calling `chooseNewCard` on the recorded session-92 offer gives 35→5×3=15, 30→6×5=30, 31→6×5=30 and picks **30**, matching the entry exactly. One thing the original write-up did not record: the fix flips card 35 against ITSELF — weighted, its own hit (15) beats its own crit (8), so the raw formula had that backwards too. The flaw was one notch wider than described. NOTE the validation floor is unchanged at 2 live card choices project-wide: the fix is defensible on argument, not on data.
2026-09-01 (session 115) — **`scripts/checkEntryTiers.ts` was understating its own evidence; fixed.** Line 235 printed "the rotation ORDER — which is UNCONFIRMED (one day observed)". That was written when n=1 and became false when session 114 measured day 20696 → Summoner; the measurement landed but the printed warning did not follow it. Left alone, the next session reads the instrument and is told the rotation is n=1 when it is n=2. Replaced with the actual state — two days on record (20695→Foxglove 139, 20696→Summoner 140), ADJACENT so many orders still fit, a NON-adjacent third point worth more than another consecutive one. No test pinned the string; `tests/entryTierRunway.test.ts` 13/13 still green, `tsc` clean. General form, worth carrying: a measurement is not landed until every place that PRINTS its evidence count has been updated too.

2026-09-01 (session 116) — ⭐⭐ **THE ROTATION IS `faction = dayOfWeek + 2`, AND THE CHOBO PREDICTION LANDED.** Third rotation point, taken on the single authorized Tier-2 run (25269116, game day 20697, `dayOfWeek` 5). Balances immediately before and after: **134 Chobo 42→39, and all six others unchanged** (135 Crusader 39, 136 Overseer 51, 137 Athena 33, 138 Archon 30, 139 Foxglove 45, 140 Summoner 42). That is **9 for 9** on the one-faction-x3 model across three faction days. The map is now `20695 → faction 5 Foxglove`, `20696 → faction 6 Summoner`, `20697 → faction 7 Chobo` — the faction INDEX incrementing by exactly 1 per day, against an index the SERVER publishes (`/offchain/static` recipes `500006`, `FACTION_CID_array`) rather than one read off id order. `dayOfWeek === day mod 7` holds on every point checkable (7 × 2956 = 20692). ⚠ **Three CONSECUTIVE points, and the WRAP IS UNTESTED**: the sequence has never crossed faction 7 → 1. Day 20698 predicts **faction 1 Crusader (135)** and is the single highest-value next observation, because it is the only one that tests the wrap. Re-opens as: *"the day→faction map is unsolved"* — it is solved up to the wrap, not beyond it.

2026-09-01 (session 116) — ⭐⭐ **EVADE DOMINATES CRIT, and `critProc`'s exclusion list was incomplete — the multiplier was never wrong.** `tests/procEffectSize.test.ts` went red on `critProc1` (3/4 status-clean) and the single miss is `run-2026-09-01-22-35-10/state-128`: enemy ATK 16, `critProc1` fired so `2*ATK = 32` predicted, **0 observed** — because `evadeProc0` fired on the same exchange. Measured rather than assumed: an exchange whose victim evaded takes **0 damage 53/53 times corpus-wide** (16 on side 0, 37 on side 1; **18/18** restricted to status-clean), and the crit co-fires sit INSIDE that 53 rather than being exceptions to it. `critProc` already excluded `blockProc${foe}` — the same class of victim-side proc that overrides the attacker's arithmetic — and evade was simply never in the list. The gap stayed invisible because evade+crit co-fires number **3 in the whole corpus and all 3 arrived in this run**. Fixed by making `excludeFlag` a list and adding `evadeProc${foe}`. This is session 113's `scaleRule` lesson in a second shape: **an incomplete exclusion list is the defect, never the multiplier.** ⚠ Noted and deliberately NOT changed: `evadeProc` excludes `critProc${foe}` and does not need to — its rule holds over the co-fires too, so removing that exclusion would make evade's claim strictly stronger at larger n. That is a separate change.

2026-09-01 (session 116) — ⚠⚠ **`BurnMastery` FLOOR-VS-ROUND IS VACUOUS, NOT OPEN. It was carried for three sessions and named again in this session's brief, and no capture could ever have met it.** Two independent defects in the request. **First, its stated precondition was already satisfied when it was written**: sessions 113/114 asked for "an ODD plain amount", and the pair set has been `{6/3, 4/2, 8/4, 10/5}` since session 114 — `3` (n=18) and `5` (n=4) are both odd. Session 114's own DECISIONS entry recorded that pair set and wrote *"Still NO odd plain amount"* on the same line. **Second, and this is the half that closes it**: every `Burn` amount and every tick in the corpus is an INTEGER — all 14 distinct values observed (1..12, 14, 24), zero non-integers — and for integer `p`, `floor(2p) === round(2p) === 2p` identically. Floor and round are not two hypotheses on this domain; they are the same function. Per CLAUDE.md rule 6 this is an unmeetable request wearing a carry-forward's clothes. **CLOSED.** Re-opens ONLY if a non-integer burn amount is ever observed. Also fixed: `scripts/statusEffects.ts` printed `x2 vs +3 UNSEPARATED (only after=3 ever seen)` on every run — stale since session 113, and it printed the pairs `4/2`, `8/4`, `10/5` directly beside a caption asserting only `after=3` existed. Same class as session 115's stale `checkEntryTiers.ts` warning: **the measurement landed, the printed caption didn't.**

2026-09-01 (session 116, fishing) — **The clamp-agreement bar in `damageEconomy` was RE-DERIVED, not widened, on its first breach since session 91.** `Math.abs(unclamped.drift - LIVE.drift) < 0.05` read **0.0517**. Bumping to 0.06 would have been the wrong move and STATE's standing warning about this family of bands says so. The real defect is the bar's SHAPE: an ABSOLUTE gap tightens on its own as `|LIVE.drift|` grows, which it has (−0.1985 at session 91 → −0.6473 now), so constant relative agreement had to breach a fixed 0.05 eventually. **This is DECISIONS 2026-08-28's "a threshold on a composition-bound statistic is not an invariant" in a second place.** Replaced with the scale-free form the prose was always about, `gap / |drift| < 0.1`, measured **0.0799**; the ratio's own history is 4.7% (s91) → 8.0% (now) and IS rising, so the new bar is not un-breakable. Pre-registered: **a breach re-examines the claim; it does not move the bar.** Separately, `LIVE.drift` moved −0.6017 → −0.6473 — still negative, still short of −1, so a pin update rather than a re-derivation, and the `bare/LIVE` ratio still clears its `> 5` bar. ⭐ **`redrawCounterfactual`'s K=10 arm moved by exactly ONE fire, ONE rescue and ONE mana, while `sacrifices` (7) and `wasted` (11) did not move at all** — the strongest evidence yet for STATE's split that K=6 is unfrozen while K=10 still carries the thesis, since the two cells the thesis rests on were untouched by the largest fishing day in the corpus.
